# Next Lane — Dev Board (Backlog)

Single prioritized board maintained by the **backlog-groomer**, fed by the two independent auditors (`docs/AUDIT-PRODUCT.md`, `docs/AUDIT-ENGINEERING.md`), the front-end QA review (`docs/UI-REVIEW.md`), and the roadmap (`docs/ROADMAP.md`). The autonomous build loop pulls from **Ready (top of queue)**.

Format: `- [ ] (P1, M) title — description [src]` · P0 critical / P1 now / P2 next / P3 later · size S/M/L. Checked `[x]` = done.

1. ✅ **Pages — issue ↔ page cross-linking** (SHIPPED 2026-07-09 — remove on
   next groom) — `PageIssueLink` parse-on-save sync via `extractIssueNumbers`
   + `GET /pages/:id/issues` + `GET /issues/:id/pages` (bounded+truncated, 6
   unit tests); issue-drawer "Linked pages" section (`LinkedPagesSection`); MCP
   `get_page_issues`/`get_issue_pages` (119 tools); e2e `issue-linked-pages.spec.ts`
   4/4 green desktop+mobile against a real stack. Independent code-review pass
   still queued (nice-to-have; hard DoD met). Original scope below for
   reference — the tight
   tracker↔docs integration the incumbent splits across two
   separately-priced products, and (once shipped) a second edge type in
   the same graph rather than a disconnected feature. **Scope:** when a
   page's content references an issue key (`NL-123`), auto-link it.
   **Recommended default (flag as an assumption, not a mandate — confirm
   or override at build time):** parse on save server-side into an
   explicit `PageIssueLink` join table (already in `schema.prisma`,
   unused until this item — mirrors `IssueLink`'s shape, page↔issue
   instead of issue↔issue) rather than a render-time text scan, so the
   issue drawer's "Linked pages" back-reference is a reliable query, not a
   live re-parse. Issue drawer gains a "Linked pages" section mirroring
   the GitHub/GitLab/Gitea Development-section link pattern already
   shipped. Feed these edges into the shipped graph endpoint as the
   `kind: 'issue-link'` edge type anticipated there (`PageGraphDto` today
   only carries page-link edges — this item is the additive change that
   layers issue-link edges in, not a breaking one), so item #3's graph
   view picks them up without a second integration. Cross-project key
   references are out of v1 scope, matching the existing
   issue-key-extraction scoping convention (GitHub/GitLab/Gitea webhooks
   already scope keys to their own project only). MCP: a
   `list_issue_pages`-shaped tool (or extend the existing
   `get_epic_overview`-style composition) so an agent can ask "what docs
   exist for this issue" in one call. **Acceptance criteria:** saving a
   page containing `NL-123` creates a discoverable back-reference on that
   issue's drawer AND a corresponding edge in `get_page_graph`; editing
   the reference out of the page's content removes both; a cross-project
   reference is correctly ignored, not silently mislinked. **Territory:**
   `apps/api/src/pages/**` (or a small dedicated `page-issue-links`
   module), `apps/web/src/components/issues/**` (drawer section),
   `apps/mcp/src/**`. **Size:** M.

5. ✅ **Pages — full-text search** (SHIPPED 2026-07-09 — remove on next groom:
   `Page.searchVector` tsvector generated column + GIN index migration
   `20260709120000_add_pages_fts`; `SearchService` page FTS/ILIKE, tenant-scoped;
   `SearchPageDto` + `pages` in `SearchResultsDto`; Cmd-K palette "Pages" group
   with a distinct page glyph, archived-muted; +4 unit tests; e2e
   `pages-search.spec.ts` 2/2 green desktop+mobile against a real stack. MCP
   `search_pages` deferred as a nice-to-have follow-up.) — Pages
   join the search surface issues already have, rather than being a
   second-class silo. **Scope:** extend the existing Postgres
   `tsvector`/GIN full-text-search infrastructure (shipped for `Issue`
   title+description) to `Page` title+content via a generated-column
   migration mirroring the issue one; surface Pages in the command palette
   (Cmd-K) and cross-project search results alongside issues, visually
   distinguished (distinct icon/badge) so a search result's type is
   obvious at a glance. **Acceptance criteria:** a page's content is
   findable via the same search bar issues already use; a VIEWER never
   sees a page from a project they can't access (same tenant-scoping the
   issue search already enforces — reuse it, don't reimplement).
   **Territory:** `apps/api/src/pages/**` (tsvector migration) or
   `apps/api/src/search/**`, `apps/web/src/components/CommandPalette*` /
   cross-project search UI. **Size:** S.

8. ✅ **Pages — delete-time backlink impact signal** (SHIPPED 2026-07-09 —
   remove on next groom: DELETE response reports `orphanedBacklinks` (counted
   pre-cascade); web confirm dialog warns "N pages link here — their [[links]]
   will become unresolved" without blocking; MCP `delete_page` description
   teaches check-backlinks-first + explains the response field. 43 API pages
   unit tests; adversarial e2e 14/14 green desktop+mobile.) — original scope:
   deleting a page today succeeds silently even
   when OTHER pages link to it. The graph stays consistent (the `PageLink`
   rows cascade-delete), but the *source* pages' content keeps a now-dangling
   `[[Title]]` that renders as an unresolved/create-it link with no warning at
   delete time — unlike the existing tree-children guard, which blocks and
   explains. An agent (or user) gets no "N pages link here" signal before
   destroying an inbound-linked hub. **Scope:** surface the inbound backlink
   count on delete — the web delete `ConfirmDialog` warns "N pages link here
   (their links will become unresolved)"; `delete_page` (MCP) + the REST
   response report the count so an agent isn't blind. Do NOT block the delete
   (deleting a linked page is legitimate, Obsidian-style) — this is an
   informed-consent affordance, mirroring the backlinks panel the editor
   already shows. **Acceptance criteria:** deleting a page with ≥1 backlink
   surfaces the count in both the web confirm dialog and the delete API/MCP
   response; deleting an unlinked page is unchanged; the graph/backlinks of
   surviving pages remain consistent. **Territory:** `apps/api/src/pages/**`,
   `apps/web/src/components/pages/PageTree.tsx`, `apps/mcp/src/tools/**`.
   **Size:** S.

7. **Cross-project issue MOVE** (P2, M, decision-needed) — a mis-filed issue
   cannot be moved to the correct project today; the only correction path is
   delete+recreate, which loses history/comments/links and the original key
   (measured: 2 calls, real data loss — MCP-QA pass 1 finding 4). Product-wide,
   not MCP-specific — no web-UI surface has a "Move" action either, unlike
   the comparable-tracker table-stakes feature. **Needs a founder/product
   call before scoping build work:** does a cross-project move re-key the
   issue (new project's number, old key redirects/aliases) or keep its
   original key; do sub-tasks/epics move with it or get orphaned; is it
   MEMBER+ or ADMIN-gated given it changes an issue's permission boundary.
   Once decided: `move_issue_to_project` (guarded, re-keys, preserves
   history) exposed both as a web-UI action and an MCP tool. Filed as a
   decision-needed item rather than blocking on speculative scope. [MCP-QA
   pass 1, finding 4; product-auditor cross-project gap]

**Org-wide Pages — cross-project links + a workspace docs space (P1, epic,
founder-approved 2026-07-10, no longer decision-gated).** Founder directive,
verbatim: **"Both."** — asked to choose between cross-project
`[[wiki-link]]`s and a dedicated workspace-level docs space, the founder
confirmed both. **This supersedes and closes the formerly decision-gated
"Workspace-level 'spaces'" item** (previously filed in § Later (P3), see the
removed entry) — the product decision is made; what follows is the
buildable, dependency-sequenced slice breakdown, each with its authz
decision made explicit in the acceptance criteria per this epic's own risk
(Pages' per-project tenant-isolation story was just re-hardened by the
engineering audit the same day, 2026-07-10, `4d3a43a` — a live `/search`
PAT-scope page-content leak, closed — moving the ownership boundary up to
the workspace must not reopen that class of bug). Full framing:
`docs/VISION.md` § The pillars item 7 (extended 2026-07-10); full slice
detail: `docs/ROADMAP.md` Phase 11's "Org-wide Pages" continuation (items
13-18, which these six queue items mirror 1:1). Sequenced strictly by
dependency: schema → workspace-scoped CRUD → cross-workspace-safe link
resolution → workspace graph endpoint → frontend nav/reuse → search + MCP
traversal.

**Key finding underpinning every authz decision below** (verified against
`apps/api/src/common/membership.util.ts`): a workspace member already has
at least VIEWER on every project in that workspace by default (no
per-project override can reduce a member below VIEWER — there is no "no
access" state). A `[[link]]` between two projects in the SAME workspace
therefore introduces no new leak surface under today's model. The boundary
that must never be crossed is cross-**WORKSPACE** — the same class of leak
the just-fixed `/search` bug was — and every slice below is scoped to
prevent exactly that, reusing the already-shipped `assertWorkspaceMember`/
`assertWorkspaceRole` helpers (same file, already used by
`WorkspacesController`) as the workspace-level chokepoint.

8. ✅ **Schema — nullable `Page.projectId` + always-present `Page.workspaceId`**
   — shipped 2026-07-10, see the ticked § Already Done entry for full detail.
   (P1, S, **schema-architect design pass done**, not a mechanical
   migration) — recommended shape: add `Page.workspaceId String`
   (non-nullable, backfilled for every existing row from
   `project.workspaceId` in the same migration) and relax `Page.projectId`
   to nullable; `projectId` set = project-scoped page (unchanged behavior),
   `projectId` null = workspace-scoped page (org docs space). **Rejected
   alternative:** a separate `WorkspacePage` table — rejected because it
   forks `PagesService`/`PageVersion`/`PageLink`/`syncWikiLinks`/the graph
   endpoint/the tree UI/all 12+ MCP page tools into two parallel
   implementations; a nullable FK lets every one reuse the same code path
   with one added branch. **Migration/back-compat implications (flag
   explicitly, do not treat as transparent):** every existing query
   assuming `page.projectId` is non-null — `PagesService`'s CRUD/tree/move/
   version methods, `syncWikiLinks`'s title-candidate query, the graph +
   backlinks endpoints, `PagesController`'s `assertProjectRole` gate, the
   Cmd-K/`/search` page results, and all 12 existing MCP page tools — needs
   an explicit `projectId === null` branch or a workspace-scoped sibling
   method; this is a second code path everywhere, not a one-line schema
   change (the real work is item 9). **Acceptance criteria:** additive-only
   migration, zero `prisma migrate diff` drift for existing data, every
   pre-existing project-page test passes unmodified. **Territory:**
   `apps/api/prisma/schema.prisma` + migration. **MCP:** n/a (schema only).
   [ROADMAP Phase 11 continuation item 13; founder directive 2026-07-10]
9. ✅ **Backend — workspace-scoped page CRUD/tree/move/version history** —
   shipped 2026-07-10, see the ticked § Already Done entry for full detail.
   MCP tools for workspace pages were a deliberate follow-up (not done in
   this slice — see the § Already Done entry's explicit MCP note); they
   **shipped 2026-07-30** as item 13 below. (P1,
   M, depends on #8) — mirrors `PagesService`'s existing project-scoped
   methods, branching on `projectId === null`. **Authz decision (explicit):**
   gated by `assertWorkspaceMember`/`assertWorkspaceRole` directly against
   `Page.workspaceId` — VIEWER read / MEMBER+ write, the same role shape
   project pages use, resolved one level up (no per-project role override
   applies — there is no owning project). **PAT scopes stay `pages:read`/
   `pages:write`, unchanged and un-split** — see the scope-wide
   recommendation in `docs/ROADMAP.md` Phase 11 continuation (the
   authorization boundary is `assertWorkspaceRole` vs `assertProjectRole`,
   orthogonal to the scope check). New REST: `GET/POST /workspaces/:id/pages`
   (list/create at the workspace root); `PATCH/DELETE /pages/:id` branches
   internally. **Acceptance criteria:** workspace VIEWER reads but can't
   write; workspace MEMBER creates/edits; a user in a DIFFERENT workspace
   gets 403/404 (new tenant-isolation-matrix rows, mirroring the pattern
   that caught the `/search` leak); every existing project-page
   tenant-isolation row unchanged. **Territory:** `apps/api/src/pages/**`.
   **MCP:** `list_workspace_pages`/`get_workspace_page`/`create_workspace_page`/
   `update_workspace_page`/`move_workspace_page`/`delete_workspace_page`
   mirroring the existing project-page tool set (deferred to item 13 with
   the graph/backlink tools, so all workspace-page MCP surface ships
   together). [ROADMAP Phase 11 continuation item 14]
10. ✅ **Backend — cross-workspace-safe `[[wiki-link]]` resolution** —
    shipped 2026-07-17, see the ticked § Already Done entry for full detail.
    (P1, M, depends on #9) — `syncWikiLinks`'s title-candidate query
    rescoped from `projectId` to `workspaceId`; candidates become "every
    page (project- or workspace-scoped) in the SAME workspace." **Authz
    decision — the founder's exact question, "what happens when a viewer
    can see the source but not the target?":** given every workspace member
    can already read every page in that workspace, this can't occur WITHIN
    one workspace. It CAN occur ACROSS workspaces via a `[[Title]]`
    colliding with a page that exists only in a workspace the viewer isn't
    a member of. **Decision: treat a foreign-workspace title collision
    exactly like a non-existent title — render as an unresolved link
    (Obsidian's existing "not-yet-created page" state), never a
    distinguishable "restricted" state**, since "restricted" would itself
    leak the target's existence/title to a non-member — the same
    suppress-don't-half-reveal principle the `4d3a43a` search-leak fix
    used. **Acceptance criteria:** a same-workspace cross-project (or
    project↔workspace-docs) `[[link]]` resolves and creates a `PageLink`
    edge exactly like same-project links do today; a cross-workspace title
    collision produces zero `PageLink` row and is indistinguishable from a
    genuinely nonexistent title — gate is a live-reproduced adversarial
    test (two workspaces, colliding titles, one viewer in only workspace
    A), mirroring how the `4d3a43a` fix was verified. **Territory:**
    `apps/api/src/pages/pages.service.ts` (`syncWikiLinks`). **MCP:** n/a
    (parsing/resolution only; surfaced via the existing/new page tools).
    [ROADMAP Phase 11 continuation item 15]
11. ✅ **Backend — workspace-wide graph + backlinks endpoint** — shipped
    2026-07-17, see the ticked § Already Done entry for full detail. (P2,
    S, depends on #9/#10) — `GET /workspaces/:id/pages/graph` unions every
    project's page graph plus the workspace docs space, gated by the same
    `assertWorkspaceRole(VIEWER)` chokepoint as #9, edges only present when
    BOTH endpoints resolve within that workspace (enforced by construction
    via #10, not a runtime filter); same `MAX_GRAPH_NODES`/`truncated` cap
    pattern as the existing per-project endpoint, which stays UNCHANGED
    (still project-scoped, for anyone wanting the narrower view).
    **Acceptance criteria:** the workspace-X graph never contains a
    workspace-Y node/edge, live-verified with a two-workspace fixture.
    **Territory:** `apps/api/src/pages/**`. **MCP:** `get_workspace_page_graph`/
    `get_workspace_page_backlinks` (bundled with item 13's MCP batch).
    [ROADMAP Phase 11 continuation item 16]
12. ✅ **Frontend — workspace Docs nav + reused tree/editor/backlinks/graph
    components** — shipped 2026-07-10 (frontend-builder, org-docs epic

13. ✅ **Search + MCP — workspace-wide search scoping and cross-project/
    workspace-docs traversal tools** — **shipped 2026-07-30**
    (`docs/RESEARCH-AGENT-MEMORY.md` R2, "close the org-wide memory hole";
    ROADMAP Phase 11 continuation item 18, now ticked with full evidence).
    Shipped **three** MCP tools, not the eight sketched below, because only
    three REST routes were actually missing an MCP door:
    `list_workspace_pages` (`GET /workspaces/:id/pages/tree`, `pages:read`),
    `get_workspace_page_graph` (`GET /workspaces/:id/pages/graph`,
    `pages:read`) and `create_workspace_page` (`POST /workspaces/:id/pages`,
    `pages:write`) — 120 → **124 tools** (60 read / 64 write). The other
    five (`get_/update_/move_/delete_workspace_page`,
    `get_workspace_page_backlinks`) were deliberately dropped: those are
    by-id routes that `PagesService` already branches on
    `projectId === null` for, so the existing `get_page`/`update_page`/
    `move_page`/`delete_page`/`get_page_backlinks` already work on
    workspace pages — a second name for the same route is tool-list bytes
    with zero capability. Fixed two verified-stale descriptions in the same
    commit (`get_page_graph` claimed nodes were `{id, title}` when they also
    carry `projectId`/`projectKey`/`updatedAt`; several page tools still
    said `[[wiki-links]]` resolve "within the project" when resolution went
    workspace-wide on 2026-07-17) and **rewrote the MCP server
    instructions** so the pages graph is named as the memory and
    `get_project_context` is described as what it is — a short, full-replace
    64 KB handoff note that points into the pages, not "the project's
    persistent memory" (the mis-framing `RESEARCH-AGENT-MEMORY.md` §2
    identified as the reason agents skip the graph). **Acceptance criteria
    met, live:** an outsider PAT holding `pages:read`+`pages:write` gets 403
    on all three workspace page routes, and an owner PAT WITHOUT
    `pages:read` also gets 403 on all three; `pat-scope-coverage` already
    rostered every workspace page route (no matrix hole); search was
    re-verified to already scope by `Page.workspaceId`, so workspace pages
    are searchable and tenant-scoped by construction (ranked snippets + a
    real result cap are `RESEARCH-AGENT-MEMORY.md` **R1**, a separate
    follow-up, not this item). **Verified:** MCP 147/147 (+37) + build
    clean; API integration 441/441; `tsc --noEmit` clean across
    api/web/mcp/shared; and a live round-trip through the REAL stdio MCP
    server — workspace graph **3 nodes / 4 edges / 1,078 B** with all four
    cross-scope edges resolved, `list_workspace_pages` 359 B (no
    project-page leak), `create_workspace_page` 936 B, handbook backlinks
    747 B carrying `sourceProjectKey`, and the per-project graph correctly
    still 1 node / 0 edges (278 B). **Territory:** `apps/mcp/src/**` (no API
    change needed). Original definition follows.
    <details><summary>Original item</summary>
    (P2, M, depends on #9/#10/#11) —
    extends the shipped `Page.searchVector` FTS and the `canReadPages`/
    `includePages` pattern (the exact mechanism the 2026-07-10 `/search`
    leak fix introduced) to workspace-scoped pages: a search result NEVER
    surfaces a page — project- or workspace-scoped — from a workspace the
    caller isn't a member of, and a PAT without `pages:read` sees zero page
    results from either scope, matching the just-hardened contract exactly.
    **MCP (bundles this item's tools with items 9/11's deferred tools):**
    `list_workspace_pages`/`get_workspace_page`/`create_workspace_page`/
    `update_workspace_page`/`move_workspace_page`/`delete_workspace_page`/
    `get_workspace_page_graph`/`get_workspace_page_backlinks` — mirroring
    the existing project-page tool shape exactly (same `pages:read`/
    `pages:write` scopes, same compact/verbose/pagination envelope), plus a
    `workspaceId`-aware traversal note in tool descriptions so an agent
    understands "walk the backlinks from this page" may now cross project
    boundaries within one workspace — the crown-jewel payoff: an agent
    answering "what's connected to this handbook page across every
    project?" in one call, something neither Confluence's per-space wiki
    nor Obsidian's single-vault graph can do at all. **Acceptance
    criteria:** a live-reproduced two-workspace fixture proves a
    `pages:read`-scoped PAT in workspace A never sees a workspace-B page
    via search OR any new MCP tool; an `issues:read`-only PAT (no
    `pages:read`) sees zero pages from either scope, mirroring the
    just-fixed `/search` regression test shape exactly. **Territory:**
    `apps/api/src/search/**`, `apps/mcp/src/tools/**`. [ROADMAP Phase 11
    continuation item 18]
    </details>

(2) a board-card "linked PR" badge — `board.service.ts`'s `issueInclude` gained a compact `githubLinks`/`gitlabLinks` state-only select (the `parent` include from `503b48d` deliberately left undisturbed), aggregated into `IssueDto.prLinkSummary: {open, merged}` by `issue.mapper.ts`, rendered as a small emerald "PR"/purple "Merged" badge on `IssueCard.tsx` mirroring the existing blocked-issue badge pattern exactly. (3) live PR/CI status in the issue drawer's Development section — the first REAL outbound calls through the previously-seamed-but-unused `GithubClient`/`GitlabClient` (`getPullRequestStatus`/`getMergeRequestStatus`, polled on drawer open via `GET /issues/:issueId/{github,gitlab}-links/live`), rendering a small CI-checks dot per PR/MR row and degrading gracefully (a quiet "live status unavailable" hint, never a hard failure) when the live call fails — live-verified in this network-isolated environment: `{"error":"GitHub API unreachable"}` per-link, exactly as designed. **SSRF (mandatory, done):** both clients' every outbound call goes through the shared `resolveAndCheckBlocked()` DNS-preflight + `redirect:'manual'` guard reused (imported, not duplicated) from `webhooks.service.ts` — defense-in-depth for GitHub's fixed host, the PRIMARY mitigation for GitLab's admin-supplied self-hosted `gitlabBaseUrl` (unit-tested with a mocked DNS resolution returning a blocked IP, confirming `fetch` is never called). **Tests:** 69 new API unit tests (1731→1800, all green) — disabled-by-default, automation-bypass interaction, idempotent no-op, actor-fallback chain, one-issue-failure-doesn't-block-sibling, wrong-project statusId rejected, board-payload `prLinkSummary` aggregation (open/merged/closed-excluded/relation-not-loaded-stays-undefined), both clients' live-status methods (SSRF-guarded, DNS-mocked, zero real egress); 4 new tenant-isolation-matrix rows (108/108 BLOCKED); `apps/web/e2e/pr-auto-transition.spec.ts` (6 cases × desktop+mobile, all green) — badge open→merged flip, settings toggle persists across reload, a **locally-HMAC-signed `merged` webhook drives a real status transition** (asserted against the REST issue endpoint, not just the UI), disabled-by-default regression case, mobile badge-renders-without-overflow; full regression re-verified green (`github-integration.spec.ts`, `gitlab-integration.spec.ts`, `board.spec.ts`, `settings-robustness.spec.ts`, `issue-detail.spec.ts`, `issue-drawer-overlay.spec.ts`). **Live end-to-end verification** (real running API + Postgres): registered a user → connected GitHub → enabled auto-transition targeting "Done" → posted a locally-signed `merged` PR webhook → the issue's `statusId` flipped to "Done" in the same response cycle; board payload showed `prLinkSummary: {open:0, merged:1}`; live-status endpoint correctly degraded with no real GitHub egress. **MCP:** 6 new tools — `get_issue_github_live_status`/`get_issue_gitlab_live_status` (real live-status reads) and `get_github_automation_config`/`get_gitlab_automation_config` (deliberately narrower than the REST GET — never return the webhook secret/PAT, even to an admin-scoped token) and `set_github_automation_config`/`set_gitlab_automation_config` (config-only writes, ADMIN-gated); connecting the repo/token itself remains web-UI-only (unchanged, secret-bearing). 104 tools total (was 98). - [x] (P1, M) **Configurable dashboards — Phase 2: cross-sprint trend + cross-workspace scoping + gadget caps — SHIPPED 2026-07-03** — Phase 1 verified strong (AUDIT-PRODUCT.md Pass 12, 8/10); this is what flipped Reporting Behind → Parity, and the natural next increment given that strength (not a new pillar). **Shipped scope:** (1) **cross-sprint velocity trend** — `GET /projects/:id/reports/velocity-trend?sprints=N` (default 6, clamped 1-24; `ReportsService.velocityTrend` queries the N most-recent ACTIVE/COMPLETED sprints DESC + reverses to oldest→newest, mirroring `velocity()`'s committed/completed derivation) rendered as a new `VELOCITY_TREND` gadget visualization — project-wide by design (the gadget's NLQL `query` is validated but not applied, since there's no single issue set to filter across every sprint's own issues) — reuses the exact same `VelocityChart` component the Reports page uses (genuinely "the gadget framework reusing an existing chart", not a bespoke report page), with a `config.sprints` picker in the gadget form. (2) **cross-workspace gadget scoping** — audited the existing implementation (already correctly project-derived, never keyed off the app's global "active workspace" selection) and closed the *verification* gap the audit actually flagged: a new e2e case proves a user in two workspaces gets fully correct, non-leaking dashboard content and editability when deep-linking into a project in the NON-active workspace (explicitly set workspace A active via the Pulse page's selector, then deep-link into workspace B's project — correct tabs/gadgets/write-affordances, zero cross-tenant leakage). Also caught and fixed a **real, previously-uncaught race** while building this: creating a second dashboard while one was already selected briefly snapped the selection back to dashboard #1 before the list-refetch landed (`DashboardsPage`'s "reset selection if not in the list" effect saw a stale pre-refetch list) — fixed by having `useCreateDashboard`'s `onSuccess` synchronously append the new dashboard to the cached list instead of only invalidating (async). (3) **Phase-1 scope deltas revisited**: drag-to-reorder gadgets — v1's up/down buttons replaced with a real dnd-kit sortable grid (`rectSortingStrategy`, a dedicated grab handle so Edit/Delete stay clickable), using a numeric fractional-midpoint `config.position` (deliberately NOT the string `rankBetween` helper — a JSON-embedded, low-cardinality field where legacy integer positions aren't valid fractional-indexing keys; the midpoint-of-neighbors approach keeps the same "touch only the moved row" guarantee) computed client-side and PATCHed for the one moved gadget only, with optimistic cache reordering; a project's first-ever dashboard is now pre-populated with 3 starter gadgets (Open issues / Status overview / My open issues) — every dashboard after that still starts empty. (4) **engineering hardening**: `MAX_DASHBOARDS_PER_PROJECT` (20) / `MAX_GADGETS_PER_DASHBOARD` (30) caps (`BadRequestException` before insert, mirroring `DASHBOARD_ISSUES_CAP`) and `getDashboardData`'s per-gadget evaluation loop parallelized with `Promise.all` (was sequential — up to ~200 serial NLQL evaluations per read before the cap existed). **Tests:** new API unit tests for cap rejection (dashboards + gadgets), default-gadget seeding, `VELOCITY_TREND` gadget evaluation (ignores query, defaults to 6 sprints, per-gadget error on failure) and `velocityTrend` aggregation/clamping (API suite 1731→1800, shared with the sibling PR-auto-transition batch landing the same day); 1 new tenant-isolation-matrix row for the trend endpoint (108/108 blocked); `apps/web/e2e/dashboards-phase2.spec.ts` (6 cases × desktop+mobile, all green) — trend gadget rendering (real sprint data, reuses `VelocityChart`), default-gadget seeding + second-dashboard-stays-empty, drag-to-reorder via a real `page.mouse` sequence (Playwright's own `dragTo` doesn't trigger dnd-kit, which listens on Pointer Events) with a `waitForResponse` guard so a reload-immediately-after-drag assertion can't race the async PATCH, and the cross-workspace scoping case; `dashboards.spec.ts` (Phase 1, 12 cases) regression re-verified green. **MCP:** new `get_velocity_trend_report` tool; `dashboardVisualizationEnum`/`dashboardGadgetConfigSchema` extended with `VELOCITY_TREND`/`sprints`/fractional `position`; `create_dashboard`/`create_dashboard_gadget` descriptions updated to name the caps proactively (cap-rejection 400s already surface the API's own message verbatim through the existing error-propagation path) — 104 tools total (was 103 after the sibling PR-auto-transition batch; +1 from this pass). [product-auditor Pass 8/9/10/11/12 flat-3→closed carryover; engineering-auditor Pass-12 P2-2; VISION.md § Better-than-Jira gaps #4]
- [x] (P2, S) **NLQL: an unresolved assignee/sprint name silently matches zero issues instead of erroring — SHIPPED 2026-07-06** — the deliberately-deferred half of the shipped NLQL name-resolution fix (`ec9f02e`; `docs/MCP-QA.md` pass 1, finding 1 residual): `assignee = "Alex Rivera"` and `sprint = "July-B"` now correctly resolve when the name is real, but a *typo'd or nonexistent* name used to return a confident, silent `0 results` instead of a 400 — an agent (or a human) could walk away believing "Alex has nothing assigned" when the real answer is "there is no Alex." **Design:** new shared `resolveQueryNames(query, ctx)` (`packages/shared/src/nlql/validate.ts`) is a fail-loud PREPARE step, deliberately separate from the pure evaluator (`evaluate`/`filterIssues` are UNCHANGED and now explicitly document their silent-fallback behavior for library consumers, locked in by existing tests). It walks the parsed AST's `user`/`sprint`-kind comparisons and flags an operand as unresolved when it is neither `me()` nor opaque-id-shaped (`looksLikeOpaqueId`: no whitespace + length ≥ 20, matching `cuid()`/UUID shape — so a legitimate raw id not in the caller's loaded context, e.g. a former workspace member, still compares silently as before, preserving the "an id not in ctx.users still compares correctly" guarantee) and matches nothing in `ctx.users`/`ctx.sprints`. **Acceptance, met exactly:** all three server evaluation call sites now run this check right after loading context via `loadNlqlEvalContext` — `IssuesService.exportCsv` (also the MCP `list_issues` query-mode oracle) throws `BadRequestException` with `Invalid NLQL query: unknown user "Alex Rivera" — use an exact display name, an id, or me(); see list_users` (sprint: `... see list_sprints`), live-verified over REST (400 on unresolved, 200 with the correct row once a real "Alex Rivera" member existed, 400 again on a fresh typo); the dashboard-gadget evaluator (`dashboards.service.ts`) returns the same message as *that gadget's* `error` field only — verified live with a two-gadget dashboard where one query was bad: the read is still 200, the sibling gadget's `data` renders untouched; the automation engine's condition eval produces a `FAILED` run with the message (mirrors its existing invalid-condition handling exactly — was previously an indistinguishable-from-real `SKIPPED`), live-verified with a bad-condition rule alongside an unconditional sibling rule that still fired. **Web UI (verified, no redesign):** the board query bar evaluates entirely client-side (never server-side), so it's a library consumer, not a fail-loud surface, by design — but `nlqlValidation` was extended to also run `resolveQueryNames` using the already-loaded users/sprints, reusing the existing `data-testid="nlql-error"` affordance so a typo surfaces immediately while typing, not just at export time; caught two real bonus bugs while wiring this: (1) the CSV-export toast was swallowing the API's actual error message entirely (`fetchCsvBlob` never read the response body, always showed "Couldn't export issues.") — now surfaces the real message for every NLQL 400, parser errors included, not just this fix; (2) the board's client-side `filterIssues`/`colorCtx` calls never populated `ctx.sprints` at all, so `sprint = "<name>"` silently never matched on the board/card-colors surface even for a real sprint — fixed via a shared `nlqlUsers`/`nlqlSprints` memo. **MCP:** verified the 400 passes through `list_issues` query mode verbatim (same propagation path as parser errors); `list_issues`' tool description + `apps/mcp/README.md` updated to state the new behavior explicitly (no longer implies `0` could mean either "no match" or "no such user"). **Tests:** 16 new shared vitest (`validate.test.ts`, 146→162) covering resolved-by-id/name/email, unresolved (both fields), `me()` never flagged, opaque-id fallback preserved, IN-list per-candidate checking, parse-error passthrough; 11 new API jest across the three call sites + 1 existing test updated to the new fail-loud contract (issues.csv.spec.ts, dashboards.service.spec.ts, automation-engine.service.spec.ts — 1839→1850, 85 suites); 1 new MCP vitest (110→111); integration suite re-verified green (293/293, unaffected — no fixture used a name-based user/sprint condition); `tsc --noEmit` clean in shared/api/web/mcp. [MCP-QA pass 1, finding 1 residual — see the ticked NLQL fix entry below for the resolved half; docs/MCP-QA.md pass-2 finding 7 also closed by this]
- [x] (P2, M) **Gitea integration v1 — two-way link (GitLab follow-up) — SHIPPED 2026-07-06** — the third self-hosted forge from the "GitLab/Gitea" Better-than-Jira gap, now that both GitHub (HMAC) and GitLab (shared-secret-token) v1s are shipped and the shared `common/issue-key.util.ts` extraction + parallel-table pattern are established precedents mirrored here. Gitea's webhook scheme is HMAC-SHA256 like GitHub's (`X-Gitea-Signature` header, hex-encoded, no "sha256=" prefix, verified against the raw body) — NOT GitLab's plain shared-secret compare — so `GithubModule`'s signature-verification shape was the closer template than `GitlabModule`'s token-verification shape, even though the DB tables (`GiteaIntegration`/`IssueGiteaLink`) mirror both providers' shape. **Shipped scope, matching the acceptance criteria exactly:** additive `GiteaIntegration` (`giteaBaseUrl` REQUIRED — Gitea has no SaaS default, unlike GitLab's gitlab.com-defaulted field; `repoFullName` "owner/repo") / `IssueGiteaLink` (PR/COMMIT/BRANCH, unique `[issueId, kind, externalId]`) parallel tables, migration `20260706000000_add_gitea_integration` (verified zero-drift via `prisma migrate diff`); ADMIN-gated `PUT/GET/DELETE /projects/:projectId/gitea` (GET role-shaped via `getEffectiveProjectRole`) + `GET /issues/:issueId/gitea-links`, both `@RequireScope`-gated with new `gitea:read`/`gitea:write` `PAT_SCOPES`; public `POST /gitea/webhook/:projectId` verifying `X-Gitea-Signature` HMAC-SHA256 against the raw body (constant-time compare, mirrors `verifyGithubSignature`'s contract minus the "sha256=" prefix); `push`/`pull_request` event parsing scoped to the project's own issue-key prefix via the shared `common/issue-key.util.ts` — Gitea's payload field paths were verified against its documented API structs (`api.PushPayload`/`api.PullRequestPayload`) rather than assumed byte-identical to GitHub's: commit author objects carry a `username` field directly (like GitHub, unlike GitLab), but the top-level `pusher`/`sender` user object's display name is `login` (not `username`) — both are checked with a fallback chain so a minor version difference degrades gracefully; issue-drawer Development section renders Gitea links beside GitHub/GitLab (three-provider layout, same pattern as the GitHub+GitLab side-by-side precedent, including the live-status-spinner precedent's absence — v1 deliberately has NO live-status client, links only); unit tests mirroring `github-signature.util.spec.ts`/`github.service.spec.ts` coverage depth (55 new tests: signature verification valid/invalid/missing/tampered/malformed/wrong-prefix, AES round-trip, ADMIN gating incl. effective-project-ADMIN override, tenant isolation, push/PR event parsing incl. multi-key commits/wrong-project-ignored/idempotent re-delivery/BRANCH-from-branch-name); e2e with a locally-HMAC-signed fake webhook (zero real Gitea egress) desktop+mobile (`gitea-integration.spec.ts`, 4 scenarios × 2 = 8/8 green); tenant-isolation matrix rows for the new endpoints (+4, all BLOCKED) + PAT-scope-rollout matrix rows (+4, DENY+ALLOW); MCP: `list_issue_gitea_links` following the compact/verbose/pagination envelope, mirroring `list_issue_gitlab_links`. **Tests:** 1905 total API tests green (85→88 suites); 301 integration tests green (293→301); `gitea-integration.spec.ts` 8/8 e2e green; `github-integration`/`gitlab-integration`/`issue-detail`/`issue-drawer-overlay`/`settings-robustness` regression re-verified green (50/50); `tsc --noEmit` clean shared/api/web/mcp. **Live round-trip verification** (real running API, not just unit/e2e): valid PR webhook → `200 {linksUpserted:1}`; tampered signature → `401`; identical replay → `200 {linksUpserted:1}` with the SAME link row id (idempotent); missing-signature push → `401`; a cross-project issue key referenced from a different project's webhook → `linksUpserted:0` (never cross-links); a PAT scoped to `gitea:read` → `200` on `GET /issues/:id/gitea-links`, a PAT without that scope → `403 "This token does not have the required scope: gitea:read"`. **MCP:** `list_issue_gitea_links` — 105 tools total (was 104); `apps/mcp/README.md` scope table/tool table/counts updated; configuring the integration itself deliberately NOT exposed (admin-only, secret-bearing, mirrors the GitHub/GitLab decision) — noted in the "Not exposed over MCP" table. [ROADMAP Phase 9; VISION.md § Better-than-Jira gaps #3, § The thesis "Open & extensible"]
- [x] (P2, M) **Dashboard sharing — public read-only embed (share-token reuse) — SHIPPED 2026-07-06** — reuse of the same `ShareToken` pattern already used for public boards, so a dashboard (not just a board) can be published read-only, no-login, to a URL a manager or client can bookmark (AUDIT-PRODUCT.md Pass 12, Ideation #1). **Schema choice (studied the board share-token surface end-to-end first):** a **parallel `DashboardShareToken` table** rather than widening `ShareToken` with a nullable `dashboardId` — `ShareToken.projectId` is a required, non-nullable column every existing read/write path (`ShareTokensService`, `PublicService`, its own unit tests) treats as always-present, and a board link must never be presentable as a dashboard link or vice versa; a parallel table (mirroring `ShareToken` field-for-field: `tokenHash` unique, `createdById`, `revokedAt`) keeps both FKs required + `onDelete: Cascade` precise, avoids a discriminator column / nullable-FK CHECK constraint, and needed zero changes to the already-tested `ShareToken` surface — additive migration `20260706120000_add_dashboard_share_tokens`, verified zero-drift. **Backend:** `DashboardShareTokensModule` (`apps/api/src/dashboard-share-tokens/`) mirrors `ShareTokensController`/`Service` exactly — ADMIN-gated `POST/GET /dashboards/:id/share-tokens` (mint/list) + `DELETE /dashboards/:id/share-tokens/:tokenId` (revoke), scope-gated `projects:write`/`projects:read` like the board equivalent, 404-not-403 on cross-dashboard token access (no oracle). Public `GET /public/dashboard/:token` (new route on the existing `PublicController`, same 60 req/60s `@Throttle`) delegates to a new `DashboardsService.getPublicDashboardData(dashboardId)` — no membership check (a valid, non-revoked token IS the authorization) — which reuses the exact same gadget-evaluation core `getDashboardData` uses (refactored into a shared `evaluateDashboardGadgets` private method) so there is no parallel evaluation path to drift. **`me()` degradation (decided + implemented + tested):** an anonymous public viewer has no `userId`; the evaluator's documented library-consumer fallback (`ctx.currentUserId ?? null`) would silently turn `assignee = me()` into "unassigned" — a confidently-wrong result, not a crash, but not correct either. Decided this must fail loud per-gadget instead: new shared `queryReferencesMe(query)` (`packages/shared/src/nlql/validate.ts`, AST-walk over comparison/IN operands, no false-positive on a quoted string literal containing the text `"me()"`) is checked before evaluation for an anonymous caller; a `me()`-referencing gadget gets an explicit `error: "This gadget uses me() and needs a signed-in user — not available on a public dashboard link."` instead of data — verified live: the seeded "My open issues" default gadget (`assignee = me() AND statusCategory != DONE`) shows this exact error on a public link while sibling STAT/BREAKDOWN gadgets render normally. BURNDOWN/VELOCITY_TREND gadgets (which call into `ReportsService`, itself membership-gated) get a new `options.skipMembershipCheck` param set only by this anonymous path — never derived from request input, never reachable via any HTTP controller — so they still render correctly for a public viewer instead of erroring on a membership check that makes no sense for a share-token-authorized read. **Web:** `/share/dashboard/:token` (`SharedDashboardPage.tsx`, chromeless like `/share/:token`) renders the same gadget-visualization components the authenticated dashboard grid uses (`GadgetResultBody`/`VISUALIZATION_LABELS` extracted from `GadgetCard.tsx` for reuse — zero duplicated visualization logic) with no add/edit/delete/reorder affordance and a read-only banner; `DashboardShareModal.tsx` (mint/list/revoke UI, mirrors `ShareSection`) opens from a new ADMIN-only "Share" button on the Dashboards page toolbar. All Dispatch tokens, all `data-testid` hooks preserved/added (`dashboard-share`, `create-dashboard-share-token-btn`, `dashboard-share-token-row`, `revoke-dashboard-share-token-btn`, `shared-dashboard-header`, etc). **Tests:** 24 new API unit tests (1906→1930, 88→90 suites) — mint/revoke ADMIN-only + missing-dashboard 404 (`dashboard-share-tokens.service.spec.ts`), public-token-validation + revoked-404 + `me()`-error-surfaces-unchanged (`public.service.spec.ts`), and 6 new `DashboardsService.getPublicDashboardData` cases incl. the `me()` degradation and the BURNDOWN/VELOCITY_TREND `skipMembershipCheck` wiring; 3 new pat-scope-rollout matrix rows + 3 new tenant-isolation matrix rows (the public `GET /public/dashboard/:token` route is `@Public()` and deliberately excluded, like the board equivalent) — integration suite 301→307, all green; 7 new shared vitest for `queryReferencesMe` (164→171); `apps/web/e2e/dashboard-share-link.spec.ts` (7 cases × desktop+mobile = 14, all green) — mint → public fetch renders seeded gadgets → `me()`-gadget shows its error → revoke → 404-equivalent error page, plus the admin mint/revoke UI flow and a mobile no-overflow case; full regression re-verified green (`share-link.spec.ts` 14/14, `dashboards.spec.ts` 12/12, `dashboards-phase2.spec.ts` 12/12); `tsc --noEmit` clean shared/api/web/mcp. **MCP: not applicable** — this is a public-web surface (an anonymous, no-token browser view), not an agent action; no `@next-lane/mcp` tool added, per the DoD convention for surfaces with no agent-appropriate shape (mint/revoke ARE ordinary ADMIN-authenticated actions and could be added later if an agent workflow needs to mint links programmatically, but the public read side itself has no MCP-relevant caller). [product-auditor Pass-12 Ideation #1; VISION.md § Better-than-Jira gaps #4]

- [x] (P1, S) **NLQL silently returned wrong (empty) results for name/email-based person and name-based sprint queries — SHIPPED 2026-07-03** (`docs/MCP-QA.md` pass 1, finding 1 — blocked "what is X working on?" / "what's left in sprint N?", the two most common PM questions after epic status; `assignee = "Alex Rivera"` and `sprint = "July-B"` both silently returned 0 items with no error). Root cause: the shared evaluator (`packages/shared/src/nlql/evaluator.ts`) already supported name/email resolution for `user`-kind fields via `ctx.users`, but every server-side evaluation call site (`IssuesService.exportCsv`, the dashboards gadget evaluator, the automation engine's condition eval) only ever populated `{currentUserId}`/`{customFieldDefs}` — `ctx.users` was always empty; separately, `sprint` was declared `kind: 'id'` in `fields.ts`, so name matching wasn't implemented at all despite the autocomplete surface (`suggest.ts`) already advertising it. Fix: added a `sprint` `FieldKind` + `ctx.sprints` (mirrors the existing `resolveUserOperand` — accepts an id OR an exact, case-insensitive name, same unresolved-name-matches-nothing-no-error semantics as `user`-kind fields, deliberately kept consistent rather than redesigned this pass); added `getReferencedFieldKinds(query)` (shared) so a call site can batch-load only the side-context a query actually needs; wired all three server call sites through a new `loadNlqlEvalContext` helper (`apps/api/src/common/nlql-eval-context.util.ts`) — `exportCsv` and the dashboard-gadget evaluator load conditionally per query/per-dashboard (one round trip, not per issue/per gadget), the automation engine (event-driven, must stay cheap) loads conditionally per triggered-rule-batch. Live-verified over REST against a real fixture (named user + sprint, real issues): `assignee = "Alex Rivera"` and `assignee = "<email>"` → 5/5 correct (0/5 before the fix, id-based lookup already worked before and after); `sprint = "July-B"` → 4/4 correct (0/4 before). 18 new shared vitest tests (evaluator + `getReferencedFieldKinds`, 128→146) + 27 new API jest tests across the three call sites (issues/dashboards/automations, 1650→1683), all green; full shared vitest and API jest suites re-run green. **Note for future work (not fixed this pass, by design):** an assignee/sprint name that resolves to no known entity still silently matches zero issues rather than 400ing — kept consistent with existing `user`-kind semantics rather than inventing a new error channel; worth a follow-up pass if agents keep hitting it blind (a "0 results, and here's why" hint would need real design work, not a one-line change).

- [x] (P1, M) **Per-project agent context memory over MCP — SHIPPED 2026-07-03** (founder directive, top priority: "for each project a LLM via the MCP should be able to dump their context for the next run or agent… We should prompt it to do so. It should keep up to date from this." + "It should be a skill."). One shared handoff document per project (`ProjectAgentContext`, 64 KB cap): `GET/PUT /projects/:id/agent-context` (VIEWER+ read / effective-MEMBER+ write, PAT-scoped, tenant-isolation rows added), MCP `get_project_context` / `update_project_context` (read-first + hand-off-last prompting in the tool descriptions AND the MCP server's protocol-level `instructions` — every connecting client gets it), measured staleness (`changesSinceUpdate` from ActivityLog+AuditEvent newer than the doc — live-verified 0→1 after an issue create), realtime `project-agent-context.updated` event, and the distributable **`skills/project-context` Agent Skill** (+ `skills/README.md`, install docs in `apps/mcp/README.md` § "Ship your agent with memory" + main README). 91 MCP tools (was 89). Evidence: live stdio round-trip (initialize → instructions present → update → get, `contentBytes`/`updatedBy`/staleness all correct). **Follow-up (P2) — ✅ shipped 2026-07-03: human-visible "Agent context" panel in the project web UI.** New `AgentContextSection.tsx` on project Settings (`SettingsPage.tsx`, placed after Members/GitLab, matching section-card conventions): one-line explainer ("Persistent handoff memory for AI agents working in this project — also editable by humans"), content rendered via the app's existing `MarkdownRenderer` with an empty-state message, a metadata row (relative `updatedAt` + `updatedBy` name) and an amber "N changes since last update" staleness pill (reusing the existing amber-pill pattern), edit-in-place (Edit → textarea prefilled → Save/Cancel, toast on save, inline error — not just a toast — on the 64 KB 400) gated on EFFECTIVE project MEMBER+ (computed from the project's effective-members list, same source as `MembersSection`, falling back to workspace role while it loads); VIEWERs get a read-only render with no Edit control. Realtime: `SettingsPage` now subscribes via the existing `useBoardRealtime` project-socket hook, which gained a `project-agent-context.updated` case (someone saved) and an issue-activity case (the staleness count moves on project activity) both invalidating the new `qk.projectAgentContext(projectId)` key — verified live, no reload, from a second browser session. New `apps/web/e2e/agent-context.spec.ts` (12 cases × desktop 1280 + mobile 393, all green): empty state, write→renders-as-markdown→survives reload, a second live session sees the save with no reload, the staleness pill appearing live after another session creates an issue, a workspace VIEWER sees read-only, and the 64 KB cap surfacing as an inline error without discarding the draft; regression re-verified green: `settings-robustness.spec.ts` (15), `gitlab-integration.spec.ts` (5), `project-members.spec.ts` (8).

- [x] (P1, M) **Agent Experience (AX) batch — founder-relayed field report from a real MCP agent user (2026-07-02, photo of an agent's honest review after organizing 40+ tickets over `@next-lane/mcp`) — ✅ shipped 2026-07-03 (both phases).** The agent's verdict validates the agent-native pillar ("closer to Linear in ergonomics… would use it again") and lists five concrete frictions — each becomes an acceptance criterion. Split in two phases so Phase A could run while the GitLab builder held `apps/mcp`:
  - [x] **Phase A (API + UI, no `apps/mcp` touches) — ✅ shipped 2026-07-03.** (1) `Issue.startDate DateTime?` — additive migration (`20260703030000_add_issue_start_date`); create/update DTOs (`startDate?: string`, `IsDateString`, null clears, cross-field `startDate <= dueDate` validation enforced in `IssuesService` — 400 otherwise, mirrors dueDate's activity-log entry); issue drawer `StartDateField` beside Due date (same picker/clear-affordance UX); CSV export "Start Date" column beside "Due Date" (20 columns) + CSV/Jira importer maps a "Start Date" column when present (GitHub/Linear exports don't have one — noted, not attempted); NLQL `startDate`/`start` field registered everywhere `dueDate` is (field allowlist, evaluator, autocomplete suggestions, `docs-site/guide/features.md`); Roadmap/Gantt timeline's epic-window derivation now prioritizes the epic issue's own `startDate`→`dueDate` range over the existing child-sprint-dates/createdAt fallback (`RoadmapEpicDto.fromOwnDates`), keeping the fallback chain intact when `startDate` is absent. The agent had to "jam start dates into description first lines" — this is a data-model gap, not just an MCP gap, now closed. Unit tests: create/update validation (reject inverted range, null clears, boundary-inclusive equal dates), NLQL startDate filtering/sorting mirroring dueDate, CSV export/import spec updates, roadmap own-dates-priority cases — API suite 1650 green. E2e: `start-date.spec.ts` (drawer set/clear/persist-after-reload + inverted-range rejection, 8/8 desktop+mobile) + `roadmap.spec.ts` extended with an own-dates-priority case; `due-date.spec.ts` regression re-verified green (fixed a pre-existing `getByLabel` strict-mode flake found while touching this area). **Phase-B handoff:** `IssueDto.startDate: string | null` (ISO 8601, same null semantics as `dueDate`); DTO field name is `startDate` on both create/update; no MCP changes made in this phase.
  - [x] **Phase B (MCP surface) — ✅ shipped 2026-07-03**, `apps/mcp` only, every fix composed from existing REST endpoints (no API/schema changes): (2) `list_issues` gains an NLQL `query` param — passed to `GET /projects/:id/issues.csv?q=` (the only existing endpoint that runs the real parser/evaluator server-side) as a match-oracle, then hydrated into full issue objects via the existing cursor-paginated `GET /issues`; an invalid query surfaces the API's own parser message (`Invalid NLQL query: ...`), not a generic failure — **missing-endpoint note:** a JSON NLQL-filtered issue-list endpoint would collapse this to one call and drop the id-hydration page cap (10 pages × 200 issues); filed for whoever next touches `apps/api/src/issues`. (3) Token-efficiency sweep across **every** `list_*`/`search_issues` tool (25 tools): uniform `{ items, total?, limit, offset?, hasMore }` envelope, a hand-picked compact field set per resource (`list_issues` → `key/title/status/assignee/priority/type/startDate`) with `verbose: true` for the full DTO, `limit`/`offset` defaulting to 50 (max 200) — live-verified against a 44-ticket fixture: the same `list_issues` call is 11 KB compact / 3.8 KB NLQL-narrowed (was 84 KB verbose, i.e. the field report's ~150 KB regime). (4) Wrong-project guard: `create_issue` response always echoes `project: {id, key, name}`; description tells agents to confirm the target; optional `expectedProjectKey` 400s on mismatch **before** creating anything (the agent filed 9 issues into the wrong project with no nudge — this closes it at the point of the mistake, not after). (5) `get_epic_overview` tool: one REST call (`GET /issues/:id`, the same parent→children relation the issue drawer's sub-tasks list uses) returns epic + compact children + per-status rollup + `{done, total, fraction}` progress; `startDate` exposed on `create_issue`/`update_issue`/`list_issues` (compact + verbose) — `get_epic_overview` children can't carry it because the API's `IssueRefDto` doesn't project `assignee`/`startDate` on that relation, filed as a small follow-up. 89 tools total (38 read / 51 write, up from 88/37/51); `apps/mcp/README.md` counts/tables updated (and a stray uncommitted GitLab-tools README claim from a since-abandoned concurrent edit was removed as untrue-against-committed-code, per this task's own instructions).
  - **QA — ✅ done.** 18 new MCP unit tests (80 total, all green) covering: `query` pass-through + invalid-NLQL error propagation, compact-vs-verbose shape for `list_issues`/`list_boards`/`list_users`/`list_notifications`, `limit`/`offset`/`total`/`hasMore` on a generic list tool, `expectedProjectKey` match/mismatch/no-op, `get_epic_overview` composition (progress, status breakdown, single-call assertion) including the zero-children case, and `startDate` on create/update/list. Live-run evidence (real API + Postgres, 44-ticket fixture project): epic overview answered in **one** tool call; NLQL-filtered "in-progress" list was 3.8 KB, not 150 KB. E2e for the drawer startDate + NLQL unit tests for the new field — done in Phase A.

- [x] (P1, S) **Mobile — Swimlanes v2's own paint regression** ✅ fixed 2026-07-02 — the board's "Group by" and filter-chip dropdown menus rendered **completely invisible** on a real 393px phone (functionally clickable, `isVisible()` true, but zero rendered pixels — Chromium's `overflow-x-clip` on the app-shell root suppressed paint of the entire absolutely-positioned menu once it extended past the viewport, not just the overflowing slice), the pre-existing quick-filter chip row silently clipped "Recently updated" off-canvas with no scroll cue, and the new "Group by" chip's label wrapped to two lines unlike every sibling chip (AUDIT-PRODUCT.md Pass 12, top-ranked finding — mobile went from "missing native app" to actively regressing on this pass's own flagship feature). Fixed at the pattern level: a new portalled, viewport-clamped `<DropdownPanel>` component (`apps/web/src/components/ui/DropdownPanel.tsx` — `createPortal`-to-`document.body`, measures the trigger+panel and clamps `position: fixed` fully inside the viewport on open/resize/scroll, flips above the trigger when there's no room below) replaces every toolbar `position: absolute` menu in `BoardPage.tsx` (Group by, Labels, Type, Priority filters, saved-filter menu, NLQL help — not just the two the audit named, the whole shared pattern); the quick-filter chip row gets real `overflow-x: auto` + `shrink-0` chips + the house `.nl-scroll` thin-scrollbar treatment (was `overflow-x: visible`, silently clipping); the "Group by" chip gets `whitespace-nowrap`/`shrink-0` matching its siblings. **Verification (paint-level, not just DOM presence):** two new e2e cases in `swimlanes.spec.ts` open the Group-by and Priority menus at 393px, assert the panel's `boundingBox()` is fully inside the viewport AND decode a real Playwright screenshot of the panel via the browser's own `<canvas>`/`getImageData` to assert non-trivial (>5) distinct sampled colors — closing the exact "isVisible() lies" gap the audit called out, via a genuine visibility-by-composite check, not a visibility-by-style one; two new cases in `quick-filters.spec.ts` assert `overflow-x: auto` and that "Recently updated" is fully reachable after scrolling, plus that the "Group by" chip stays single-line. 106/106 e2e green desktop+mobile (`swimlanes.spec.ts`, `board.spec.ts`, `quick-filters.spec.ts`, `nav-sidebar.spec.ts`); `tsc --noEmit` + `pnpm --filter @next-lane/web build` clean. - [x] (P1, M) **In-app SSO/OIDC admin configuration screen** ✅ shipped 2026-07-02 — an instance admin now sets provider issuer URL/client ID/client secret/button label from a new settings page (`/admin/sso`) instead of an env-var edit + API redeploy — closing the first of the two "Admin controls" blockers (product-auditor Pass-12 rank #4). **Schema (additive):** singleton `OidcConfig` table (`enabled`, `issuerUrl`, `clientId`, `clientSecretEncrypted`, `label`) + `User.isInstanceAdmin` (migration `20260703000000_add_instance_admin_oidc_config`, hand-authored per the established `GithubIntegration`-migration precedent) — no existing instance-admin concept existed, so this is the new gate: strictly narrower than workspace `Membership.role: ADMIN` (appropriate for a secret that isn't scoped to any one workspace), defaulting the very first user on a fresh install to `true` (`AuthService.register`'s `isFirstUser` check, mirrored in `OidcService.findOrProvisionUser` for a first-ever SSO login) and backfilling the oldest existing user on an already-provisioned install (the migration's own `UPDATE`); `demo@nextlane.dev` is explicitly `isInstanceAdmin: true` in `seed.ts` for local/CI determinism. **Encryption:** the GitHub PAT's AES-256-GCM implementation was extracted into a shared `apps/api/src/common/crypto/secret-crypto.util.ts` (`deriveEncryptionKey`/`encryptSecret`/`decryptSecret`) so `github-crypto.util.ts` and the new `auth/oidc/oidc-secret-crypto.util.ts` (`OIDC_CONFIG_ENCRYPTION_KEY`, falling back to `JWT_SECRET` — zero-config self-host path unaffected) delegate to ONE audited primitive instead of a second copy; the GitHub module's behavior/env vars/tests are byte-for-byte unchanged (its own spec still passes verbatim). **Precedence + live effect (the acceptance criterion that mattered most):** `OIDC_ISSUER_URL`/`OIDC_CLIENT_ID`/`OIDC_CLIENT_SECRET` env vars still WIN over the DB config when all three are set (12-factor — operators who pin config in the environment keep that behavior unchanged; the settings-page form renders a read-only "env-managed" banner in that state and the server rejects a write outright rather than silently no-op'ing it); a new `OidcConfigService` (`apps/api/src/admin-settings/`) resolves the *effective* config fresh on every call (env branch, else an enabled+fully-populated DB row) — `OidcController.login`/`callback` and `AuthController.providers()` (`GET /auth/providers`, the login page's capability probe) now call it instead of the old sync env-only `isOidcConfigured()`, and `OidcService`'s OIDC-client discovery cache is keyed by a fingerprint (issuer+client id+secret hash) of the *effective* config rather than being a process-lifetime singleton — so saving from the UI takes effect on the very next login attempt, confirmed live end-to-end (`PATCH /admin/oidc-config` → immediate `GET /auth/providers` flip, no restart, verified via curl during build). **API:** `AdminSettingsModule` (`admin-settings.controller.ts`/`.service.ts`, `GET`/`PATCH /admin/oidc-config`) — instance-admin gated via a new `assertInstanceAdmin` helper alongside `assertProjectRole`/`assertProjectMember` in `common/membership.util.ts`; the client secret is write-only (`clientSecret?` in the PATCH DTO, omit to keep the stored value) and NEVER returned by any GET — `OidcConfigDto.hasClientSecret: true` only; enabling without a fully-populated config (issuer + client id + a secret, new-or-already-stored) is rejected with a friendly 400. Deliberately no import cycle: `AdminSettingsModule` depends on neither `AuthModule` nor `OidcModule` (both of which depend on IT for `OidcConfigService`) — `PrismaService` is global, `CurrentUser`/`AuthUser` are plain decorator imports. **Frontend:** `AdminSsoSettingsPage.tsx` (route `/admin/sso`, sidebar entry `nav-sidebar-admin-sso` gated on `useAuth().user?.isInstanceAdmin` — both in `AppSidebar` and `MobileSidebarDrawer` via the shared `SidebarNavContent`) — enabled toggle, issuer/client id/secret/label fields, secret input shows `placeholder="••• saved"` once `hasClientSecret`, inline validation, success/error toasts, an unsaved-changes `beforeunload` guard, and the env-managed read-only banner; `UserDto` deliberately stays unchanged (still embedded everywhere as a reference — comment author, issue assignee, attachment uploader) and a new `MeDto extends UserDto` (adds `isInstanceAdmin`) is used ONLY for "yourself" (login/register/`GET`+`PATCH /auth/me`/SSO session) so this didn't ripple a required field through a dozen unrelated mappers. **Tests:** 33 new API unit tests (`secret-crypto.util.spec.ts`, `oidc-secret-crypto.util.spec.ts`, `oidc-config.service.spec.ts` — env>DB precedence, secret never in `toDto()`, disabled/missing-field DB configs ignored — `admin-settings.service.spec.ts` — instance-admin gate on both routes, env-pinned-deployment write rejection, enable-requires-all-fields validation, partial-merge semantics) plus the existing `oidc.service.spec.ts`/`oidc.controller.spec.ts`/`auth.controller.spec.ts` updated for the new `OidcConfigService` dependency (behavior-preserving refactor, not new coverage) — **1554 API tests green** (was 1521); `tsc --noEmit` + `pnpm --filter @next-lane/api build` clean. New `apps/web/e2e/admin-sso-settings.spec.ts` (non-admin: no sidebar entry + access-denied on direct route navigation; instance admin: configure+enable via per-keystroke typing, persists across reload, unsaved-changes indicator, login page's "Continue with `<label>`" button appears with zero API restart, disabling removes it — desktop AND mobile, including a drawer-nav path) — 27/27 green (7 correctly skipped on the `mobile-chrome` Playwright *project* specifically to avoid a real cross-project race on the shared singleton `OidcConfig` row reproduced during QA — both viewports are still fully exercised via nested `test.use({viewport})` sub-describes within the one project that runs them, serialized); `sso.spec.ts`/`auth.spec.ts`/`password-reset.spec.ts`/`realtime-auth.spec.ts`/`nav-sidebar.spec.ts`/`workspace-switcher.spec.ts` full regression re-verified green desktop+mobile (127 tests), zero regressions; `tsc --noEmit` + `pnpm --filter @next-lane/web build` clean. **MCP:** deliberately **not exposed** — instance SSO secrets are not agent-appropriate (same shape of risk as the already-skipped GitHub-integration-config tools); noted in `apps/mcp/README.md`'s "Not exposed over MCP" table. SAML, multi-provider, and JIT workspace/role provisioning remain the tracked Phase 2 follow-up below; per-project role override (next two items) has since shipped both its schema+backend AND frontend UI slices, same day — the "Admin controls" gap is now fully closed. [product-auditor Pass-12 Backlog-Groomer Ingest #4; VISION.md § Better-than-Jira scorecard, Admin controls row]
- [x] (P1, M) **Per-project role override — schema + backend (Phase 1 of 2)** ✅ shipped 2026-07-02 — a `ProjectMembership` layer above the workspace-wide `Membership` so a user can be Viewer on Project A and Admin on Project B in the same workspace; closes the second (and, pending the frontend UI slice below, final) "Admin controls" blocker (schema-confirmed absent since Pass 9). **Schema (additive):** `ProjectMembership { id, projectId, userId, role, createdAt, updatedAt }` (migration `20260703010000_add_project_membership`, hand-authored per the established `GithubIntegration`/`OidcConfig` precedent — `prisma migrate dev` is non-interactive-unsupported in this environment), `@@unique([projectId, userId])`. Sparse by design: NO backfill for existing members — absence of a row means "inherit the workspace role." **Resolution rule (single source of truth):** a new `getEffectiveProjectRole(prisma, userId, workspaceId, projectId)` in `apps/api/src/common/membership.util.ts` — (1) the user MUST have a workspace `Membership` row at all, else `null` (a stray `ProjectMembership` row never grants access on its own — tenant isolation); (2) a workspace ADMIN always resolves to ADMIN, ignoring any override row (belt-and-braces: the write path also refuses to create one for an admin); (3) otherwise an existing override row's role WINS over the workspace role — both ELEVATING (MEMBER → project ADMIN) and RESTRICTING (MEMBER → project VIEWER, still read-only, never locked out of viewing); (4) absent an override, the workspace role applies unchanged. **Zero call-site changes needed:** `assertProjectRole` — already the single chokepoint every project-scoped write/role check in the codebase calls (labels, statuses, sprints, board, workflows, webhooks, versions, components, dashboards, share-tokens, GitHub integration, issue-templates, custom-fields, work-logs, automations, checklist, issue-links, poker, standups, saved-filters, attachments, issues) — now routes its internal role lookup through `getEffectiveProjectRole` instead of the raw workspace `Membership.role`; every one of those ~140 call sites gets override support for free with a one-function change. `assertProjectMember` (membership-only, no role check — used for read access) is deliberately UNCHANGED: it stays workspace-membership-based, so a VIEWER-restricted user still keeps read access to the project (confirmed live: restricted user got `GET /projects/:id` → 200, `POST .../labels` → 403). **REST** (`apps/api/src/project-memberships/`, new `ProjectMembershipsModule`/`Controller`/`Service`): `GET /projects/:id/members` (every effective member — workspace role, effective role, `isOverride` flag; any workspace member may list); `PUT /projects/:id/members/:userId/role` (set/replace an override; requires EFFECTIVE project ADMIN — a project-level override can itself grant this, not just workspace ADMIN, confirmed live; 404 if the target isn't a workspace member; 400 refuses to override a workspace ADMIN); `DELETE .../role` (clear an override, revert to inherited; 404 if none exists). Both mutations audit-logged (`project_membership.override_set`/`_clear`, mirroring `membership.add`/`.role_change`/`.remove`); no realtime socket event added (mirrors the workspace-membership precedent, which is audit-only too). **Shared types:** `ProjectMemberDto`/`SetProjectRoleOverrideDto` in `packages/shared` (no `Role` redefinition). **Tests:** 9 new `membership.util.spec.ts` cases (elevate, restrict, admin-override-ignored-and-short-circuited, tenant-isolation-without-workspace-membership) + 13 new `project-memberships.service.spec.ts` cases — 1575 API tests green (was 1562; the jump also reflects `projectMembership: { findUnique: ... }` added to ~20 existing service-spec Prisma mocks whose `assertProjectRole` call path now reaches the new query). 3 new tenant-isolation-matrix rows (`GET .../members`, `PUT`/`DELETE .../members/:userId/role`, all cross-tenant BLOCKED) — 96 rows total. `tsc --noEmit` + `pnpm --filter @next-lane/api build` clean. **Live end-to-end verification** (not just unit tests): registered two users in one workspace, confirmed a plain MEMBER gets 403 archiving a project, set an ADMIN override → 200 archiving that same project; set a VIEWER override → 403 creating a label but still 200 reading the project; confirmed a workspace ADMIN cannot be overridden (400) and a cleared override correctly reverts access. **MCP:** `list_project_role_overrides` (read) / `set_project_role_override` / `remove_project_role_override` (write) — 88 tools total (was 85); role management is agent-appropriate, not secret-bearing, so unlike the GitHub/SSO config surfaces this one IS wired up. **Frontend UI (Phase 2 of 2) — ✅ shipped 2026-07-02, see the ticked entry below.** [product-auditor Pass 9/10/11/12 carryover, rated 3 vs 5; VISION.md § Better-than-Jira gaps #2]
- [x] (P2, M) **Per-project role override — frontend UI (Phase 2 of 2)** ✅ shipped 2026-07-02 — depended on the schema+backend slice above (`GET`/`PUT`/`DELETE /projects/:id/members[/:userId/role]`, `ProjectMemberDto` in `packages/shared`), consumed as-is with zero backend changes. A new "Members" section on project Settings (`apps/web/src/components/settings/MembersSection.tsx`, between Project details and Columns, matching every neighboring settings-section convention — `Section`-card shell, `LoadingState`/`ErrorState`, dashed-border empty state) lists every effective member (avatar/name/email, workspace-role chip, effective-role control, "Inherited" vs "Override" badge — amber for override, matches the design system's amber scale rather than a new color). An effective-project-ADMIN viewer gets a role `<select>` (ADMIN/MEMBER/VIEWER, no-op skipped when the picked role already matches) per row plus a "Revert to inherited" action behind a `ConfirmDialog` (satisfies the "ConfirmDialog on remove" acceptance criterion) on overridden rows; a workspace ADMIN's row is a disabled fixed-ADMIN control with a tooltip ("Workspace admins always have full access to every project") mirroring the server's 400; the viewer's own row is never self-editable (mirrors `WorkspaceMembersPage`'s existing "isMe" convention, preventing an accidental project-access self-lockout); a solo-member project shows a "No other members" empty state. **Non-admin read-only decision:** a non-project-admin viewer sees the identical member list with zero controls (no `<select>`, no revert) — matches the existing project-settings convention (`SettingsPage.tsx`'s `editable`/`readonly-hint` pattern) rather than hiding the section outright, since `GET /projects/:id/members` is itself readable by any workspace member. New `apps/web/src/api/projectMembers.ts` (`useProjectMembers`/`useSetProjectRoleOverride`/`useClearProjectRoleOverride`) + `qk.projectMembers(projectId)` in `apps/web/src/api/keys.ts`, invalidate-on-settle (mirrors `ComponentsSection`'s mutation-hook convention — no optimistic update, since a role change isn't latency-sensitive). **e2e** (`apps/web/e2e/project-members.spec.ts`, 4 scenarios × desktop 1280 + mobile 393, 8/8 green): admin sees the effective list with inherited roles; setting a VIEWER override flips the badge AND is verified as a **real** access change, not a cosmetic one — a second, independent Playwright browser context logged in as the target member creates an issue successfully through the real board UI *before* the override (positive-control baseline), then, after the admin sets the override, the identical real-UI create-issue attempt (per-keystroke title) gets a genuine 403 error toast ("Requires MEMBER role in this project") with no issue created (confirmed via reload); "Revert to inherited" restores both the badge and write access (re-verified the same way); a workspace-ADMIN row's control is confirmed disabled with the tooltip; a non-project-admin viewer gets a fully read-only list (no comboboxes, no revert link anywhere in the section). **Mobile fix caught during this pass's own screenshot review** (not shipped un-reviewed, per CLAUDE.md's design-elevation directive to surface screenshots during progression): the row's `flex-wrap` layout crushed the name/email column against the fixed-width role controls at 393px, truncating names to 1-2 characters — fixed by switching the row to `flex-col` below `sm` and `sm:flex-row` at/above it, so identity and controls stack cleanly on narrow viewports. `tsc --noEmit` + `pnpm --filter @next-lane/web build` clean; regression re-verified green desktop+mobile: `settings-robustness.spec.ts` (18/18), `components.spec.ts` (10/10), `viewer-aware-ui.spec.ts` (6/6), `board.spec.ts` (8/8) — 42/42, zero regressions. Screenshots: `/tmp/nav-shots/members-desktop-before.png`/`-after.png`, `/tmp/nav-shots/members-mobile-before.png`/`-after.png`. **MCP:** already covered by the Phase 1 backend batch (`list_project_role_overrides`/`set_project_role_override`/`remove_project_role_override`) — no new tools needed for this UI-only slice. **Closes the "Admin controls" Better-than-Jira gap** (alongside the already-shipped in-app SSO/OIDC config screen) — see `docs/VISION.md` § Better-than-Jira scorecard for the next vision-steward pass to re-score. [product-auditor Pass 9/10/11/12 carryover; VISION.md § Better-than-Jira gaps #2]
- [x] (P1, M) **GitLab integration v1 — two-way link (Gitea follow-up noted)** ✅ shipped 2026-07-03 — same two-way linking shape as the shipped GitHub v1, GitLab semantics, implemented as a **parallel** `GitlabIntegration`/`IssueGitlabLink` table pair (deliberately NOT a provider-tagged generalization of the GitHub tables — additive/zero-risk to the already-shipped GitHub rows; a future "unify behind `ScmIntegration`" migration is an option once Gitea makes the duplication cost outweigh the migration risk, not attempted this pass). **Schema (additive):** `GitlabIntegration { projectId (unique), gitlabBaseUrl (default "https://gitlab.com" — self-hosted GitLab is first-class, unlike GitHub's hardcoded host), projectPath ("namespace/project", nested subgroups allowed), tokenEncrypted (AES-256-GCM via the shared `common/crypto/secret-crypto.util.ts` primitive, `GITLAB_TOKEN_ENCRYPTION_KEY`→`JWT_SECRET` fallback), webhookSecret }` + `IssueGitlabLink { kind MR/COMMIT/BRANCH, externalId, title, url, state, authorLogin }` (unique `[issueId, kind, externalId]` for idempotent re-delivery), migration `20260703060000_add_gitlab_integration` (verified against `schema.prisma` via `prisma migrate diff` — zero drift on the new tables; `prisma migrate status` reports "up to date"). **`GitlabModule`** (`apps/api/src/gitlab/`): ADMIN-gated `PUT/GET/DELETE /projects/:projectId/gitlab` (GET is role-shaped via `getEffectiveProjectRole` — full config incl. `webhookSecret` for the caller's EFFECTIVE project ADMIN, workspace ADMIN or per-project-override ADMIN alike, `{projectPath, gitlabBaseUrl, hasToken}` summary otherwise); public `POST /gitlab/webhook/:projectId` verifies the literal `X-Gitlab-Token` header via `crypto.timingSafeEqual` (GitLab does NOT HMAC-sign its payloads, unlike GitHub — this is a plain constant-time equality check, length-guarded before compare) before processing `Push Hook` (per-commit + pushed-branch-name key scan → COMMIT/BRANCH links) and `Merge Request Hook` (title + description + source-branch key scan → MR links, state normalized `opened→open`/`merged`/`closed`/`locked`) events. Issue-key extraction was extracted from `github/github-issue-key.util.ts` into a shared `common/issue-key.util.ts` (GitHub's own file now re-exports it, byte-for-byte behavior-preserving) so both providers share one scoping implementation. `GitlabClient` — the single seam for any future outbound GitLab REST call (self-hosted-base-URL-aware, unlike `GithubClient`'s hardcoded host) — exists, unit-tested/mocked, unused by v1 (webhook payloads carry everything v1 needs), zero gitlab.com egress. `GET /issues/:issueId/gitlab-links`. Two new PAT scopes (`gitlab:read`/`gitlab:write`) added to `PAT_SCOPES` in `packages/shared`; every route `@RequireScope`-gated (no ungated routes, a past review finding). **Frontend:** Settings "GitLab" section (`GitlabSection.tsx`, mirrors `GithubSection.tsx` — ADMIN form with project path + optional self-hosted instance URL + PAT, generated webhook URL/"Secret Token" with copy buttons; MEMBER/VIEWER read-only "Connected to namespace/project" summary, hidden when unconfigured) and a `GitlabLinksSection.tsx` ("GitLab" sub-heading, tanuki-glyph icons, MR/commit/branch rows + state badges) rendered beside `GithubLinksSection` in the issue drawer's Development area — both providers' links visible side-by-side when both are configured. Realtime: webhook upserts reuse the existing `issue.updated` socket event; `apps/web/src/api/socket.ts` also invalidates the new `qk.gitlabLinks(id)` cache key. **Tests:** 5 new spec files mirroring the GitHub coverage depth (`gitlab-token-verify.util.spec.ts` — valid/tampered/missing/empty/case-sensitive/length-mismatch, never throws; `gitlab-crypto.util.spec.ts` — AES round-trip, distinct-ciphertext-per-call, wrong-key failure, independent of the GitHub key's env var; `gitlab-client.service.spec.ts` — mocked `fetch`, self-hosted URL + nested-subgroup path encoding, never throws; `gitlab.service.spec.ts` — 667 lines, ADMIN/effective-ADMIN-override gating, tenant isolation, real Push-Hook/Merge-Request-Hook payload shapes, idempotent re-delivery, wrong-project-key ignored) — **1665 total API tests green**; real-DB `tenant-isolation.integration.spec.ts` still green, **102/102 endpoints blocked, 0 issues** (4 new GitLab rows: GET/PUT/DELETE integration + GET issue links); `apps/web/e2e/gitlab-integration.spec.ts` (5 scenarios × desktop+mobile, 10/10 green — settings save + webhook URL/secret display; a **locally token-verified fake GitLab webhook POST** links the issue and the MR row appears in the drawer; invalid/missing token → 401 + no link created; MEMBER sees the read-only summary, no form); `github-integration.spec.ts`/`issue-detail.spec.ts`/`issue-drawer-overlay.spec.ts`/`settings-robustness.spec.ts` regression re-verified green (40/40, zero regressions); `tsc --noEmit` + both apps' builds clean. **Live round-trip verification** (real running API, not just unit/e2e): valid MR webhook → `200 {linksUpserted:1}`; tampered token → `401`; identical replay → `200 {linksUpserted:1}` with the SAME link row id (idempotent, no duplicate); missing-header Push Hook → `401`; a PAT scoped to `gitlab:read` → `200` on `GET /issues/:id/gitlab-links`, a PAT without that scope → `403 "This token does not have the required scope: gitlab:read"`. **MCP:** `list_issue_gitlab_links` (read-only, `gitlab:read`-scoped, following the compact/paged envelope) — live-verified end-to-end against the running API via a scripted tool invocation, including the 403 scope-denial path returning as an MCP tool error; **92 tools total** (was 91); `apps/mcp/README.md` counts/tables updated (also fixed a pre-existing duplicated `update_project_context` table row found by MCP-QA pass 1, finding 9); configuring the integration itself deliberately NOT exposed (admin-only, secret-bearing, mirrors the GitHub decision) — noted in the "Not exposed over MCP" table. **Gitea follow-up (explicitly deferred, not this pass):** Gitea uses HMAC-SHA256 like GitHub (not GitLab's shared-secret-token scheme) and its own webhook payload shapes (`X-Gitea-Signature`, `push`/`pull_request` event names) — filed as a new Next item below rather than attempted speculatively against an unverified payload shape. [ROADMAP Phase 9; VISION.md § Better-than-Jira gaps #3]
