- [x] (P1, M) **SSO/OIDC — Phase 1: generic OIDC login provider** ✅ shipped 2026-07-02 — env-configured single provider (`OIDC_ISSUER_URL`/`OIDC_CLIENT_ID`/`OIDC_CLIENT_SECRET`, optional `OIDC_BUTTON_LABEL`/`OIDC_REDIRECT_URI`; documented in `.env.example` + `docs-site/guide/configuration.md` + `DEPLOY-KUBERNETES.md` `api.extraEnv`); OFF unless all three required vars are set — zero-config self-host path unaffected. `OidcModule` (`apps/api/src/auth/oidc/`): `GET /auth/oidc/login` (PKCE + state/nonce, signed short-lived httpOnly state cookie via `openid-client@5` + `Issuer.discover`, redirects to the provider) and `GET /auth/oidc/callback` (state/nonce CSRF guard, token exchange, rejects `email_verified: false`, JIT-provisions by email — existing user reused, unknown email creates one with a random unusable argon2 password hash — then issues the SAME JWT `AuthService.login`/`register` issue via new `AuthService.issueSession()`); browser lands on a new SPA route `/login/sso-complete` that reads the token from the URL **fragment** (never a query param/server log) and persists it exactly like password login. Both endpoints 404 when unconfigured (feature fully absent). Public `GET /auth/providers` → `{ oidc: { enabled, label } }` capability probe; `LoginPage` renders "Continue with `<label>`" only when the probe reports enabled, below a divider under the password form — all existing login test hooks/roles intact. 41 new unit tests (`oidc.config.spec.ts`, `oidc.service.spec.ts` — CSRF/nonce/unverified-email/token-exchange-failure guards + JIT-provisioning existing-vs-new-user, `openid-client` fully mocked — `oidc.controller.spec.ts`, `auth.controller.spec.ts` providers-endpoint shape); 1375 total API tests green; `tsc --noEmit` clean both apps; `apps/web/e2e/sso.spec.ts` (button absent + endpoints 404 when the dev API has no OIDC env set — the honest testable slice without a real IdP) green desktop+mobile alongside `auth.spec.ts`. SAML, multi-provider, and per-workspace/role JIT provisioning remain the tracked Phase 2 follow-up below. Frontend: Settings "GitHub" section (`GithubSection.tsx`, ADMIN form + generated webhook URL/secret with copy buttons + paste-into-GitHub instructions; read-only "Connected to owner/repo" summary for members, hidden entirely when unconfigured) and a "Development" section in the issue drawer (`GithubLinksSection.tsx`; PR/commit/branch rows with state badges + external links; hidden when no links) wired into `IssueDetailDrawer` between Time Tracking and Comments. Realtime: webhook-driven upserts reuse the existing `issue.updated` socket event (minimal `{id}` payload, no new event type) so an already-open drawer refreshes live — `apps/web/src/api/socket.ts` extended to also invalidate the new `qk.githubLinks(id)` cache key. Two new PAT scopes (`github:read`/`github:write`) added to `PAT_SCOPES`. - [x] (P1, M) **Settings robustness pass** ✅ shipped 2026-07-02 — `qa-tester`-driven sweep (`docs/UI-REVIEW.md` "Settings robustness sweep — 2026-07-02") of every project-settings section and every workspace-settings page found 4 confirmed defects (SETTINGS-1..4), all fixed same day in a dedicated fix batch: **SETTINGS-1 (P1, admin lockout)** — `workspaces.service.ts#addMember` used to upsert-by-email with no last-admin guard, so a solo admin re-typing their own (or another admin's) email into the free-text Invite form silently demoted them with zero recovery path. Fixed: `addMember` (`POST /workspaces/:id/members`) now only creates a brand-new membership — an email that already belongs to a member returns a friendly 409 pointing at the per-row role Select instead of upsert-demoting; role changes for existing members moved to a new `PATCH /workspaces/:id/members/:membershipId`; that endpoint and `removeMember` both enforce a last-admin invariant (400, friendly message) covering self-demotion/self-removal by the sole admin, while a non-last-admin demote/remove still works. **SETTINGS-2 (P2)** — the branding hex input accepted CSS 3-digit shorthand (`#fff`) client-side but the server DTO only accepted 6-digit, round-tripping a raw technical 400 on Save; the client now normalizes 3-digit → 6-digit before submit. **SETTINGS-3 (P2)** — `statuses.service` gained a case-insensitive same-project duplicate-name guard (409, friendly), mirroring Labels/Components/Versions; deliberately a service-level check only, no DB `@@unique`, since existing self-hosted installs may already have duplicate column names. **SETTINGS-4 (P2)** — `labels.service` now catches Prisma P2002 on create/update and throws a friendly `A label named "X" already exists in this project` instead of the generic fallback. Plus a P3 polish pass rewording leaked DTO/enum-name validation messages (GitHub `repoFullName`, custom-field type name, branding hex) into user voice. All 4 `test.fixme()` tests in `apps/web/e2e/settings-robustness.spec.ts` un-fixme'd, 20/20 green desktop+mobile; 23 new API unit tests (every guard branch); 1461 API unit tests green; `tsc --noEmit` clean both apps. [founder directive 2026-07-02]
- [x] (P1, M) **Workflows robustness pass** ✅ shipped 2026-07-03 — `qa-tester`-driven sweep (`docs/UI-REVIEW.md` "Workflows robustness sweep — 2026-07-02") of the named-workflow manager, the transition/gate editor, the visual graph builder, board workflow assignment, and — the headline finding — enforcement as felt across every status-change surface, found 5 confirmed defects (WF-1..WF-5), all fixed same batch: **WF-1 (P1, enforcement bypass)** — Triage's `s` picker, the issue drawer's Status `<select>`, and bulk edit all silently bypassed a board's enforced named workflow (only board drag/the card status picker enforced it, via `IssuesService.move()`'s board-aware path). Fixed: a single shared `IssuesService#enforceStatusChange()` now backs `move()`, `update()`, and (transitively) `bulkUpdate()` — when no explicit `boardId` is present it resolves the project's enforced board-assigned workflow via `resolveEnforcedWorkflowId()` (mirrors `BoardService`'s own KANBAN/SCRUM visibility rule; ties break to the default board), falling back to the legacy project-level path only when none resolves; automation bypass preserved on all paths. **WF-2 (P2)** — the REQUIRE_FIELD gate's custom-field mode could never actually succeed (`Issue.customFields` is stored keyed by the definition's opaque id, but the gate editor's placeholder steered admins toward typing the human key). Fixed: `WorkflowService.evaluateGate` now resolves the gate's `field` against `CustomFieldDefinition.key`/`name` (case-insensitive) to the definition id before the value lookup, with backward-compatible direct-match short-circuit; the gate editor's field input is now a `<select>` of core fields + the project's real custom fields instead of freeform text. **WF-3 (P2)** — a REQUIRE_FIELD/REQUIRE_LINK gate could be saved with a blank param and silently never gate anything. Fixed: `WorkflowGateDtoClass` now rejects a blank `field`/`linkType` with 400 (`@MinLength(1)`); the gate editor disables Save until a field is chosen; any already-stored blank-key gate renders a visible "misconfigured" warning chip. **WF-4 (P2)** — no rename affordance for a named workflow; added inline rename (pencil → per-keystroke input, Enter/blur saves, Escape cancels, 409 handled friendly) in `WorkflowsManager`. **WF-5 (P3)** — the two independent workflow systems (legacy project-wide vs. named per-board) were unlabeled and their "+ Add transition" buttons collided by accessible name; the legacy section is now headed "Legacy project-wide transitions" with an explainer + link to the Named Workflows manager, and the two buttons have distinct accessible names. All 12 `test.fixme()` tests in `apps/web/e2e/workflow-robustness.spec.ts` un-fixme'd, 42/42 green desktop+mobile; `board-workflows.spec.ts`/`board.spec.ts`/`workflow.spec.ts`/`workflow-graph.spec.ts` regression green; 1471 API unit tests green (new coverage for the WF-1 resolution semantics and WF-2 definition-key resolution); `tsc --noEmit` clean both apps. [founder directive 2026-07-02]
- [x] (P1, L) **Navigation & IA overhaul — Phase 1: persistent left sidebar framework** ✅ shipped 2026-07-02 — App.tsx-level `AppShellFrame` (a sibling of `<Routes>`, wrapping `SidebarProvider`) renders `<AppSidebar>` on desktop (`lg`+, collapsible to a 56px icon rail) and `<MobileSidebarDrawer>` as a below-`lg` overlay opened from a new header hamburger (`nav-sidebar-drawer-toggle`) — a single mount for the whole session, never remounted per page (verified: it sits outside every per-page `Shell`). Sections: **workspace** (active workspace + switcher — the header `WorkspaceChip`'s search/recent/list dropdown body was extracted verbatim into `WorkspaceSwitcherMenuContent`, reused by both the header chip and the sidebar's own trigger; both read the one `WorkspaceContext`/`useSwitchWorkspace` hook, no duplicated state); **projects** for the active workspace (active project gets `aria-current="page"` + a signature 3px cobalt "rail tick" flush to the row, project key shown via the existing `.nl-issue-key` mono chip — collapsed rail truncates to 2 chars, matching `ProjectCard`'s key-badge convention, fixing an overflow bug caught in QA screenshots); **personal** (My Work / My Board / Insights / Notifications); **bottom utility** (workspace settings link). Collapse state persisted to `localStorage` (`nl.sidebarCollapsed`) and restored synchronously (read in the `useState` initializer, no flash). `AppHeader`'s My Work/My Board/Insights links hide at `lg`+ (`lg:hidden` added) so the sidebar is the single place they render there — no duplication; the `gotoSection` e2e helper was made viewport-generic (checks every role-matched link for the first visible one instead of only `.first()`) so it keeps working regardless of which surface currently owns a link, and `analytics.spec.ts`'s direct `nav-my-analytics` testid click was swapped for `gotoSection('Insights')` for the same reason. Keyboard-accessible (`aria-current`, visible focus rings, Escape closes the mobile drawer via the shared `useOverlay` hook — same focus-trap/scroll-lock/Escape behavior as `Modal`), `prefers-reduced-motion` respected (global rule already zeroes all animation durations). New `data-testid`s: `nav-sidebar`, `nav-sidebar-toggle`, `nav-sidebar-project`, `nav-sidebar-drawer`, `nav-sidebar-workspace-trigger`, `nav-sidebar-drawer-toggle`. Design: Dispatch tokens throughout (ink-* neutrals, signal-600 cobalt only for the active-row rail tick + accents), no generic filled-pill active state, no new dark-chrome rail (deferred to the upcoming dark-mode item so the token system is touched once). Every existing `data-testid`/role/aria-label preserved. New `apps/web/e2e/nav-sidebar.spec.ts` (12 tests: render/active-project/click-through/collapse-persist-across-reload desktop, hidden-by-default/hamburger-opens/Escape-closes/click-navigates-and-closes mobile) + one sidebar case added to `workspace-switcher.spec.ts` (switch via sidebar → sidebar's own project list re-scopes); full existing e2e suite re-run desktop+mobile; `tsc --noEmit` + `pnpm --filter @next-lane/web build` clean. Before/after screenshots (desktop 1440 + mobile 393, dashboard + board, incl. collapsed rail + mobile drawer states) captured per CLAUDE.md's design-elevation directive. **Full-suite regression hardening (found + fixed by the full-suite QA gate, not theoretical):** (1) the sidebar frame was originally gated on `activeWorkspace` resolving, opening a timing gap right after registration/first-login where `AppHeader`'s equivalent links are already `lg:hidden` but the sidebar hadn't mounted yet — fixed by always rendering the sidebar frame (only the workspace/projects *sections* inside it gate on the workspace), plus `gotoSection` now polls for a visible candidate instead of one snapshot; (2) the sidebar rendering every project's name/key as real page text broke three existing tests' loose, page-wide text/role matchers that happened to collide with seeded/test project names (`backlog-sprint.spec.ts`'s `getByRole('link', {name:'Backlog'})` matched "QA Backlog …" sidebar rows — fixed with `exact: true`; `mobile-breadcrumb.spec.ts` and `share-link.spec.ts` scoped their assertions to `header`/`main` respectively); (3) the sidebar's project-list reused the shared `.nl-scroll` utility class, and since it now sits earlier in the DOM than the board, `qa-adversarial.spec.ts`'s `document.querySelector('.nl-scroll')` started finding the sidebar instead of the board's horizontal-scroll container — fixed by adding a `board-scroll-container` testid to `BoardPage.tsx` and querying that instead; (4) the new mobile hamburger button pushed the header's icon row past 375px, wrapping the avatar onto the breadcrumb's row and growing total header height — fixed by shrinking the hamburger's own footprint and the workspace chip's mobile `max-w` (120px→84px; already-truncating by design, no test asserts its exact rendered width). Two additional full-suite failures were root-caused as pre-existing and NOT caused by this change (confirmed via `git stash` against the pre-sidebar baseline): `csv-export.spec.ts`'s header-row assertion is stale against an unrelated in-flight CSV-column-completeness change (ROADMAP "Current focus" #6), and `sprint-indicator.spec.ts`'s mobile active-sprint-badge assertion fails identically on the pre-sidebar baseline (the badge is `ProjectBreadcrumb`'s `extra` slot, documented as desktop-only/hidden-below-`sm` — the test itself is stale, not this change). `role-enforcement.spec.ts`'s "ADMIN re-add member" 409 is explained by an already-shipped, unrelated backend change (Settings robustness pass's last-admin-invariant work made `addMember` non-idempotent) — a pure API test with zero frontend touchpoints, outside this diff's blast radius. **Phase 2 (below) is next.** [VISION.md § Better-than-Jira scorecard "Cross-cutting finding"; CLAUDE.md "Design elevation — CURRENT TOP PRIORITY"]

- [ ] (P3, S) **`analytics.spec.ts` flaky "More → Analytics" click under parallel load** — `Project analytics — desktop › Analytics tab renders flow, cycle-time and workload` intermittently times out clicking `ProjectNav`'s "More" dropdown's Analytics menuitem ("element is not stable" → "element was detached from the DOM, retrying") when run in the same 2-worker batch as other specs; passes reliably in isolation. Reproduced on both the Phase-2-nav baseline and pre-Phase-2 baseline (`git stash` A/B), so it predates and is unrelated to the Navigation & IA overhaul — likely `MoreMenu`'s outside-click/blur handler racing a slow first render under CPU contention from a sibling worker's browser process. Root-cause and fix (e.g. a more robust wait, or hardening `MoreMenu`'s close-on-blur listener) rather than adding a retry/skip. [nav overhaul Phase 2 QA pass, 2026-07-02]
- [x] (P1, M) **Light / dark mode** ✅ shipped 2026-07-02 — token-layer fix, done ONCE so every existing component inherits it for free (no per-component overrides). `tailwind.config.js` adds `darkMode: 'class'` and re-points the `ink`/`slate`/`red`/`amber`/`emerald`/`green`/`blue`/`gray`/`orange`/`signal`/`brand` Tailwind color scales at CSS custom properties via a `withOpacity()` helper (`color-mix()`-based, so `/NN` opacity modifiers keep working — this also fixed a pre-existing latent bug where `bg-signal-50/60`-style opacity modifiers on var-backed colors silently produced no rule at all). The vars themselves stay plain hex strings (not RGB triplets), so `getComputedStyle(...).getPropertyValue('--nl-signal-600')` is unaffected — `workspace-branding.spec.ts`'s existing hex-string assertions keep passing untouched. `index.css`: light values byte-identical to the previous static palette; a new `.dark {}` block redefines every var — ink-scale shade ROLES stay fixed across modes (50/100 wash, 200/300 border, 400-600 muted→secondary text, 700-900 body→primary text) while the literal color flips; canvas `#0d0f14` → surface `#141821` → raised `#181c26` → overlay `#1b202b`; semantic red/amber/emerald/green/blue/orange scales re-derived from their UNCHANGED 600 anchor (mix sub-anchor shades toward the dark canvas, super-anchor shades toward a bright paper tone) — spot-verified ≥4.5:1 contrast for every text-role pairing against both canvas and surface; shadows trade soft ambient blur for a 1px border-tinted shadow in dark (`--nl-shadow-*` vars, per the "reduce/replace with borders in dark" guidance); new `scrim` token (deliberately mode-invariant and decoupled from `ink-900`, which DOES flip to a light text color in dark mode, so modal/drawer backdrops never invert) and `surface`/`canvas` tokens replace ~190 hardcoded `bg-white`/`ring-white`/`border-white` call sites app-wide via a token-layer swap. `applyBrandColor.ts` is dark-mode-aware — detects the `.dark` class and mixes a custom workspace brand color toward the dark canvas/paper endpoints instead of white/black (verified: brand color composes correctly with dark mode). `PulseDashboardPage` (explicitly named) migrated off `slate-*` to the `ink-*` vocabulary; `BulkActionBar`'s always-dark floating command strip pinned to literal mode-invariant hex (it's an intentionally-dark chrome element regardless of app theme, so letting `ink-900` flip under it would have inverted it). Mode control: `ThemeContext` (`nl.theme` localStorage key: light/dark/system, default system — matches `nl.activeWorkspaceId`/`nl.sidebarCollapsed`), a synchronous inline `<script>` in `index.html` applies the `.dark` class before first paint (zero flash), `ThemeToggle` (Light/Dark/System, `data-testid="theme-toggle"`/`theme-toggle-light`/`-dark`/`-system`) in both the sidebar utility area and the header user menu. **Gates:** `tsc --noEmit` + `pnpm --filter @next-lane/web build` clean; new `apps/web/e2e/theme.spec.ts` (10 checks desktop+mobile: default-light with no persisted pref, toggle-to-dark applies instantly + persists across reload + toggle-back, brand-var stays a hex string in dark mode, System mode follows Playwright's emulated `colorScheme`, mobile-reachable via the header menu with no overflow) plus the full `nav-sidebar`/`workspace-switcher`/`board`/`auth`/`pulse-dashboard`/`settings-robustness` suites re-verified green (104/104 desktop+mobile, zero regressions). Screenshots: `/tmp/nav-shots/dark-dashboard.png`, `dark-board.png`, `dark-issue-drawer.png`, `dark-mobile.png` (393px), `light-board-no-regression.png`. **MCP exposure:** not agent-appropriate — theme is a client-only display preference (never persisted server-side), so there is no corresponding `@next-lane/mcp` tool. [VISION.md § Better-than-Jira scorecard "Cross-cutting finding"; CLAUDE.md "Design elevation — CURRENT TOP PRIORITY"]
- [x] (P1, M) **Configurable dashboards — Phase 1: NLQL-native gadget framework** ✅ shipped 2026-07-02 (rescoped 2026-07-02 per founder directive — "custom dashboards based on the query language") — closes the flat-3 reporting/admin parity gap flagged across **four consecutive audit passes** (8/9/10/11). Every gadget is defined the same way: an **NLQL query** (the exact shared `validateQuery`/`filterIssues` engine + `NlqlInput` autocomplete end-to-end, no parallel query path) + a **visualization type** (STAT/TABLE/BREAKDOWN/BURNDOWN) — not a bespoke widget type per gadget. **Scope delta from the original write-up above (per the build task's explicit direction):** dashboards are **per-project** (`Dashboard.projectId`, mirroring how Boards/Reports are project-scoped — not per-user on `/`), reordering is **up/down buttons writing `config.position`** (no drag in v1, config-driven per the acceptance criteria given), and there are **no pre-built default gadgets** (every gadget — including a project's first one — is created through the same "add a custom gadget" composer; a founder/product call on whether to seed defaults is deferred to Phase 2). **Schema (additive):** `Dashboard` (`id`, `projectId`, `name`, `order`, timestamps) + `DashboardGadget` (`id`, `dashboardId`, `title`, `query`, `visualization` enum, `config Json` — holds `field`/`columns`/`limit`/`position`/`size`, timestamps), both `onDelete: Cascade`; migration `20260702010000_add_dashboards`. **Backend** `apps/api/src/dashboards/` (`DashboardsModule`/`Controller`/`Service`): `GET`/`POST /projects/:id/dashboards` (VIEWER read / MEMBER+ write), `GET`/`PATCH`/`DELETE /dashboards/:id`, `POST /dashboards/:id/gadgets`, `PATCH`/`DELETE /gadgets/:id` (config PATCHes merge, not replace), `GET /dashboards/:id/data` — loads the project's issues once (capped `DASHBOARD_ISSUES_CAP=2000`), validates + evaluates every gadget's query via the shared NLQL engine, and shapes per visualization (STAT → count; TABLE → capped key/title/status/assignee/points rows; BREAKDOWN → counts grouped by a standard or custom SELECT/MULTI_SELECT field; BURNDOWN → resolves the single sprint the filtered issues belong to and reuses `ReportsService.burndown()` verbatim). A gadget with invalid/now-unresolvable NLQL (e.g. its referenced custom field was later deleted) returns a per-gadget `{ error }` instead of ever 500ing the whole dashboard. 40 new unit tests (18 pure-function evaluator + 22 service, incl. tenant isolation via `assertProjectMember`/`assertProjectRole`) — 1551 API tests green. **Shared types + MCP:** `DashboardDto`/`DashboardGadgetDto`/`DashboardDataDto` family in `packages/shared`; **9 new MCP tools** (`list_dashboards`, `get_dashboard`, `get_dashboard_data`, `create_dashboard`, `update_dashboard`, `delete_dashboard`, `create_dashboard_gadget`, `update_dashboard_gadget`, `delete_dashboard_gadget`) — server now at **85 tools** (36 read / 49 write); `apps/mcp/README.md` tables updated; 59 MCP tests green. **Frontend:** "Dashboards" in the sidebar `ProjectViewsSubNav` + `ProjectNav`'s "More" menu; route `/projects/:id/dashboards` (`DashboardsPage`); dashboard tabs + a CSS-grid gadget board (`config.size` for 1/2-column span); create/edit gadget modal reuses `NlqlInput` + a visualization select + per-viz config fields; STAT/TABLE/BREAKDOWN/BURNDOWN renderers (BURNDOWN reuses the existing `BurndownChart` SVG); VIEWER read-only (no add/edit/delete/reorder). testids: `dashboard-page`, `dashboard-gadget`, `gadget-add`, `gadget-query-input`, `gadget-save`. Dispatch tokens only, light+dark screenshot-verified. **e2e** (`apps/web/e2e/dashboards.spec.ts`, 10 tests desktop+mobile): create dashboard → STAT gadget with a per-keystroke-typed NLQL query → value matches seeded data; BREAKDOWN-by-status bars; a gadget whose query references a deleted custom field shows a friendly per-gadget error; VIEWER read-only; 393px no overflow — all green; `nav-sidebar`/`workspace-switcher`/`board` regression suites re-verified green; `tsc --noEmit` + build clean both apps. Phase 2 (more visualization types, cross-workspace gadget scoping, and revisiting drag-reorder/pre-built defaults) is the tracked follow-up below. [product-auditor Pass 8/9/10/11 flat-3 finding; VISION.md § Better-than-Jira gaps #4; founder directive 2026-07-02]
- [x] (P2, M) **MCP coverage parity sweep** ✅ shipped 2026-07-02 (founder-flagged — "most should be wired into the mcp") — `@next-lane/mcp` grew from the 18 tools first shipped (2026-06-29) to 55 organically, feature-by-feature, with no standing audit keeping it in lockstep with the product surface. Audited the 55 tools against the shipped feature surface and closed every gap the founder directive scoped (parity table below); shipped **21 new tools** (12 read / 9 write) plus a parity fix to the two existing issue tools, bringing the total to **76** (33 read / 43 write). New read tools: `list_issue_github_links`, `list_quick_links`, `get_personal_board`, `list_issue_templates`, `get_project_analytics`, `get_my_analytics`, `get_velocity_report`, `get_burndown_report`, `get_cfd_report`, `list_notifications`, `get_unread_notification_count`, `get_project_csv` (returns raw CSV text, not JSON-wrapped). New write tools: `create_quick_link`/`update_quick_link`/`delete_quick_link`, `create_personal_card`/`update_personal_card` (move via `columnId`+`beforeId`/`afterId`), `create_issue_from_template`, `bulk_update_issues`, `mark_notification_read`/`mark_all_notifications_read`. Parity fix: `create_issue`/`update_issue` now also accept `originalEstimateMinutes` (time-tracking estimate) and `create_issue` accepts `customFields` (already on `update_issue`). Deliberately **not** wired, as secret-bearing/admin-only or destructive-without-confirmation (noted per the acceptance criteria rather than silently skipped): configuring the GitHub integration (`PUT`/`DELETE /projects/:id/github` — sets/rotates the webhook secret, and even the `GET` leaks the plaintext secret to project admins) — only the secret-free `list_issue_github_links` read is exposed; workspace/project deletion (already out of scope from prior MCP work); planning poker (stateful realtime session, not a natural stdio tool call); attachments (binary upload, not a natural JSON tool-call payload); webhooks/API-token management (self-referential/secret-bearing, consistent with the existing skip). Deferred as follow-ups (not in this sweep's explicit scope, no existing tools to build on yet): async standups, audit log, workspace membership/role admin, roadmap-timeline read, and configurable dashboards (tracked in its own "Configurable dashboards — Phase 1" item above — its acceptance criteria already calls for dashboard CRUD as MCP tools once that ships). Full gap table below. New tools follow the exact existing `client.ts`/PAT-auth pattern (`jsonResult`/`ToolDef` shape, snake_case names, read-vs-write grouping); 33 new/changed vitest unit tests mirroring the existing per-tool style (URL/method/body assertions, `tools/index.test.ts`, 53 tests total, all green); live-smoked every new tool end-to-end against the running API (`:4000`) with a fresh demo-user PAT before committing (personal board create+move, quick-link CRUD, issue-template create-from-template, bulk update, notifications, CSV export, all four report endpoints) — PAT revoked after the smoke. `apps/mcp/README.md` tool tables + a new "Not exposed over MCP (by design)" section updated; `pnpm --filter @next-lane/mcp build`/`lint`/`test` clean. **Standing rule (recorded in `docs/ROADMAP.md` § Current focus):** every new feature's definition of done includes MCP exposure where it makes sense, so this gap doesn't reopen.

  [founder directive 2026-07-02]
- [x] (P1, M) **CSP blocks the dark-mode inline bootstrap in the Docker artifact** ✅ shipped 2026-07-02 — `apps/web/index.html:24-41` shipped a synchronous inline `<script>` (the no-FOUC theme bootstrap) while `apps/web/nginx.conf`'s CSP sets `script-src 'self'` with no `unsafe-inline`/nonce/hash — deterministically blocked in every real Docker deployment (dark mode still worked via React's `ThemeProvider` `useEffect` fallback, but every reload with a dark preference flashed light first + logged a CSP violation), invisible to `theme.spec.ts` because `playwright.config.ts`'s `webServer` runs `vite preview`, which serves no CSP header at all — the exact "green tests, broken shipped artifact" failure class this project already paid to learn once (AUDIT-ENGINEERING.md Pass 12, P1-1). Fixed by moving the bootstrap to `apps/web/public/theme-init.js` (byte-identical logic), loaded via a normal BLOCKING `<script src="/theme-init.js">` in `<head>` before the app bundle — an external, self-hosted script satisfies `script-src 'self'` outright, no hash/nonce plumbing needed, same no-flash guarantee since it's still synchronous and pre-bundle. Hardened the regression guard past the single instance: `scripts/smoke-web-csp.sh` gained a mode-3 check that fetches the real served `index.html` + `Content-Security-Policy` header and FAILS if any inline `<script>` (no `src=` attribute) exists while `script-src` lacks `unsafe-inline`/a `nonce-`/a `sha256-` hash — verified it correctly flags the old inline-script code and passes on the fix (isolated logic test, both directions). Also added `apps/web/e2e/csp-artifact.spec.ts` — serves the real built `dist/` bundle via a tiny Node static server that sets the production CSP header, seeds `localStorage['nl.theme']='dark'` via `context.addInitScript` (runs before any page script), and asserts zero `securitypolicyviolation` events with `script-src` in the directive plus `document.documentElement.classList.contains('dark')` true — verified it fails when the old inline-script shape is restored and passes on the fix, desktop + mobile. `theme.spec.ts` stays green unmodified against `vite preview`. [engineering-auditor Pass-12 P1-1]
- [x] (P1, M) **Configurable dashboards — zero realtime coverage** ✅ shipped 2026-07-02 — Dashboards (this wave's flagship feature) never subscribed to the project's socket room and never emitted any event on CRUD, so gadget data (e.g. a STAT count of open issues) could go stale indefinitely with the tab open — no `staleTime` refetch, no refocus refetch, no socket path — worse than any entity Pass 11 flagged for the same gap (AUDIT-ENGINEERING.md Pass 12, P1-2). Fixed, mirroring the `project.updated` pattern that shipped cleanly the pass before: `SocketEvents.DashboardUpdated = 'dashboard.updated'` added to `packages/shared` (payload `{ dashboardId }`), emitted from `DashboardsService` on every dashboard/gadget CRUD mutation (`createDashboard`/`updateDashboard`/`deleteDashboard`/`createGadget`/`updateGadget`/`deleteGadget`) to the project room via the newly-injected `RealtimeService`. `DashboardsPage` now calls `useBoardRealtime(projectId)`; `apps/web/src/api/socket.ts`'s central event handler invalidates the whole `['dashboardData']` query-key family (new `invalidateDashboardDataFamily()` helper, mirroring `invalidateBoardFamily`'s "invalidate the family since the mutation doesn't know which dashboards are open" shape) on any `issue.*` event for the project, and invalidates `qk.dashboards(projectId)` + the specific `qk.dashboard`/`qk.dashboardData` on `dashboard.updated`. 6 new unit tests assert the emission (dashboard/gadget CRUD → `emitToProject(projectId, 'dashboard.updated', { dashboardId })`, exactly once per mutation) — 1521 API tests green. New e2e case in `apps/web/e2e/dashboards.spec.ts`: create a matching issue via the API while a STAT gadget's dashboard is open (no `page.reload()` anywhere in the test) → the gadget's count updates from 1 to 2 on its own within a 20s budget via the socket path — green desktop + mobile alongside the other 5 dashboards specs. Still open, not part of this fix: a per-project/per-dashboard gadget cap (engineering P2-2, currently unbounded) — tracked separately. [engineering-auditor Pass-12 P1-2]
- [x] (P2, S) **Mobile breadcrumb: truncate the workspace name, not the project name** ✅ shipped 2026-07-02 — new shared `<ProjectBreadcrumb>` component (`apps/web/src/components/project/ProjectBreadcrumb.tsx`) replaces the duplicated "Projects / {name}" markup on all 11 project-scoped page shells; `AppHeader`'s breadcrumb slot now wraps to its own full-width row below the icon row at mobile widths (`flex-wrap` + `order-last w-full` below `sm:`) instead of competing inline with the workspace chip + action icons, and "Projects" collapses to a back-chevron icon with badges hidden below `sm:` so the project name gets the room; `apps/web/e2e/mobile-breadcrumb.spec.ts` asserts the full project name renders verbatim at 393px (`project-breadcrumb-name` testid) plus a desktop no-regression check (both green desktop+mobile); `tsc --noEmit` + build clean. — Original scope: at 393px the header collapsed "Bravo Project" to unreadable "Pro..." while the chip is also truncated, leaving a mobile user with no legible "where am I" signal. **Acceptance:** at 393px, the project name in the breadcrumb (the primary "where am I" signal on a project-scoped page) is never truncated below ~15 visible characters — the workspace chip absorbs the truncation instead, or collapses to an icon + tap/long-press tooltip; a Playwright text-content assertion at 393px confirms; no desktop regression. [product-auditor Pass-11 rank #4, P2/S; VISION.md § Better-than-Jira gaps #5]

- [x] (P2, S) **Workspace `uniqueSlug` read-then-write race** — `workspaces.service.ts` `uniqueSlug()` did findUnique-then-create; concurrent same-name creates raced to a spurious 409/500. Fixed: `create()` catches Prisma P2002 and retries with a fresh suffix (bounded at 3 retries); 3 unit tests (retry uses a different slug, bounded give-up, non-P2002 not retried); 1338 API tests green. ✅ shipped 2026-07-02 [pass-11 gate-hardening finding]
- [x] (P1, S) **Personal board: spaces unusable in edit modal** (founder-reported) — dnd-kit KeyboardSensor preventDefaulted Space (drag activator) for keydowns bubbling from the edit modal through the sortable card wrapper (portal events bubble via the React tree). Fixed with shared `EditableSafeKeyboardSensor` (`apps/web/src/lib/dndSensors.ts`) ignoring editable/dialog targets, applied to PersonalBoardPage + BoardPage + BoardSwimlanesView; per-keystroke regression e2e added (`.fill()` was masking it); 22/22 personal-board e2e green desktop+mobile. ✅ shipped 2026-07-02
- [x] (P1, M) **Workspace chip sync — remaining seven pages** — Both independent Pass-11 audits found the same gap: `useSyncActiveWorkspace` had only been wired into 8 of 15 workspace/project-scoped pages, so deep-link/bookmark/back-forward to Reports, Roadmap, Project Analytics, Workspace Members, Workspace Audit Log, Workspace Settings, or Workspace Branding left the header chip misreporting the active workspace. Wired the hook into all seven, top-level of each component (project-scoped pages read `boardQuery.data?.project.workspaceId`; workspace-scoped pages read `workspaceId` straight off `useParams`, which is authoritative for their route). Also removed a surprising side effect in `WorkspaceBrandingPage`'s `ColorSection`: saving/resetting the brand color previously called `setActiveWorkspaceId(workspaceId)` as a byproduct of the mutation — redundant now that the page syncs on load, so it's gone. Deleted `DashboardPage.tsx` (confirmed dead: unreferenced anywhere, re-implemented the original unsynced-selector bug). Extended `workspace-switcher.spec.ts` with a deep-link-to-`/workspaces/:id/settings` regression case; 17/17 green desktop + mobile (was 16/16); `tsc --noEmit` clean. ✅ shipped 2026-07-02 [product-auditor + engineering-auditor Pass 11]
- [x] (P1, M) **Cross-page state-coherence QA gate + workspace-selector correctness fix** — the founder-reported root bug (header chip and the Pulse dashboard's own `<select>` were two independent, unsynced "active workspace" copies; no persistence across reload; chip misreported the workspace on a project board). `WorkspaceContext` made the single source of truth, persisted to `localStorage` (`nl.activeWorkspaceId`) and restored synchronously on load, healing to the first workspace only when the stored one is gone (covers delete-active-workspace and delete-last-workspace); switching via the chip navigates home so content re-scopes; reusable `useSyncActiveWorkspace(workspaceId)` hook introduced. Closed the process gap that let it through: permanent regression suite `apps/web/e2e/workspace-switcher.spec.ts` (7 scenarios × desktop + mobile = 14 checks — chip/dashboard agreement, switch-via-either-surface re-scopes content, reload persistence, deep-link chip sync, delete-active-workspace heal, single-workspace chip-as-link); `qa-tester`/`playwright-qa` now mandate a cross-page coherence matrix for any global/stateful UI. ✅ shipped 2026-07-01 [founder QA report; commits `c8bf9c8`, `12c7d5a`]
- [x] (P2, S) **Blocked-badge clears when the blocker resolves** — the board's `blockedByCount` counted every `BLOCKS` link regardless of the blocker's status, so the red "Blocked" badge stayed forever even after the blocking issue moved to Done. The filtered Prisma `_count` now adds `source: { status: { category: { not: DONE } } }`. Board e2e extended: after moving the blocker to Done the badge disappears on reload. 43 board unit tests + 66 board/workspace-switcher e2e green desktop + mobile. ✅ shipped 2026-07-02 [engineering-auditor Pass-11 P2-3]
- [x] (P2, S) **`qk.boardView` cache-family invalidation helper (`invalidateBoardFamily`)** — `useUpdateIssue`, `useBulkUpdateIssues`, `useAssignIssueToSprint` (`issues.ts`), `useUpdateIssueCustomFields` (`custom-fields.ts`), and all three sprint mutations (`sprints.ts`) only invalidated the legacy `qk.board(projectId)` key — a cache nothing renders from once a non-default board is selected — leaving the visible board stale for up to the global 30s `staleTime` after issue updates, bulk edits, sprint start/complete, and custom-field value changes. New `invalidateBoardFamily(qc, projectId)` helper in `apps/web/src/api/keys.ts` invalidates the legacy key AND every board-id-keyed `boardView` entry; applied to all six mutation sites. 66 e2e green desktop+mobile; `tsc --noEmit` clean. ✅ shipped 2026-07-02 [engineering-auditor Pass-11 P2-2 / Ideation #2]

_Engineering-audit Pass-7 hardening — ✅ all shipped 2026-06-28:_

- [x] (P1, S) Scope `personalAnalytics` to workspaces the user is a member of — `prisma.issue.findMany({ where: { assigneeId: userId } })` had no workspace scope; a user assigned to an issue in a workspace they are not a member of would see it in their personal analytics (open/done/overdue counts, throughput, byType, byPriority, cycle-time); fixed by adding `project: { workspace: { memberships: { some: { userId } } } }` predicate to the query in `analytics.service.ts`; 2 regression tests added [engineering-auditor Pass 7] ✅ shipped 2026-06-28
- [x] (P2, S) `promoteCard` idempotency guard — double-call created a second Issue and orphaned the first; added `if (card.promotedIssueId !== null) throw new BadRequestException('Card already promoted')` inside the transaction in `personal-boards.service.ts`; 1 regression test added [engineering-auditor Pass 7] ✅ shipped 2026-06-28
- [x] (P2, M) `AutomationRun` serial inserts → single `createMany` batch — engine called `prisma.automationRun.create()` once per rule in a loop (N round-trips); refactored to collect run-row objects during the loop and issue one `prisma.automationRun.createMany({ data: [...] })` after all rules evaluated; actions still execute per-rule in order; FAILED mid-rule rows collected and included in batch; 1 new batch-insert regression test + all 37 existing engine tests updated to assert `createMany` instead of `create` [engineering-auditor Pass 7] ✅ shipped 2026-06-28
- [x] (P2, M) `projectAnalytics` query cost reduced + `ActivityLog` composite index — (a) `projectAnalytics` now runs two targeted queries instead of one full-project scan: workload uses full scan (needs all open issues), flow chart uses `createdAt: { gte: wStart }` window-scoped query (eliminates JS-side date filtering of the entire issue table); (b) composite index `ActivityLog(field, to, createdAt)` (migration `20260628080000_add_activitylog_status_index`) added to support the `completionMap` raw query which filters `field='status' AND to = ANY(doneIds) AND createdAt BETWEEN`; index design justification: `(field, to)` equality access on the two most selective columns for status-completion lookups, `createdAt` trailing column supports range scan inside matched rows [engineering-auditor Pass 7] ✅ shipped 2026-06-28

- [x] (P1, S) Guard password reset token log with `NODE_ENV !== 'production'` — raw reset URL (including 32-byte token) is emitted via `logger.log()` with no NODE_ENV guard; pino-http redact rules do not cover explicit logger calls; any log aggregator captures the token in plaintext; add `if (process.env.NODE_ENV !== 'production')` guard; skip log entirely when SMTP_HOST is set [engineering-auditor Pass 5] ✅ shipped 2026-06-27 (Pass 5 security/perf/infra batch)
- [x] (P1, S) Remove `image/svg+xml` from ALLOWED_MIME_TYPES (or serve as `application/octet-stream`) — SVG files with embedded `<script>` tags accepted and served with `image/svg+xml` Content-Type; direct-navigate to the download URL executes script in browser context; `nosniff` partially mitigates but does not close the direct-navigate vector; simplest fix is to remove SVG from the allowlist [engineering-auditor Pass 5] ✅ shipped 2026-06-27 (Pass 5 security/perf/infra batch)
- [x] (P1, M) Rewrite CFD and burndown as DB-level `generate_series` aggregation — `reports.service.ts` fetches all project issues and all activity logs into memory with no `take` limit; for any active project (5k+ issues × 5 status changes each = 25k+ rows) the in-memory O(issues × days) loop will OOM or timeout the API process; replace with a Postgres `generate_series` window-function query bounded by `windowDays × 3` rows regardless of project size [engineering-auditor Pass 5] ✅ shipped 2026-06-27 (Pass 5 security/perf/infra batch)

- [x] (P2, S) **Complete the `@RequireScope` PAT-scope rollout on projects/workspaces controllers.** ✅ shipped 2026-07-06 (Hardening Night item 1) — expanded well beyond the original projects/workspaces/reports scope once a full inventory showed the gap was repo-wide: **every** controller under `apps/api/src/*` was audited; the ~30 that carried zero `@RequireScope` decorators (admin-settings, analytics, api-tokens, attachments, audit, automations, board, checklist, comments, components, custom-fields, dashboards, issue-templates, labels, notifications, personal-boards[promote route], poker, projects, reports, roadmap, saved-filters, search, share-tokens, sprints, standups, statuses, users, versions, work-logs, workflows, workspaces) are now fully gated, mirroring `issues.controller.ts`; 6 controllers documented in-code as legitimately exempt (`auth`, `auth/oidc`, `health`, `public` — `@Public()`/no `request.user` for `ScopeGuard` to check — plus `me` and the non-`promoteCard` routes of `personal-boards` — purely-personal caller-identity data with no shared-resource blast radius). Three new additive scope pairs in `PAT_SCOPES` (`packages/shared/src/types.ts`): `workspaces:read`/`workspaces:write` (workspace CRUD/membership + the `users` co-member directory), `admin:read`/`admin:write` (instance SSO/OIDC settings), `tokens:read`/`tokens:write` (closes a real self-escalation hole: a scoped-down PAT could previously mint itself a brand-new *unrestricted* token via `POST /me/tokens`). `projects:read`/`projects:write` and `issues:read`/`issues:write` doc comments extended to name every newly-covered project-scoped/issue-scoped resource (boards, statuses, labels, sprints, custom fields, components, versions, workflows, dashboards, automations, poker, standups, saved filters, share tokens, roadmap, reports, analytics / checklist, work logs, attachments, notifications, search, issue-scoped label+version assignment). New `pat-scope-rollout.integration.spec.ts` (real HTTP app, real `ScopeGuard`, no mocks) data-drives all 143 newly-gated routes through a DENY (wrong-scope PAT → 403 + exact `ScopeGuard` message) and ALLOW (correctly-scoped PAT never blocked by the guard) pair — 286 assertions green; `tenant-isolation.integration.spec.ts` unaffected (108/108 still BLOCKED); full API unit suite 1808 green; shared vitest 146 green; MCP 110/104-tools green; `tsc --noEmit` clean api/web/mcp. `apps/web/src/components/settings/ApiTokensSection.tsx` scope-label map and the `@next-lane/mcp` README's new "PAT scopes" reference table both updated for the 6 new scopes, flagging that some previously-any-scope-works MCP tools (`list_workspaces`, `list_projects`, `list_users`, all report tools) now enforce their matching scope. **Acceptance met:** every controller mutation/read route carries an appropriate `@RequireScope` (or a documented exemption); the new integration suite proves a mismatched-scope token is rejected (403) on project/workspace/report/and-every-other-newly-gated-domain's routes, with a matching-scope ALLOW check per route as well. [code-reviewer, role-override review 2026-07-02; founder directive Hardening Night item 1]

Ownership logic itself was already correct and unit-tested — this closed the regression-guard gap, not a live vulnerability. [engineering-auditor Pass-10 Risk #4 / Pass-11 P2-4 / Pass-12 P2-3]
- [x] (P2, S) **`resolveEnforcedWorkflowId` N+1 in bulk status updates** ✅ shipped 2026-07-02 — `bulkUpdate`'s existing perf preload (`bulkWorkflowEnforced`, one `isEnforcementEnabled` call per batch) only ever fed the *legacy* project-level fallback inside `enforceStatusChange`, not the WF-1 named-workflow resolution branch (`resolveEnforcedWorkflowId`) that branch actually hits when no explicit `boardId` is passed — exactly how `bulkUpdate` calls `update()`. For a named-workflow-enforced project, a 100-issue bulk status edit did up to ~200 extra sequential `board.findMany`/`sprint.findUnique` round trips the preload comment claimed were avoided. Fixed: new `buildBulkWorkflowResolution(projectId, issueRows)` loads the project's enforced-workflow boards ONCE and every DISTINCT sprint's ACTIVE state in ONE `sprint.findMany` (not once per issue), applies the same visibility/tie-break rule (factored into a new pure `pickEnforcedWorkflowId()` helper shared with the single-issue path) per issue in memory, and threads the result through a new `MutationOpts.resolvedWorkflowId` field (`undefined` = not pre-resolved, `null` = pre-resolved to "none applies", `string` = pre-resolved workflow id) so `enforceStatusChange`'s WF-1 branch becomes a plain map lookup instead of a fresh query per issue. 2 new unit tests assert `board.findMany` is called exactly once for a 5-issue batch (not 5) and `sprint.findMany` is called exactly once for a 4-issue, 2-distinct-sprint batch (not 4), plus that the resolution is actually load-bearing (every visible issue is enforced via `enforceTransitionForWorkflow`, not the legacy `enforceTransition` fallback); full API unit suite (1521 tests) green; `tsc --noEmit` clean. [engineering-auditor Pass-12 P2-1]

- [x] (P2, M) **Kanban sections by field (Swimlanes v2)** ✅ shipped 2026-07-02 (founder-flagged — "swim lanes is pretty distinct, I want to be able to add sections within the kanban based on fields") — extends the shipped swimlane group-by (`BoardSwimlanesView.tsx` `computeLanes()`, previously Assignee/Priority/Issue type/Epic only) to **Component**, **Sprint**, **Labels**, and project-defined **custom SELECT fields**, plus a **per-board default grouping**. `computeLanes()` stays a pure function generalized over a `GroupByDimension` union (`assignee|priority|type|epic|component|sprint|label|cf:<fieldId>`, `apps/web/src/components/board/BoardSwimlanesView.tsx`); Component/Sprint get an "unset" lane (`No component`/`No sprint`); **decision: Labels duplicates an issue into every one of its label lanes** (an issue with N labels appears in N lanes) rather than a "first label + N hint" — matches the existing multi-label-filter mental model elsewhere in the app, and per-lane independent-`DndContext` isolation (cross-lane DnD stays structurally out of scope, unchanged from v1) makes duplicate lane membership safe; plus a `No labels` lane. Custom SELECT fields render one lane per configured option (field order) then a literal `None` lane; a value later removed from the field's option list still surfaces its own lane instead of silently dropping affected issues. Lane headers gained an optional color dot (`SwimLane.color`) for label lanes. **Schema (additive, as scoped):** `Board.defaultGroupBy String?` (migration `20260702020000_board_default_group_by`) — a core dimension key or `cf:<fieldId>`, server-validated (`BoardService#updateBoard`) against the project's actual SELECT fields (rejects unknown/foreign-project/non-SELECT); `Board`'s `issueInclude` in `board.service.ts` also gained `component` (missing before — the board view never carried component data, so cards couldn't show it either, a latent gap this surfaced). URL stays `?group=<dimension>` (`cf:<fieldId>`-safe); no `?group=` falls back to `defaultGroupBy`; explicit `?group=<dim>` always overrides it; a new `?group=none` sentinel lets a user explicitly flatten the board even when a default is configured (bare param removal would just re-apply the default). `GroupBySelector` and `BoardSettingsModal`'s new "Default grouping" `<select>` (`board-default-groupby`) share one source of truth (`apps/web/src/lib/groupByDimensions.ts`) so the toolbar and settings never drift. 15 new API unit tests (1519 API tests green); `update_board` MCP tool extended with `defaultGroupBy`. 3 new/extended `swimlanes.spec.ts` cases (component incl. "No component"; custom SELECT incl. "None" + reload persistence; per-board default applies on fresh load / URL override wins / reload persists / explicit None beats a configured default) plus full `board.spec`/`board-switcher.spec`/`nav-sidebar.spec`/`components.spec`/`custom-fields.spec`/`dashboards.spec` regression, all green desktop+mobile; `api`/`web`/`mcp` `tsc --noEmit` clean; light+dark screenshots verified. **Note:** interpretation flagged for founder confirmation (the founder said "if we need to talk about that item we can") but scoped to proceed rather than block, per direction. - [x] (P1, M) **Workspace selector correctness fix** (2026-07-01, frontend-only) — QA of the workspace switcher (founder report "the workspaces selector seems buggy") reproduced four real bugs with Playwright and fixed them: (1) the header `WorkspaceChip` and the Pulse dashboard's own `<select>` were **two independent, unsynced states** — changing one never updated the other; (2) switching the chip **only re-themed the header** and left you on the previous workspace's content; (3) the active workspace **did not persist** across reload (always reset to the first workspace); (4) the chip **misreported** the current workspace when viewing a project board. Fixes: `WorkspaceContext` is now the single source of truth, persisted to `localStorage` (`nl.activeWorkspaceId`) and restored synchronously on first render, healing to the first workspace only when the stored id is gone (covers deleting the active workspace); `PulseDashboardPage` reads/writes the context instead of a local `selectedWs` (removed the local state + sync effect); the chip switcher now `navigate('/')`s so content re-scopes to the newly-active workspace; new reusable `useSyncActiveWorkspace(workspaceId)` hook exported from `WorkspaceContext`, wired into `BoardPage`, `BacklogPage`, `TriagePage`, `SettingsPage`, `StandupsPage`, `PokerStartPage`, `PokerSessionPage`, `AutomationsPage` so the chip tracks the workspace of whatever you're viewing. All `data-testid`s preserved. Verified: 7/7 Playwright QA scenarios green (incl. single-workspace user renders chip as a link, and deleting the active workspace heals to a remaining one); web `tsc --noEmit` + `pnpm build` clean; 40 desktop + 17 mobile e2e (pulse-dashboard, board, board-switcher, auth, my-work, personal-board, realtime-auth) green — no regressions (the transient failures during the run were the API rate limiter, resolved by running with `RATE_LIMIT_DISABLED=true` per the e2e preconditions). [founder QA report]

## Already Done (recent shipments — ticked for reference)

- [x] (P1, S) **Sticky Gantt date axis** ✅ 2026-08-05 [founder: *"the Gantt chart header should become sticky as I scroll down"*]
  - The axis sat inside the horizontal scroller, which is a scroll container on BOTH axes — so `sticky` there means sticking to something that never moves vertically. Lifted into its own band, horizontal scroll mirrored from the grid via `scrollLeft` (not state: it runs every scroll frame).
  - Chart wrapper `overflow-hidden` → `overflow-clip`: same rounded corners, no scrollport for the sticky element to resolve against.
  - **Two wrong turns, both caught by measuring:** (1) offsetting by the app header's height was wrong — `<main>` is the scroller and the chrome sits outside it, so the offset opened a 37px band of rows sliding through; `top: 0` is correct. (2) `z-20` tied with the bars and lost on tree order, so bars slid over the month labels — `z-30`.
  - Milestone diamonds moved fully inside the band (they used to straddle the boundary and got sliced into triangles by the new clipped viewport).
  - Presenting mode inherits it free. e2e builds a plan taller than the viewport, scrolls `<main>`, asserts the axis holds position and nothing paints over it. 31 passed / 7 skipped.

- [x] (P1, S) **Resizable epic column + dependency lines behind the bars** ✅ 2026-08-05 [founder: *"story titles are getting cut off"* / *"linking line should have a lower z index then the cards it's self"*]
  - Rail was a fixed 248px (~20 chars), so the column whose job is naming things truncated the names. Now dragged from the divider, clamped 160–560, persisted, double-click to reset, arrow-key resizable. Narrow phone layout stays fixed — it's a different design, not a smaller one.
  - **The z-order comment was lying:** the overlay was `z-10` while claiming to sit under the bars, and the bars had no z-index — a positive z-index beats `z-auto` regardless of DOM order.
  - **The obvious fix is wrong, and e2e caught it:** `z-0` and `z-auto` share one painting layer ordered by tree position, so dropping the overlay to `z-0` put it behind every row div and made the remove-dependency control unhittable. Raise the **bars** to `z-20` instead: rows (auto) < arrows (10) < bars (20). Today marker to `z-[25]` so it stays the top reading aid.
  - Both asserted by **hit-testing** (`elementFromPoint` over a linked bar must return the bar), not by class name — the class name was never the thing that was wrong.
  - roadmap-gantt + roadmap + roadmap-present 29 passed / 7 skipped; tsc + build clean.

- [x] (P1, M) **Drag a story from one epic to another on the Gantt** ✅ 2026-08-05 [founder: *"give me the ability to drag sub items from epic to epic"*]
  - A child bar meant two things and only one was draggable: horizontal position = schedule, containing block = parent. Reparenting meant leaving for the drawer.
  - **Destination from row geometry, not `elementFromPoint`** — the dragged bar is the topmost element under the pointer for most of the gesture, so DOM hit-testing answers "the thing you're dragging". The `rows` array already knows each row's `y` and owning epic.
  - The whole destination **lane** highlights (an epic owns its lane whatever its bar's length), and the tooltip names the destination key — the one fact the bar's position can't give you.
  - **Two guards had to change:** the drag threshold now counts vertical travel, and `dayDelta === 0` is no longer a no-op, or a straight-up drag would be silently swallowed. Conversely a pure vertical drag does **not** reschedule — the two meanings are independent.
  - **Backend:** `parentId` was already accepted and validated on PATCH; `growParentEpicToFit` now fires on a parent change too, since moving a dated story to another epic changes which epic must cover which window. Both epics' child lists are invalidated.
  - e2e drops onto an epic that ends *before* the story starts so the cascade has work: asserts new `parentId`, unchanged dates, the pre-drop highlight, and the grown destination `dueDate`. 26 e2e / 6 skipped, 364 api issues tests, tsc + build clean.
  - **Known gap:** an undated story renders as a paint row with no bar to grab, so it can't be reparented this way yet.

- [x] (P1, M) **Presenting mode: full-bleed read-only roadmap** ✅ 2026-08-05 [founder: *"we need a kiosk mode full bleed view for presenting the road map"*]
  - `/projects/:id/roadmap/present`, entered from a **Present** button beside the chart. No sidebar, header, project nav, page title, or card — full-bleed reads as one surface.
  - **Read-only by construction:** no mutation handlers are passed, and the chart already hides every affordance when they're absent. e2e asserts the *same admin* sees Create-epic and link handles on the normal chart and neither here.
  - **Scoped with the founder first; declines recorded:** no health strip, no release countdown, no scaled type, no pre-flight filter, no unattended auto-scroll. Live presentation, mouse-driven. In-app full-bleed over the Fullscreen API (their call) keeps it deep-linkable.
  - **Signature:** chrome fades after 2.5s of pointer stillness, returns on movement. Off under `prefers-reduced-motion`; never hidden while focused, since a keyboard user can't move a pointer to recover it.
  - **Escape leaves only when nothing else is open** — closing the drawer and ending the presentation on one press would be a bad surprise mid-room.
  - Chart is **centred, not stretched**: row geometry feeds the dependency-arrow offsets. `min-h-full` on the inner column keeps it from clipping the top once the plan outgrows the frame.
  - New `CHROMELESS_SUFFIXES` in App.tsx: the existing prefixes are root-level route families, and this is a leaf under a fully-chromed page.
  - `roadmap-present.spec.ts` 4/4 desktop+mobile; roadmap-gantt / roadmap / nav-sidebar regression 49 passed / 5 skipped; tsc + build clean.

- [x] (P1, M) **Draw and remove epic dependencies on the Gantt** ✅ 2026-08-05 [founder: asked how epic linking works on the chart → *"Yes please"*]

  - **Drop target via `elementFromPoint`,** not enter/leave: the source holds pointer capture, so bars under the cursor never get a `pointerover`. Band coordinates are grid-relative so it stays glued to the calendar when the grid scrolls mid-drag.
  - **Remove:** hover a line → × at the elbow (a gap between bars by construction). A 1.5px stroke isn't a pointer target, so an invisible 14px twin carries the hover with `pointer-events: stroke`; the layer stays `pointer-events-none` so an elbow's empty bounding box never steals a bar's click. `RoadmapDependencyDto` gained `id`.
  - **No client-side validation on purpose** — the server rejects self-links, cross-project, duplicates and reverse-duplicates with usable messages and enforces MEMBER+; the UI surfaces its wording. A silent no-op would be worse than the old behaviour.
  - **Bug found by looking at the result:** the overrun badge was gated on `childrenOutside > 0` but printed `overrunDays` — a child starting *before* its epic gave a meaningless "+0d". Now `−Nd` / `+Nd`, or nothing.

A closed epic looked identical to an untouched one — and worse after the previous day's pass, because its child stories *did* go green, so the parent reads as bad data. `EPIC_COLORS` now drives bar, text, progress track, fill and resize grip together.

A story with real dates and no sprint contributed nothing, and its epic rendered as a zero-width dot on its creation date. Every roadmap fixture in the suite put its children in sprints, so nothing caught it.

- [x] (P1, S) **A share link you just created didn't appear in the list — permanently** ✅ 2026-08-01 (found chasing an e2e failure under parallel load)
  - **The symptom looked impossible:** the new-token banner shows the freshly minted URL, and directly beneath it the list reads "No share links yet". Reloading fixes it; nothing else does.
  - **Cause:** `invalidateQueries` does not start a second fetch while one is already in flight. Open Project Settings and click "+ Create link" before the on-mount list GET has answered, and the invalidation is absorbed by that request — whose response predates the token. It lands, becomes the final state, and nothing is scheduled to correct it.
  - **Fix:** `cancelQueries` before `invalidateQueries`, and thread React Query's `AbortSignal` into the query so the cancel actually aborts the request rather than just detaching from it.
  - **Diagnosis is worth recording, because the first hypothesis was wrong.** The natural trace showed two GETs in flight and looked like an out-of-order response race; threading the signal alone changed nothing. Holding the first response back with `page.route` turned a 1-in-6 flake into a deterministic repro and showed the truth: **there was no second GET at all.** The regression test uses that same hold-back, so it fails deterministically if this returns.
  - **The class is bigger than this fix.** 86 `queryFn`s across 41 files pass no `signal`, and every list-plus-mutation pair can hit the same "act before the list loads" window. Filed in `docs/UI-REVIEW.md` as an owed audit rather than swept blind.

- [x] (P2, XS) **Selecting a knowledge-graph node hid the zoom controls behind the side rail** ✅ 2026-08-01
  - The zoom/fit cluster and the focus/orbit rail both live bottom-right; the rail is `z-20` and 22rem wide from `sm` up, so it covered the controls outright. The controls now slide clear of it, and hide below `sm` where the rail is a full-width sheet.
  - Found by Playwright reporting that the rail "intercepts pointer events" while it retried a Fit click 268 times — a dead click for a user, too.

- [x] (P1, M) **Knowledge graph: fit-to-content, and the layout world that made it necessary** ✅ 2026-08-01 [founder: *"the graph looks rather empty"* → the reshoot exposed why]
  - **The camera was the symptom; the world was the cause.** The force layout ran inside a world the exact size of the visible canvas, so past a few dozen pages it had nowhere to put nodes: repulsion pushed them outward, `clampCenter` caught them at the boundary, and they stacked into perfectly straight rails along the top and bottom of the frame with their labels overprinting each other. Visible in the 2026-08-01 `pages-graph-desktop` capture.
  - **Three parts.** The layout world now grows with √n, so per-node density stays constant as a wiki grows. `fitToContent` frames the content bounding box — measured over rendered node *boxes*, not centre points, so the outermost labels land inside the frame — on settle and from the toolbar button, now labelled **Fit graph to view** rather than "Reset zoom". And the world is pre-framed the instant its size is known, so a dense graph never spends its whole settle animation overflowing before snapping into place.
  - **Fit is capped at 100%.** It means "you can see everything", not "fill the pixels" — a three-node graph must not be blown up to 300%. Falling out of that: the two existing graph specs that assert `100%` after reset keep passing honestly, because on a graph that fits, fit and reset are the same operation.
  - **`MIN_SCALE` 0.35 → 0.15.** A dense graph on a 393px phone needs a scale below the old floor; fit would have clamped and still left nodes off-frame. The floor also has to be reachable by the − button, or the readout shows a zoom the user cannot return to by hand.
  - **`GraphMinimap` now takes world *and* viewport dimensions.** It had been using one number for both; the camera transform pivots on the viewport centre, so once the world was bigger than the canvas the "what you can see" rectangle would have been skewed.
  - **Nothing tested this, and the reason is instructive:** every existing graph spec uses a two- or three-node graph, which fits trivially. Density was the whole bug. New `knowledge-graph-fit.spec.ts` seeds 40 linked pages, asserts no node box escapes the canvas on open, then zooms and pans away — asserting that nodes really *do* go off-frame first, so the recovery check cannot pass vacuously — and asserts Fit brings them back. 18/18 across the graph specs, desktop + mobile.
  - The capture script's "click zoom-out three times" workaround is deleted; it was only ever evidence of the gap.

- [x] (P1, L) **Design elevation pass — board + nav** ✅ 2026-08-01 [founder: *"I want something before v1.0.0. It should look a whole lot better"*]
  - Run through the `frontend-design` skill, per the standing design-elevation directive.
  - **The board's problems were objective, not taste.** `--nl-canvas` and `--nl-ink-50` were byte-identical (`#f4f6f9`) — the page and the lane columns resting on it were the same colour, so the board was one grey field with hairlines. Canvas recedes to `#eef1f6`: desk < lane < card, which is what the DISPATCH "ink on paper" thesis needs and never had.
  - **The accent bar became information.** A `border-t-2` drew the same line whether a lane held one card or was over its WIP limit. It is now a capacity rail filling with `count/limit`, red when over. Lanes with no limit get a flat track — a full bar there would assert a limit that does not exist.
  - **The toolbar got a hierarchy.** Sixteen equal-weight controls in three ragged rows, with `+ Create issue` stranded bottom-right under the filter chrome. Split by intent (Act / Refine); export, import and colours demote to icon ghosts so one element is solid. Their accessible names already lived on `aria-label` and the specs use test ids, so no contract changed.
  - **I broke mobile doing it and the existing guard caught me** — the action cluster added 40px between search and filters, pushing the Type filter y=173 → y=217. Fixed with `display: contents` below `sm` so the cluster orders itself last on a phone. Worth recording: the guard existed because someone previously cared about exactly this, and it paid for itself.
  - **Nav:** four of five audit findings closed — Home row, WORKSPACE heading, `Workspace docs` (two rows both said `Docs`), and `Insights` reconciled with the `My Analytics` heading it opened. Sidebar/tab-bar mismatch and skip-link/tab-order left open and recorded in `docs/UI-REVIEW.md`.
  - **Evidence: full e2e 1140/1140, 0 failed** (14 skipped, desktop + mobile, 18.4 min). 96/96 board and 68/68 nav during development. All 26 screenshots re-shot in both themes; docs site builds; every referenced image resolves.

- [x] (P2, XS) **Header search trigger was 4px taller than every control beside it** ✅ 2026-08-01 [founder: *"fix the padding around the search bar"*]
  - **Measured, not eyeballed.** The trigger rendered at **40px** while notifications, quick links and the avatar were all **36px** — and since the cluster is `align-items: center`, the one element in the group with a visible border was also the only one standing 2px proud top and bottom. That reads as bad padding even though the padding values themselves were fine.
  - **Root cause was an inherited line-height.** Nobody had specified the button's height; it was whatever its contents computed to. The `⌘K` chip is `text-[10px]`, but Tailwind's `text-sm` on the button sets an ABSOLUTE `line-height: 20px`, which the chip inherited — so a 10px glyph occupied a 26px box and dragged the button to 40px.
  - **Fix:** `leading-none` on the chip so it stops driving layout, and an explicit `h-9` on the button so it is pinned to the cluster's 36px rather than depending on its contents. Height set explicitly on purpose: tuning `py-*` would have worked today and broken again the next time the chip changed.
  - **Verified both directions.** New `header-alignment.spec.ts` asserts every visible control in the cluster shares one height and one top edge, and that the chip is shorter than the button. Reintroducing the old classes makes it fail with `mixed heights: [40, 36, 36, 36]`; restoring them makes it pass. It asserts GEOMETRY, not class names — a class assertion would have passed throughout the bug's life.
  - **Checked the other search inputs while there** — the board's "Search cards…" is already 36px with 12px padding, identical to its toolbar peers. No change needed; the defect was specific to the header.
  - **Evidence:** `header-alignment.spec.ts` 1/1 (skipped on mobile, where the trigger is icon-only); `command-palette` + `nav-sidebar` 31/31 desktop+mobile as regression siblings; web unit 33/33; `tsc --noEmit` clean.

- [x] (P1, M) **Screenshots reshot + reshooting made reproducible** ✅ 2026-08-01 [founder: *"the docs need new screenshots and updated"*]
  - **Root cause was process.** The dressed "Nova Analytics" workspace lived only in whoever staged it last, so reshooting meant rebuilding it from memory — which is why the set sat at 2026-07-03 and two `pages-graph-*` entries stayed marked "planned" from the day the knowledge graph shipped. Now: `apps/api/prisma/seed-screenshots.ts` + `apps/web/e2e/screenshots.capture.ts` + `playwright.screenshots.config.ts`. Two commands, documented in `docs/screenshots/README.md`.
  - **Kept separate from `seed.ts` deliberately.** That one is the honest first-run experience a self-hoster gets from `docker compose up` and should stay small; this one is a showroom.
  - **Three bugs the first capture exposed**, all the seed misrepresenting the product: (1) a board `updateMany` matched zero rows — the API creates the default board lazily — so the board shot was Kanban "Main Board" with no colour rules and backlog items leaking into To Do; (2) the knowledge graph rendered six isolated dots, because `PageLink` edges are synced by `PagesService` on save and a direct Prisma write never triggers it (fixed using the same shared `parseWikiLinks` the app uses, so the documented graph is the graph the product draws); (3) the Docs tree truncated every title at the 240px default — the exact papercut the new resize handle fixes.

The docs site defaults to dark, so its pages now point at the dark variants. The seeded wiki went from 6 pages to **101 pages / 358 edges** in seven clustered sections with deliberate cross-links — uniform random edges would render as a hairball, and six nodes rendered as an empty canvas.
  - **Honest about coverage.** 26 reshot (5 new Docs surfaces); 8 carried over and **listed by name** in the README as not-reshot, because the capture script does not yet stage a four-gadget dashboard, a Gantt, a workflow graph or worklogs. Extending it is the follow-up.
