- [x] (P1, M) **Docs nav is draggable — resizable panel + drag-to-reorder/nest** ✅ 2026-08-01 [founder: *"on the docs pages the inner left doc nav should be draggable"*; founder chose BOTH readings]
  - **Resize:** new `ui/ResizeHandle` primitive + `lib/usePersistentWidth`. Pointer-capture (a fast drag leaves the 6px hit area and, without capture, dies mid-gesture), `role="separator"` with Arrow/Home/End, width persisted and CLAMPED on read — an out-of-range stored value could otherwise render a 4px panel with no way back. Built as a primitive because the board and issue drawer share the fixed-panel problem.
  - **Drag:** top/bottom quarter reorders, middle half nests. Native HTML5 DnD, not dnd-kit — a tree needs three outcomes per row and the row geometry gives that directly, where dnd-kit would mean hand-rolled nesting collision detection. Nesting gets the bigger target because it has no keyboard fallback; reordering does.
  - **The up/down buttons stay.** Drag is an addition. Removing them would take reordering away from keyboard and screen-reader users entirely.
  - **Two real bugs found, neither in the new code.** (1) `onDragOver` gated on React state set during `dragstart` — same tick, so `preventDefault()` never ran and the browser refused the drop. Now ref-driven; this fixes a fast real drag, not just the test. (2) **The bigger one:** `useMovePage.onSettled` deliberately does not refetch the tree on success (a refetch between rapid clicks used to swallow moves), which makes `optimisticallyReorderTree` the ONLY thing that updates the UI — and it explicitly did not handle re-parenting. A nest persisted server-side and never appeared on screen. Now handles it, with 7 new unit tests including refuse-to-move-into-own-subtree.
  - **Process note:** the first version of the e2e drove the drag with `page.mouse.down/move/up`, which does NOT trigger HTML5 DnD in Chromium — it reported the row unmoved and looked exactly like a product bug. `locator.dragTo` with `targetPosition` is what fires the real sequence. Recorded in the spec header so the next person doesn't lose the same hour.
  - **Evidence:** `docs-nav-drag.spec.ts` **5/5** on the real stack; **52/52** existing Pages e2e (incl. the up/down reorder spec); web unit **33/33**; `tsc --noEmit` clean.

- [x] (P1, S) **Agents can author doc templates, not just use them — 4 new MCP tools** ✅ 2026-07-31 [founder: *"MCP should be able to make doc templates"*]
  - **The gap.** The doc-templates slice shipped `list_page_templates` + `create_page_from_template`, so an agent could *use* the house format but not *establish* it. "Set up a runbook template for this workspace" was a dead end over MCP.
  - **Now:** `create_page_template`, `get_page_template`, `update_page_template`, `delete_page_template`. Server goes 126 → **130 tools (62 read / 68 write)**.
  - **Why `get_page_template` is its own tool.** `list_page_templates` is compacted and omits the markdown body; the update is a partial patch. An agent that edits `content` without reading it first replaces the template wholesale — so the description tells it to get first, and a test asserts the replace-not-merge semantics so the warning stays true.
  - **Two contract details I had WRONG** until a live round trip against the running API corrected me, both of which had already gone into the tool descriptions the model reads: an explicit `title` on `create_page_from_template` REPLACES `titleTemplate` rather than feeding `{{title}}` into it, and a `content` update replaces rather than merges. That round trip now ships as a test gated on `NL_LIVE_API` instead of living only in a commit message.
  - **Consistency:** scope selection mirrors `list_page_templates` (exactly one of `workspaceId`/`projectId`, rejected with an actionable sentence, never reaching the API when ambiguous). Writes need workspace ADMIN + `pages:write`, unchanged from REST.
  - **Evidence:** MCP **162/162** (16 new) plus the live test green end-to-end (create → get → list → update → stamp a page on both title paths → delete → template gone, page survives). `tsc --noEmit` clean. Every "126 tools" claim in README, `apps/mcp/README.md`, and the docs site reconciled against the RUNTIME count of `allTools` — grepping `group: 'read'` gives 63 and is wrong; the array has 62.

- [x] (P1, M) **Images in documentation pages — paste/drag into a page body** ✅ 2026-07-31 [founder: *"add images to our documentation"*; the follow-on to object storage, in the sequence the founder chose]
  - **How an image is addressed, and why it matters.** The page body stores `![alt](nl-image:<id>)`, an app-internal scheme rather than a URL. Two things a URL would have cost us: it bakes the deployment origin into user content (move an install from `localhost` to a domain, or export the page, and every image breaks), and it has to be fetchable without an `Authorization` header — which would make an image embedded in a private page *less private than the page*. The renderer resolves the scheme at display time, fetching each image with the reader's own token and swapping in a `blob:` URL. So an image inherits the page's permissions exactly and there is no second ACL to drift. Founder picked this (authenticated blob) over public-unguessable URLs and signed URLs.
  - **The risky change is the sanitizer, and it is fenced.** DOMPurify's URI allowlist rejects any unknown `scheme:`, so `nl-image:` had to be added to it. Added `nl-image` and nothing else; the post-sanitize hook requires `src` to be a bare id (`[A-Za-z0-9_-]{1,64}`, so no traversal, no query, no smuggled second scheme); and `nl-image:` is stripped from anchor hrefs so it can never be a link target. The 12 new web unit tests are mostly the negative cases, and were **verified failing** when the allowlist change is reverted.
  - **Storage & lifecycle.** New `PageImage` model — cascade on page delete, `SetNull` on uploader delete (keep the image if the account goes). Blob deleted *before* the row, because the row is the only pointer to the object. Uploads ride the `StorageDriver` from the entry above, so local disk or Ceph/S3 with no extra wiring. PNG/JPEG/GIF/WebP with magic-byte verification; no SVG (active document, rendered inline). 10 MB cap defined once in `packages/shared` so the editor rejects before spending the upload.
  - **Editor.** Paste or drop into the markdown editor. A text placeholder goes in at the caret immediately and is rewritten to the real reference only once the bytes are stored; a failed upload removes it again, so a saved body can never reference an image that does not exist. Save is blocked while an upload is in flight — otherwise a placeholder would be persisted into a version snapshot with nothing left to rewrite it.
  - **MCP: deliberately not exposed.** The write is "paste these bytes", which an agent has no good way to originate, and the read would hand an agent a binary blob it cannot use. Agents already receive the page body, where the reference is legible as text.
  - **Evidence:** API unit **2164/2164** (17 new — both directions of the privacy claim: a VIEWER can read, a non-member cannot, and storage is never touched before authorization fails). Web unit **23/23** (12 new). PAT-scope coverage **6/6** with the 4 new routes in the matrix. New `page-images.spec.ts` **4/4** (2 specs × desktop+mobile) against the real running stack: pastes a real PNG, asserts it *decodes* (`naturalWidth > 0` — a corrupted upload passes a `blob:`-src check but fails this one), survives a reload, is refused to another tenant and to an anonymous caller, and comes back byte-identical to the owner. Existing Pages e2e **52/52** still green.

- [x] (P1, L) **Object storage (S3-compatible) — Ceph RGW, MinIO, AWS S3, R2, Wasabi** ✅ 2026-07-31 [founder: *"How can we add object storage to this… Could we use ceph for both docker deployments and kubernetes?"*; scope confirmed: S3-compatible driver, storage first then doc images]. Uploads now go through a `StorageDriver` interface with two implementations: **`local`** (default, byte-for-byte the previous behaviour — same `UPLOADS_DIR`, same keys, so no migration for anyone) and **`s3`**.
  - **Why S3-compatible rather than Ceph-native.** Ceph's RADOS Gateway speaks S3, so one driver covers Ceph *and* MinIO *and* AWS/R2/Wasabi. Targeting Ceph's own API would have bought tighter Ceph integration while excluding every other backend — and would have forced a single-host `docker compose` user to run a Ceph cluster, contradicting the runs-first-try promise. Compose therefore ships **MinIO behind a profile** (`docker compose --profile minio up -d`, default `up` unchanged and verified), while Kubernetes documents Ceph via Rook.
  - **A real bug the real-protocol test caught before shipping.** The AWS SDK v3 defaults to `requestChecksumCalculation: WHEN_SUPPORTED`, which sends streamed uploads as `Content-Encoding: aws-chunked` with an `x-amz-checksum-*` trailer. Real AWS parses that; **MinIO and several Ceph RGW versions do not — they store the chunk framing AS the file content.** Every upload would have been silently corrupted, discovered only when someone opened an attachment. The s3rver integration test stored literally `8\nprefixed\n0\nx-amz-checksum-crc32:slcoPQ==` instead of `prefixed`. Fixed with `WHEN_REQUIRED`. **A mocked SDK would have passed** — this is the third time this week that testing the real artifact caught what a mock could not.
  - **Other decisions worth keeping.** Path-style addressing defaults ON whenever a custom endpoint is set, because virtual-host style needs `bucket.host` to resolve in DNS and a self-hosted RGW/MinIO is reached by a bare hostname or IP. Credentials are optional so the AWS provider chain (IRSA / instance profile) works; **half a pair is rejected at startup** in the API and at render time in the chart, since a partial pair shadows the chain and fails later with a 403 that points nowhere. Misconfiguration **fails at boot rather than falling back to local disk** — a silent fallback yields a working-looking app whose uploads vanish when the pod is replaced.
  - **Helm:** `storage.driver` + `storage.s3.*`, credentials inline **or** via `existingSecret` with configurable key names — the Rook-Ceph path, since `CephObjectStoreUser` publishes `AccessKey`/`SecretKey` and those must be remapped. The `validateUploadsScaling` guard is now object-storage-aware: `storage.driver=s3` **lifts the ReadWriteMany requirement entirely** (every replica shares one bucket), and the failure message now recommends s3 first. Verified in both directions — `replicaCount=3` still fails on local storage, and renders cleanly with s3.
  - **Evidence:** API unit **2147/2147** (100 suites, +7 real-protocol S3 tests via in-process `s3rver`, no Docker needed so CI runs them too). Chart verified across 9 configurations (local / s3+endpoint / existingSecret remap / IRSA-no-creds / missing bucket / half-credential / guard-fails-local / guard-passes-s3 / `helm lint`). Config-parity smoke green (55 documented vars). **Live end-to-end against a real S3 server:** logo upload `201`, `GET` byte-identical, the stored object byte-identical to the source PNG, **zero** files written to local disk, and the previous object cleaned up on replace; then the same round trip re-verified on the `local` driver to prove the default path is untouched.

- [x] (P0, XS) **REAL BUG: every file upload 500s in Docker and Kubernetes — `EXDEV: cross-device link not permitted`** ✅ 2026-07-31 [founder report: *"the logo upload gives 500 errors on upload"*]. Both upload paths — workspace logo (`workspaces.service.ts:478`) and issue attachments (`attachments.service.ts:220`) — moved the multer temp file with a bare `fs.renameSync`. `rename(2)` **cannot cross a filesystem boundary**, and every real deployment crosses one: multer writes to `os.tmpdir()` (`/tmp`) while `UPLOADS_DIR` is `/app/uploads`, a **named volume** in docker-compose and a **separate `emptyDir`/PVC** in the Helm chart. The throw was unhandled, so it surfaced as a blunt 500 with no clue as to the cause.
  - **Why it was invisible:** on a dev host `/tmp` and `./uploads` are the same disk, so `rename` succeeds. The 2026-07-06 hardening entry that claims a "live-verified real multipart `curl` round-trip (workspace logo + issue attachment, byte-identical)" was true — and still missed this, because it ran against a host filesystem, not a container volume. Same root class as the Kustomize and CSP-smoke defects: **the artifact that ships was never the artifact that was tested.**
  - **Reproduced, not inferred.** Ran the real API with `UPLOADS_DIR=/dev/shm/nl-uploads` (tmpfs — a genuinely different device, exactly mirroring the container topology) and got the founder's error verbatim: `POST /workspaces/:id/logo` → **HTTP 500**, log line `EXDEV: cross-device link not permitted, rename '/tmp/nl-logo-…' -> '/dev/shm/nl-uploads/nl-logo-…'`.
  - **Fix:** new `common/move-file.util.ts` → `moveUploadedFile()` tries the cheap atomic rename and falls back to `copyFileSync` + `unlinkSync` **only** on `EXDEV`; any other errno still propagates, so a genuine failure isn't masked. A failed unlink does not fail the upload (the destination is already written).
  - **Verified after the fix on the same cross-device setup:** logo **500 → 201**, `GET` round-trips **byte-identical**, file present on the other device; attachment upload **201** and download byte-identical. Regression test uses the real `/dev/shm` device (a mocked `fs` would "prove" a fix that still breaks on a volume) and **asserts the premise** — that the two paths differ by `st_dev` and that a bare `renameSync` throws `EXDEV` — so it can never silently degrade into a same-device move. API unit **2129/2129** (+3), `tsc` clean.

- [x] (P1, S) **REAL BUG: the Activity log didn't update after your own change — it waited for a socket echo** ✅ 2026-07-31. Surfaced as the intermittent `issue-detail.spec.ts:53` mobile failure, but the test was right and the product was wrong. **Two independent defects, both found by instrumenting rather than reasoning:**
  - **(1) No mutation invalidated `qk.activity` at all.** The ONLY thing that refreshed the activity log was the `issue.updated` handler in `api/socket.ts`. So observing your *own* write required a round trip through the realtime channel and back — fine when the socket is connected and fast, silently broken otherwise (no Redis adapter, a proxy that drops WebSockets, a reconnect window). Real-user symptom: change a status, and the Activity panel just doesn't show it. Fixed in `useUpdateIssue`, `useBulkUpdateIssues`, `useMoveIssue` (a board drag IS a status change and the server logs one) and `useToggleIssueLabel` (LabelsService logs attach/detach).
  - **(2) Invalidating mid-flight silently adopted stale data.** Adding the invalidation only cut the failure rate from ~17% to ~4%. Instrumented trace of a survivor — **one request and one response total**: `t=105543` activity GET fires as the drawer opens · `t=105583` status change starts · `t=106843` invalidate lands **while that GET is still in flight** · `t=107027` it resolves with `rows=1 fields=created`, i.e. pre-change data, and marks the query fresh. No refetch ever follows. TanStack deduped the invalidation onto a request issued *before* the write. Fixed with `cancelRefetch: true`, centralised in a documented `invalidateIssueActivity()` helper in `api/keys.ts` so the flag can't be dropped by a future caller who doesn't know why it's there.
  - **Proven in both directions**, same build and same load (40 trials, 8 workers): `cancelRefetch: true` → **0/40**; `cancelRefetch: false` → **2/40**; no invalidation at all → ~4/24 baseline on `main`. Regression: `issue-detail` + `board` + `issue-links` 22/22, web unit 11/11, `tsc` clean.
  - **Correcting my own earlier filing:** the first version of this entry blamed `useOverlay`'s outside-click handler closing the drawer on mobile. That was wrong on both counts — `useOverlay` has no outside-click close (only Escape + Tab trapping), and the failure snapshots show the drawer **open** with the Activity panel rendered. I had misread one CI snapshot and theorised from it instead of reproducing. The drawer never closed; the list was simply stale.

- [x] (P1, M) **Doc templates for Pages — preloadable markdown formats, workspace-wide and per-project** ✅ 2026-07-30 [founder directive: *"for my docs I would like templates that I can preload with certain markdown formats"*; scope confirmed by the founder as **both** scopes and **both** starters+custom]. New `PageTemplate` model scoped exactly like `Page` (`workspaceId` always set, `projectId` nullable = workspace-wide), so a shared house style lives once at the workspace and is offered when creating a page *anywhere* in it — including inside a project — while a project can layer its own on top. The picker sorts project rows first and badges each row's scope, so a same-named project template reads as a deliberate override rather than a duplicate. Six starters ship seeded (Meeting notes, Decision record, Runbook, Spec/PRD, Retrospective, How-to guide) as ordinary rows the user can edit or delete. Placeholders (`{{date}}`, `{{datetime}}`, `{{title}}`, `{{author}}`, …) are substituted server-side by a renderer in `packages/shared` that the editor reuses for its preview; unknown tokens survive verbatim (a fill-me-in marker must not be silently deleted) and the editor warns about them first.
  - **Three decisions worth keeping.** *Partial unique indexes, not a composite `@@unique`:* Postgres treats NULLs as distinct, so `@@unique([workspaceId, projectId, name])` would have constrained nothing for workspace-wide rows — precisely the collision worth preventing. Proved in SQL: two workspace "Runbook"s rejected, a project override allowed. *Seeding keyed on a `Workspace.pageTemplatesSeededAt` marker, never on a zero-count:* keyed on the count, a workspace whose owner deleted all six starters would get them back on the next restart. Verified live — a null-marker workspace was seeded 6 on boot; a purged one stayed at 0. *Backfill at boot, not lazily in the list endpoint:* a GET that writes races itself and breaks on a read-only replica; the marker is claimed with a conditional `updateMany` so concurrent replicas can't double-seed.
  - **Surface:** 8 REST routes gated by `pages:*` rather than `projects:*` (a template is page content; a wiki-scoped agent shouldn't need project administration), picker in the New-page modal, management section in project **and** workspace settings, and 2 MCP tools — `list_page_templates`, `create_page_from_template` — taking the server to **126 tools (61 read / 65 write)**.
  - **Evidence:** API unit 2126/2126, shared 210/210, MCP 155/155, web 11/11; DB-backed integration **464/464** including 9 new cross-tenant rows (list/read/create/patch/delete/create-page in both scopes) and all 8 routes added to `pat-scope-matrix.fixture.ts` — whose coverage guard caught them missing, exactly as designed. A temporary canary row was used to prove the new isolation rows genuinely execute rather than sitting inert. E2E: new `page-templates.spec.ts` 8/8 plus 42/42 pages and 68/68 settings/workspace regressions, all desktop + mobile. Full stack exercised against a real Postgres end-to-end (seeding, rendering, inheritance, and every 400/409 guard).
  - **Two real bugs the tests did not catch, found by running the real artifact:** every mutation double-encoded its JSON body (`request()` already stringifies) — typecheck and unit tests both passed it, and only the live UI produced `Unexpected token '"' … is not valid JSON`; and the picker's bare `grid` sized its implicit column to max-content, pushing rows *wider than the modal* and clipping every description mid-word, worst on a 390px phone. Both fixed; the second now has a geometry assertion comparing row bounds to the dialog. A third, smaller one came from the screenshots: the starters used GFM task lists (`- [ ]`), which this app's `MarkdownRenderer` cannot render (`input` is not in the DOMPurify allowlist) so they displayed as literal `[ ]` — starters switched to plain bullets, with a test forbidding the syntax in future starters.

- [x] (P1, S) **R1 — recall that returns answers, not titles: search snippets, real server-side pagination, and a comment index** ✅ 2026-07-30 — the highest-leverage item in `docs/RESEARCH-AGENT-MEMORY.md` (§4.1 measured recall as the weakest axis of Next-Lane-as-agent-memory). `searchPagesFts` ranked on the indexed body and then threw it away, returning titles only; the only way to judge a hit was `get_page`, capped at 256 KiB. **Measured before: 19,810 B across 4 calls** for "what do we know about rate limiting?" (1 search + 3 full page reads). **After: 1,112 B in 1 call — −94.4%.** Shipped: `ts_headline` snippets on pages/issues/comments (`MaxFragments=2, MaxWords=20, MinWords=8`, ≈260 chars, hard-capped at 400) delimited with U+E000/U+E001 Private Use Area sentinels — deliberately NOT `ts_headline`'s `<b>` default, because the DTO is rendered as text by the web (raw HTML would either print literally or invite injection of user-authored content) and any ASCII marker collides with the markdown being excerpted; the headline runs in an outer select over an already-`LIMIT`ed subquery because it re-parses the document per row. Real `limit`/`offset` (default 20, max 50) replaced the hard-coded `RESULT_CAP = 20` whose only "paging" was a client-side slice in MCP — match #21 was previously unreachable and `total` was a lie; `total` now comes from `COUNT(*) OVER()` in the same query (no extra round trip) with `hasMore`, and a deterministic `ORDER BY rank DESC, id` so paging can't duplicate or skip rows. New `?groups=` param so a caller asking only for comments doesn't pay for four queries. **Comments are searchable for the first time** (migration `20260730120000_add_comment_fts`, `Comment.searchVector` generated tsvector + GIN mirroring `Page`/`Issue`): "what did we decide about rate limiting?" is now 1 call / 441 B and was previously unanswerable — `list_comments` is per-issue only, so you had to already know the answer's location. Scoped Comment → Issue → Project → workspace under the route's existing `issues:read` (a comment is issue data — no new scope). MCP updated in the same change: `search_pages` and `search_issues` page server-side with accurate descriptions, `search_pages` drops the per-row `workspaceId` (which also makes its previously-inaccurate published compact-shape description true), and a new `search_comments` tool exposes the group. Web Cmd-K renders the snippet as a second line with `<mark>`-highlighted matches, built from text nodes via `splitSearchHighlight` (never `dangerouslySetInnerHTML`) and `aria-hidden` so option accessible names stay the title. **Evidence:** API unit 2102/2102, shared vitest 194, MCP 137, DB-backed integration 448/448 — including a new tenant-isolation block with positive AND negative controls proving the snippet TEXT (not just the id) and the new comment index never cross a tenant boundary, plus an out-of-range-`limit` 400; `tsc --noEmit` clean on api/web/mcp. **Deliberately not in scope:** the board/backlog `GET /issues?q=` search path (a different service, no DTO change needed) and R2–R5 from the research doc.

- [x] (P1, XS) **REAL BUG: reopening "New page" kept the previous title in the box, and typing spliced the two together** ✅ 2026-07-30 — create a page, then create another from the tree: the title input briefly still held the FIRST page's title with the caret parked at offset 0, so a fast typist's text landed *in front of it*. Typing "Other Doc" over a leftover "Draft Doc" persisted a page genuinely named **"Other DocDraft Doc"** (verified in the database, not just the DOM). **Cause:** `CreatePageModal` stays mounted while closed (`Modal` renders `null`), so its `title` state survives the previous session, and the reset lived in a `useEffect`. A post-commit effect is one render too late: the reopened modal *committed* a render carrying the stale title into the real `<input>`, and `autoFocus` put the caret at 0. A keystroke arriving in that window wins outright — `onChange` reads the stale DOM value and its higher-priority update supersedes the still-pending reset. **Fix:** reset the draft *during render* (React's documented "adjusting state when a prop changes" pattern) so React re-runs the component before touching the DOM and the stale value is never observable. **Evidence:** instrumented build captured the losing interleaving (modal mount render with the previous title → reset effect fires → first `onChange` reads `"OOther Doc 7"` with no reset commit in between); a 300-trial harness measured 8/300 (2.7%) corrupted titles on `mobile-chrome` before the fix and **0/300 after** (Fisher exact p≈0.007), with `pages-p1-fixes.spec.ts` green 200/200 at `--repeat-each=20` across desktop + mobile. **Not a regression from PR #52** (`modulePreload.polyfill: false`) — measured at the same rate on the parent commit `33c5e4b` (7/300) and on `main` with `polyfill: true` restored (6/300); the three configurations are statistically indistinguishable, and the parent commit's built entry chunk is byte-identical (md5) to `main`-with-polyfill-true. The green E2E runs before `fe4c646` were luck: the suite takes exactly one sample of this race per run. Desktop measured 0/300 because only the mobile tree drawer's create button unmounts a whole portal in the same commit that mounts the modal, which is what widens the window. **Same pattern still present (not fixed here, no test coverage exercising it):** `CommandPalette.tsx:59` (resets `query` on open) and `BoardSwitcher.tsx:76` (resets the board `name` on open).
- [x] (P0, S) **REAL BUG: reordering was silently broken on any non-C-collation Postgres** ✅ 2026-07-29 (PR #43) — drag/move an item to the TOP of a list, refresh, and it reappears at the BOTTOM. No error, no failed request, nothing in the logs — the write is stored correctly and read back in the wrong order. Affects **every ordered list in the product**: board cards + backlog (`Issue.rank`), the personal board (`PersonalCard.rank`) and the page tree (`Page.rank`). **Cause:** ranks are `fractional-indexing` keys over `[0-9A-Za-z]`, correct only under ASCII/byte comparison. Prepending walks DOWN from `a0` into the uppercase range (`Zz`, `Zy`, `Zx`, …) *because* uppercase sorts before lowercase in ASCII. Under a linguistic collation (`en_US.utf8` — the default for the Debian `postgres` images and for most managed Postgres) case is only a tertiary weight, so `'a0' < 'Zy' < 'Zz'` and `ORDER BY rank` returns an order the algorithm never intended. **Fix:** migration `20260729160000_rank_columns_c_collation` pins `COLLATE "C"` on the three rank columns, which also rebuilds their indexes with the right sort order. Correct on any database whatever its default collation. **Evidence:** created a PG16 database with an ICU `en-US` collation and replayed the six moves the page-reorder e2e performs — client expects `Beta, Gamma, Alpha`, server returned `Alpha, Beta, Gamma`; the same probe against a `C.UTF-8` database returned `Beta, Gamma, Alpha`; after the migration the en-US database returned `Beta, Gamma, Alpha` too. That wrong array is byte-identical to what CI had been reporting. **This is why CI and local disagreed for four rounds:** CI's `postgres:16` service initialises `en_US.utf8`, the sandbox's Postgres is `C.UTF-8` — the suite was right and the product was wrong, and no amount of local repetition could ever have shown it. The local harness now runs against an ICU `en-US` database so it can. `e2e.yml` documents that the Debian image is deliberate coverage; `packages/shared/src/rank.ts` carries the byte-order requirement. [PR #43]

- [x] (P0, M) **E2E flakiness eliminated — CI went red with a *different* test every run** ✅ 2026-07-29 (PR #43, follow-on to the gate restoration below) — after the four product bugs were fixed the local suite was green (1120/0) but CI still failed ~1 test per run, rotating between three specs. That rotation was the tell: systemic flakiness, not a broken test. **Cause 1 — specs raced their own writes.** `pages-qa-extra` (page reorder), `qa-adversarial` (issue status + drag-drop), `inline-card-status`, `personal-board`, `dashboards-phase2` and `card-colors` all mutate optimistically and then `page.reload()`. The optimistic cache updates the instant the click is handled, so every pre-reload assertion passes while the POST/PATCH is still in flight — and the reload aborts it. CI's slower I/O widened the window; the reorder spec came back with the *pristine* seed order, i.e. none of its six moves had reached the server. The guard two of those specs already had, `page.waitForLoadState('networkidle')`, **does nothing**: Playwright's lifecycle events are sticky per document (`playwright-core/lib/server/frames.js` — `_startNetworkIdleTimer()` returns early once `networkidle` has fired, and only the idle timer ever removes it), so on a page that has been open a while the call returns immediately. Replaced with a new `trackApiWrites(page)` helper in `apps/web/e2e/helpers.ts` that records every non-GET `/api/**` request and its response; `settle({ match, atLeast })` waits for the server to *answer* the writes and asserts none 4xx'd. This is strictly **stronger** than before — the reorder spec now proves all six moves were accepted (a swallowed click fails as `acked=5/6` instead of an opaque array diff) and the reload still re-reads from the server. `card-colors` got a matching `saveColors()` wrapper around `page.waitForResponse`. **Cause 2 — two ambiguous text selectors.** `settings-robustness`'s breadcrumb check used `getByText(newName).first()`; the always-mounted `AppSidebar` also lists the project by name and sits *before* the header in the DOM, and it is `hidden lg:flex` — so on mobile `.first()` resolved to a genuinely `display:none` element, but only once the sidebar's own projects query had landed. Now targets `project-breadcrumb-name`. The same file's GitHub `owner/repo` guard asserted `getByText(/owner\/repo.*format/i)`, which matched the **Gitea** section's permanent hint on the same page — so it passed without ever seeing the error, and strict-mode-failed whenever the real error toast rendered first. Now asserts on the error toast (`[data-toast][data-variant="error"]`). **Not a cause:** the `Workspace_slug_key` duplicate-key lines in CI's Postgres log. They come from the app's own first-run auto-create (`PulseDashboardPage` creates "My Workspace" for every brand-new user) and are absorbed by `WorkspacesService.create`'s P2002 retry — proven by firing 24 concurrent identical-name creates against a live API: **0 failures, 24 distinct slugs**. Benign log noise, no spec change needed. **Diagnosability:** `scripts/summarize-playwright.mjs` now prints the failing **assertion's** line (`↳ failed at file:line:col`), not just the test's — the reorder spec asserts the same expected order both before and after its reload, and a CI tail could not tell which one blew up. **No test weakened:** no skips, no deleted assertions, no relaxed matchers, no timeout or retry bumps. **Gates:** full local suite `retries=1`, both projects, run against an ICU `en-US` collation database (CI-faithful — see the rank-collation entry above) — **1122 passed, 0 failed, 0 flaky, 8 skipped**; the three rotating specs stressed `--repeat-each=10` across both projects under deliberate CPU contention (370/370, `retries=0`); CI green across all 4 shards. Note the earlier 1130/0 figure was taken on a `C.UTF-8` database, which — as the entry above explains — could not have caught the real bug underneath these flakes. [PR #43]

- [x] (P0, M) **E2E gate restored — 4 real product bugs + 5 stale specs (red 25 days)** ✅ 2026-07-29 (PR #43) — the Playwright suite had been red since 2026-07-01 (~180 consecutive failed runs), so *nothing* merging since then was actually gated. Diagnosed by standing the full stack up in-sandbox (Postgres 16 + Redis + API + vite preview; no Docker daemon available) and running the real suite instead of inferring from CI tails. Baseline: **21 failed / 1097 passed** locally, 15 in CI. **Four REAL product bugs, all user-visible:** (1) **ProjectNav overflowed and buried its own controls** — the component assumed "the primary FOUR tabs + More + Settings always fit in one row"; promoting Docs to a first-class tab made five, so at 393px the row overflowed and, because the shell is `overflow-x-clip`, the spill was invisible AND unreachable — the right-pinned Settings block rendered *on top of* the "More" button (Chromium: gear icon "intercepts pointer events"). Primary tabs now scroll horizontally (`.nl-tabstrip`); More + Settings sit outside the scroller so both stay hit-testable at any width (an `overflow-x` ancestor clips on Y too and would eat the dropdown); dropdown right-anchored below `sm`. (2) **The More menu closed itself** — every early return in `BoardPage` rendered a bare `<Shell>` while the loaded branch rendered `<CardFieldDefsProvider><Shell>`; React compares element type by position, so the moment board data landed the whole shell unmounted and remounted, taking `ProjectNav` with it and resetting its state. Open "More" on a still-fetching board and it silently snapped shut (reproduced 3/6 with an instrumented probe, 6/6 clean after). (3) **A 401 on an unauthenticated request logged you out** — the app boots logged-out, fires `GET /api/workspaces` with no token, and the blanket `clearAuth()` on that 401 wiped a token established while the request was in flight. Now only clears when the request actually presented a token. (4) **Reordering a page swallowed the next click** — `useMovePage` invalidated the tree after every move; that refetch reshuffled rows between clicks, so `handleMove` (which resolves the node's index in the tree it holds) decided the next move was out of bounds and returned silently — six clicks, five requests, no error. Tree is no longer invalidated on a successful move (the optimistic reorder already encodes the intent); moves are also serialised so concurrent writes can't rank against neighbours that haven't moved yet. **Five stale specs** (app changed deliberately, specs never followed — all trace to `1c23627`, landed under a `docs:` subject, which is exactly where the suite went red): csv-export's exact header (export gained Start Date/Description/Component/Fix Versions/Parent/Original Estimate), role-enforcement's "idempotent upsert" assumption (`addMember` 409s on an existing member *by design*, asserted in `workspaces.service.spec.ts` — positive control now adds a new user, plus a new test pinning the 409), sprint-indicator's badge (breadcrumb drops badges below 640px on purpose so the project name wins the width — split into a desktop assertion + a mobile one pinning the collapse), attachments' `getByText('Attachments')` (substring match that resolved to the project's own name "Attachments Test"), and three `loginViaToken` helpers that injected the JWT *after* `goto('/login')` (now `addInitScript`, the correct Playwright pattern). **No test was weakened** — no skips, no deleted assertions, no timeout bumps to hide a hang; two specs gained coverage. Gates: web `tsc` clean; affected specs 114/114 then 40/40 desktop+mobile; pages reorder stressed 128/128 (`--repeat-each=8`) and 80/80 on the instrumented probe that had reproduced the swallowed click. [PR #43; e2e red since 2026-07-01]

- [x] (P1, M) **Org-wide Pages — Slices 15+16: cross-workspace-safe `[[wiki-link]]` resolution + workspace-wide graph (backend)** ✅ shipped 2026-07-17 (Ready queue #10-#11) — the two slices Slices 1-2 (`docs/BACKLOG.md` #8-#9) deliberately deferred: `docs/ROADMAP.md` Phase 11 continuation items 15-16. **Territory:** `apps/api/src/pages/**` only (no schema change — `PageLink` was already scope-agnostic). **Slice 15 — resolution:** `syncWikiLinks`'s title-candidate query is rescoped from `scopeWhere(scope)` (project-only, or workspace-docs-only) to a flat `workspaceId: scope.workspaceId` filter — the candidate set becomes "every page, project-scoped OR workspace-scoped, in the SAME workspace," so a project-A page's `[[link]]` can now resolve to a page in project B, to/from the workspace-docs space, and so on, creating a real cross-project `PageLink` edge. **Authz (verbatim, non-negotiable per the founder's exact question):** the candidate query stays filtered to the page's own `workspaceId` — a `[[Title]]` whose only match lives in a DIFFERENT workspace resolves to NOTHING (zero `PageLink` row), rendered identically to a genuinely nonexistent title (Obsidian's "not-yet-created page" state), never a distinguishable "restricted" state, which would itself leak the foreign page's existence — the same suppress-don't-half-reveal posture the `4d3a43a` `/search` leak fix used. Safe within a workspace because every member already holds ≥VIEWER on every project in it (`getEffectiveProjectRole`), so a cross-project link within one workspace never crosses a visibility boundary. **Tie-break (regression-proofed, not a redesign):** candidates are fetched once (`createdAt asc`), then reduced `title -> id` in TWO passes — same-scope candidates first (same `projectId` for a project page, or another workspace-docs page for a workspace page — so a same-project title match ALWAYS beats an other-project one, proven by a dedicated test with an intentionally-older other-project match), then a second pass over every candidate fills any title with no same-scope match, oldest-wins either way. `MAX_OUTGOING_LINKS` cap unchanged; `syncIssueLinks` untouched (issues stay project-scoped). **Slice 16 — graph:** `GET /workspaces/:id/pages/graph` now calls `buildGraph({ workspaceId })` (dropped the `projectId: null` filter) — nodes are every page whose `workspaceId` matches (union of every project's pages + workspace-docs), edges scoped to that retained node-id set exactly like the per-project graph (no dangling edges, verified under the node cap). `GET /projects/:id/pages/graph` is UNCHANGED and re-verified to still drop a cross-project edge to a non-retained node (new regression test — the per-project graph was already correct by construction, just never previously exercised against a REAL cross-project edge since none could exist before Slice 15). **DTO widening:** `PageBacklinkDto`/`PageResolvedLinkDto` (`packages/shared/src/types.ts`) gained nullable `source-`/`targetProjectId`, nullable `source-`/`targetProjectKey`, and always-present `source-`/`targetWorkspaceId` — `GET /pages/:id/backlinks`/`GET /pages/:id/links` can now legitimately return a page in a different project or the workspace-docs space, and these fields let a client route to it and label it (a cross-project badge) without a second round trip; `pages.service.ts`'s `backlinks()`/`links()` now `select` the extra `project.key`/`workspaceId` fields on the included source/target page. **Tests:** `pages.service.spec.ts` net +6 (2 pre-existing tests — "does NOT resolve … cross-scope isolation" in both directions — deliberately INVERTED to assert the new cross-scope resolution instead of the old isolation, since that isolation was the explicitly-scoped-out behavior Slice 15 was built to remove; new cases for cross-project resolution + backlinks/links DTO fields, the same-scope tie-break, the cross-workspace-zero-resolution case, the workspace-graph union, and the per-project-graph-unchanged case; 70/70 in that file, 2081/2081 across all 96 API unit suites). **Adversarial acceptance gate** (mirroring exactly how the `4d3a43a` search-leak fix was verified): a new `tenant-isolation.integration.spec.ts` describe block ("org-level-docs epic — cross-workspace-safe `[[wiki-link]]` resolution + workspace graph") sets up two REAL tenants (workspaces) with a COLLIDING page title — tenant A's `[[link]]` to it resolves to zero `PageLink` rows, `unresolvedTitles` contains it exactly like a nonexistent title, and neither the foreign page id nor the foreign workspace id ever appears in any response body; a same-run POSITIVE CONTROL (a second real project created inside tenant A's own workspace) proves the legitimate cross-project case resolves and both directions' backlinks/links carry the correct cross-project scope fields; a final pair of tests confirms tenant A's workspace graph unions both of its own projects with zero tenant-B nodes/edges, and tenant B's own graph is symmetric — 4 new integration tests, 441/441 across 3 integration suites, run against a throwaway `nextlane_slice3` DB (`prisma migrate deploy` + `test:isolation`, dropped after — never the shared dev DB/`:4000` API). No new `pat-scope-matrix.fixture.ts` row needed (reuses the existing `GET /workspaces/:id/pages/graph` route, already rostered). `tsc --noEmit` clean api/web/mcp/shared. **MCP: deliberately NOT touched this slice** — the existing `get_page_graph`/`get_page_backlinks`/`get_page_links`/workspace-graph MCP tools already call these same REST routes, so they transparently inherit the broader resolution and the new DTO fields with zero server-side change required; a client-side "cross-project badge" affordance for MCP responses is a natural follow-up but not required for correctness (the raw fields are already in the JSON an agent receives). **Frontend cross-project scope-indicator/link-routing UI (item 12's originally-deferred half) is now unblocked by this real backend contract but NOT built this slice** — filed as new Ready-queue item #12b. New `PagesService.createWorkspacePage()` + `POST /workspaces/:id/pages`, `GET /workspaces/:id/pages/tree`, `GET /workspaces/:id/pages/graph` mirror the project entry points — the existing by-id routes (`GET/PATCH/DELETE /pages/:id`, move, versions, links, backlinks, issues) already work for both kinds unchanged, since the service branches internally. **`[[wiki-link]]` resolution scope (explicit, matches the Ready item's own sequencing — cross-workspace-safe resolution is item #10/ROADMAP item 15, a LATER slice, deliberately not done here):** a workspace page resolves `[[titles]]` among that workspace's OTHER workspace-level pages only; a PROJECT page's resolution is completely UNCHANGED (still same-project only) — verified with adversarial tests in both directions (a workspace page does NOT resolve a title matching a project page, and vice versa). **Issue-key sync is skipped entirely for a workspace page** (documented as the deliberate slice-2 behavior in-code) — `Issue` is project-scoped and a workspace page has no project to resolve keys against; a control-case test confirms issue-key sync is unchanged for project pages. **Realtime:** new `RealtimeService.emitToWorkspace()` + `workspaceRoom()` (`workspace:<id>`, mirroring `userRoom`'s `<kind>:<id>` convention) broadcasts `page.updated` to the workspace room for a workspace page instead of the project room. **`SearchService` — two silent workspace-page-drop bugs fixed ahead of any user report** (flagged by the schema-architect before this slice landed): `searchPagesIlike` scoped tenancy via `project: { workspaceId: { in } } }`, a relation filter that silently excludes every `projectId: null` row; `searchPagesFts`'s raw query used `JOIN "Project"`, an inner join that silently drops every workspace page (no `tsc` error — caught only by reasoning about nullability). - [x] (P0+P1, S–M) **Pass-14 audit hardening — `/search` page-data scope leak (P0) + integration specs into CI (P1) + parser caps (P2)** ✅ shipped 2026-07-10 [engineering-auditor Pass 14] — **P0 (live-reproduced):** the combined `GET /search` (route-gated `issues:read`) also returned knowledge-base **page** hits in its `{issues, pages, projects}` payload, so a PAT scoped only `issues:read` received wiki content even though `/pages/*` and the pages-only `/search/pages` correctly 403 it — contradicting the token-creation UI's "restricts this token to only those operations" promise. **Fix:** `SearchController` computes `canReadPages(user)` — JWT sessions + unscoped PATs are full-access (`patScopes` undefined), a scoped PAT must explicitly hold `pages:read` — and threads an `includePages` flag into `SearchService.search()`, which now skips the pages query entirely (returns `pages: []`) when false; `searchPagesOnly`/`/search/pages` unchanged. **Live-reproduced closed** end-to-end against a real API+Postgres on a throwaway instance: registered a user, created a page with a distinctive marker, minted an `issues:read`-only PAT → `GET /search?q=<marker>` now returns `issues=0 pages=0` (was `pages=1`); control checks all pass — a `pages:read` PAT still finds it via `/search/pages` (1), a JWT session still gets `pages=1` from `/search` (full access unaffected), and the `issues:read` PAT still 403s on `/search/pages`. **P1 root-cause (the audit's deeper point):** the three DB-backed safety nets — `tenant-isolation`, `pat-scope-rollout`, `pat-scope-coverage` — ran in NO CI workflow (`ci.yml`'s unit lane is deliberately DB-free/mocked), so a correct guard that never executes caught nothing; `pat-scope-coverage` was in fact **red at HEAD**, flagging 4 real `@RequireScope('pages:read')` routes never added to the matrix (`GET /search/pages`, `/pages/:id/links`, `/pages/:id/issues`, `/issues/:id/pages`). Added those 4 rows to `pat-scope-matrix.fixture.ts` (rollout spec's DENY/ALLOW now data-drives them too) and wired a new `integration-test` job into `.github/workflows/ci.yml` — Postgres 16 service container + `prisma migrate deploy` + `pnpm --filter @next-lane/api test:isolation` — so all three specs gate every push/PR. **P2:** `syncWikiLinks`/`syncIssueLinks` capped their per-save `[[title]]`/issue-number resolution at `MAX_OUTGOING_LINKS`/`MAX_LINKED_ISSUES` (500) — was unbounded from up to 256 KiB of page content, inconsistent with the file's own `MAX_*` read caps; the cap matches the ceiling the read endpoints (`links()`/`pageIssues()`) already enforce. **Gates:** +1 regression unit test (`search` suppresses pages + never queries them when `includePages=false`); API unit **2051/2051** (96 suites), integration **431/431** (3 suites, incl. `pat-scope-coverage` now green), `tsc --noEmit` clean. MCP: no surface change — `search_pages` already correctly used `/search/pages` under `pages:read`. **Two Pass-14 P3s also closed the same day:** (Risk 4) page-`remove()`'s child-count guard + `orphanedBacklinks` count + `page.delete` wrapped in one `$transaction` — the advisory backlink count can no longer undercount a concurrently-added backlink, and the child guard is atomic with the delete (`apps/api/src/pages/pages.service.ts` `remove()`); (Risk 3) the stale `pages:read`/`pages:write` "reserved now (no routes exist yet)" doc comment in `packages/shared/src/types.ts` corrected — both scopes are live and enforced, comment now points at `pat-scope-matrix.fixture.ts` as the authoritative coverage. - [x] (P1, M+M) **Pages — MCP tools: CRUD + version history, and graph/backlink traversal (crown jewel)** ✅ shipped 2026-07-09 (former Ready queue #4/#5) — agents read AND write the knowledge base, and — uniquely, per the founder framing — *traverse* it, over the same PAT-authenticated stdio server every other Next Lane MCP tool uses. **Territory:** `apps/mcp/src/tools/index.ts` (+`apps/mcp/src/tools/index.test.ts`, `apps/mcp/README.md`) only — the shipped backend (`092793c`/this-slice's prior commit) needed zero changes. **12 new tools, 105→117:** `list_pages` (project-scoped, compact `{id, title, parentId, archived}` refs), `get_page`, `create_page`, `move_page`, `update_page`, `delete_page` (CRUD); `list_page_versions`/`get_page_version`/`restore_page_version` (history); `get_page_graph`/`get_page_backlinks`/`get_page_links` (the traversal trio). **`list_pages` design note (a deliberate deviation from the Ready item's literal field list):** the REST surface has no flat, paginated "list pages" route — only a nested `GET .../pages/tree` (sidebar shape, no `updatedAt`) and a whole-project `GET .../pages/graph`. `list_pages` flattens the tree client-side (pre-order, `parentId` filled in from tree position) and paginates over it locally (`paginateOnly`, matching the existing `list_labels`/`list_comments` "fetch-all-then-slice" convention already used elsewhere in this file for REST routes with no native pagination) rather than adding new REST surface (out of this task's territory). Compact refs therefore omit `updatedAt` (not cheaply available); `verbose: true` hydrates every page in the **returned slice only** (bounded by `limit`, one extra `GET /pages/:id` per item — the same bounded-extra-round-trips posture `fetchNlqlFilteredIssues` already established for `list_issues` query mode) with the full object including content/timestamps/authors, rather than silently multiplying every page in the project. **`get_page` composes its own one-call orientation:** defaults to also fetching backlink count (`GET /pages/:id/backlinks`, count only) and resolving outgoing `[[wiki-links]]` (see below) into a `links: {backlinkCount, outgoing}` field, so "open a page, understand what it connects to" costs one tool call, not four; `includeLinks: false` opts out for a bare-content read. **Outgoing-link resolution (`get_page`/`get_page_links` shared `resolveOutgoingLinks` helper):** parses `page.content` for `[[Title]]`/`[[Title|Alias]]` via a **local, minimal mirror of `packages/shared/src/wikilink.ts#parseWikiLinks`** (title-extraction only) rather than importing the canonical shared parser — `@next-lane/mcp` deliberately ships with zero `workspace:*` runtime deps (see the README's "Run without cloning" `npx`-installable claim) and `@next-lane/shared` is `private: true`/unpublished, so importing it would break that guarantee; the duplication is documented in-code with an explicit "keep in sync" note. Resolves each title against the project's page titles (fetched via the cheap `/pages/tree`, not `/pages/graph`) case-insensitively, self-title excluded, first-in-tree-order wins a duplicate title (a documented approximation of the API's own oldest-page-wins tie-break, since the tree payload carries no `createdAt`) — split into `resolved: {pageId, title}[]` and `unresolvedTitles: string[]` (referenced but not yet written; explicitly documented as NOT retroactively resolving when the missing page is later created — only the next save of the LINKING page re-syncs, matching the shipped backend's actual `syncWikiLinks` semantics exactly, not an idealized one). **`get_page_graph`/`get_page_backlinks`/`get_page_links` tool descriptions were written to actively teach the traversal pattern**, not just describe the shape — `get_page_graph`: "load the graph to understand how the project's knowledge connects," flagged "CROWN-JEWEL traversal call," explains the `truncated` cap semantics (nodes AND edges both cut, always internally consistent) and points back to the per-page tools as the exact-neighbors fallback; `get_page_backlinks`: "Walk backlinks to find everything referencing this page," suggests chaining backlink walks to map a whole subject area; `get_page_links` explicitly cross-references `get_page_backlinks` (reverse direction) and `get_page_graph` (whole-project, wider than page-by-page) so an agent discovers the trio as a set, not three unrelated tools — a dedicated registry test (`'... descriptions teach the traversal pattern'`) asserts this language survives future edits, not just today's diff. **`move_page` added beyond the Ready item's exact tool enumeration for CRUD completeness** (drag-and-drop-style reorder/reparent via the already-shipped `POST /pages/:id/move`, fractional-rank computed server-side) — cheap, in-territory, and closes the gap between "MCP can create/edit/delete pages" and "MCP can also organize them," the same capability `move_issue` gives issues. **`create_page`/`update_page`/`delete_page`/`restore_page_version` (write group, `pages:write`-scoped):** partial-update semantics mirror `update_issue` (undefined fields dropped by `JSON.stringify`, explicit `null` kept — e.g. `parentId: null` detaches to top-level); `delete_page` and `move_page` descriptions surface the shipped API's precise rejection messages (400 child-count / 400 cycle) rather than a generic error. **Tests:** 19 new vitest (112→131) — registry presence, `list_pages` flatten/paginate/verbose-hydration-bounded-to-slice, `get_page` default-orientation + `includeLinks:false` skip, cursor-forwarding for `list_page_versions`, `get_page_graph` pass-through, `get_page_backlinks` pagination, `get_page_links` resolved/unresolved/self-exclusion + the zero-links-skips-the-tree-call fast path, full CRUD + `move_page` request-shape assertions, 400-message-passthrough for both `delete_page` and `move_page`, and the traversal-language assertion above. `tsc --noEmit` clean; `pnpm --filter @next-lane/mcp build` clean (fresh `dist/`, no stale `.tsbuildinfo`). `apps/mcp/README.md` tool table (all 12), scope table (`pages:read`/`pages:write` row), tool count (105→117), and a new "Read AND write the knowledge base — and traverse its graph" prose section (mirrors the existing "Ship your agent with memory" section's structure) all updated in the same commit. **Live-verified against a running API:** deferred to the next MCP-QA pass per this task's scope (no `:4000` claimed — a sibling frontend-builder and code-reviewer were active in the same worktree); unit-level request-shape/response-shape coverage above is the gate for this commit. - [x] (P0/P1, S+M) **Pages — schema + backend module (CRUD, tree-move, version history, `[[wiki-link]]` parsing + `PageLink` sync, backlinks + graph endpoints)** ✅ shipped 2026-07-09 (former Ready queue #1-#4) — the Confluence×Obsidian-hybrid knowledge base's foundation + full backend slice, landed as two commits (schema in `092793c`, backend module this commit). **Schema** (`092793c`, unchanged by this pass): `Page` (project-scoped nestable tree via a self-relation `parentId`, `onDelete: Restrict` — deliberately, see below; live `content`; fractional `rank` scoped to `(projectId, parentId)`, reusing `packages/shared/src/rank.ts` verbatim, no second ranking scheme), `PageVersion` (immutable per-save snapshot, service-assigned monotonic `versionNumber`), `PageIssueLink` (reserved for a later slice — issue cross-linking, item #6 in the Ready queue — deliberately untouched by this backend module), `PageLink` (directed page↔page wiki-link edge, unique `[sourcePageId, targetPageId]`, `onDelete: Cascade` both sides); `pages:read`/`pages:write` `PAT_SCOPES` reserved. **Backend module** (`apps/api/src/pages/**`, new `PagesModule`/`Controller`/`Service`, mirroring the `components`/`dashboards`/`agent-context` module shape): 11 routes, every one `@RequireScope`-gated (`pages:read` GETs / `pages:write` mutations) + `assertProjectRole` (VIEWER read / MEMBER+ write, the existing `getEffectiveProjectRole` chokepoint, no parallel authz path) — `POST /projects/:id/pages` (create, content capped ~256 KiB via a `Buffer.byteLength` UTF-8 `IsMaxByteLength` DTO validator mirroring `agent-context`'s), `GET /projects/:id/pages/tree` (nested `PageTreeNode[]`, ordered by rank), `GET /projects/:id/pages/graph` (below), `GET/PATCH/DELETE /pages/:id`, `POST /pages/:id/move`, `GET /pages/:id/versions` (paginated, newest-first, compact — no `content` field, cursor = base64 `versionNumber` since it's already a unique monotonic per-page key), `GET /pages/:id/versions/:n` (one full snapshot), `POST /pages/:id/versions/:n/restore`, `GET /pages/:id/backlinks`. **Two deliberate design decisions, both documented in code:** (1) **delete-with-children → explicit 400**, not a cascade-subtree-delete — `Page.parentId`'s `onDelete: Restrict` (schema-architect's own posture, see the model-level comment in `schema.prisma`) already forces this at the DB layer; the service surfaces it as a clear `BadRequestException` naming the child count rather than silently cascading or building a separate "delete subtree" endpoint — the conservative choice for a v1 document tree where losing a whole branch should never be an implicit side effect. (2) **an unresolved `[[wiki-link]]` is silently skipped for `PageLink` edges, not persisted as a "phantom" row** — the shipped `PageLink` schema has two required, non-nullable FK columns (`sourcePageId`/`targetPageId`), so there is no schema-level slot to persist a title-only unresolved reference; `packages/shared/src/wikilink.ts#parseWikiLinks` is exposed to BOTH the backend sync logic and (for the not-yet-built frontend, item #1 in the Ready queue) an editor that re-parses content client-side against the known page-title list to render the "not yet created" affordance — one parser, two consumers, no backend "phantom row" needed. **`[[wiki-link]]` parsing + sync** (`packages/shared/src/wikilink.ts`, new, exported from the package root): `parseWikiLinks(md)` extracts `[[Title]]`/`[[Title|Alias]]` references (trims whitespace, skips empty titles, resolves the innermost pair on malformed nesting); `PagesService.syncWikiLinks` (private, runs inside the SAME transaction as every content-changing create/update/restore) resolves each unique title to a page in the SAME project via a case-insensitive `OR`-of-`equals` query (self excluded from the candidate set, so a page linking its own title never creates a self-edge), diffs against the page's current outgoing `PageLink` rows, and adds/removes only the delta (`createMany`/`deleteMany`, not delete-then-recreate) — idempotent, re-saving identical content is a zero-row diff. **Version history contract** (matches the `UpdatePageDto`/`PageVersion` doc comments already shipped in `092793c`): a new version is written on CREATE always and on UPDATE only when `title` and/or `content` is part of the change — a `parentId`/`rank`-only move or an `archived` toggle does NOT write a version, keeping tree-reorganization and content-history orthogonal; `restoreVersion` always writes a NEW version copying the target's content forward, never mutates or truncates history. **Tree move** (`POST /pages/:id/move`, `MovePageDto {parentId?, beforeId?, afterId?}`): computes the new rank via `rankBetween` inside a transaction (falls back to a one-time sibling rebalance, mirroring `IssuesService.rebalanceAndPlace`, only when neighbor ranks are exhausted — never renumbers on the normal path); cycle rejection (`assertNoCycle`, walks the `parentId` chain from the proposed new parent up to the root, 400s if it ever reaches the page being moved, including the trivial "become your own parent" case) runs inside the SAME transaction as the write, matching the TOCTOU-safety posture `IssuesService.move`'s neighbor-rank read+write already uses in this codebase (documented as a deliberate scope call, not a `SELECT ... FOR UPDATE` guarantee). **`PATCH /pages/:id`** additionally accepts a direct `parentId`/`rank` assignment (cycle-checked the same way) for a caller that already computed its own fractional rank — the move endpoint is for the neighbor-relative drag-and-drop UX, the PATCH field is for a caller (agent or already-rank-aware UI) that wants to set an exact value. **Graph endpoint** (`GET /projects/:id/pages/graph` → `PageGraphDto {nodes, edges, truncated}`, `truncated` a new field added to the already-shipped shared type): capped at `MAX_GRAPH_NODES` = 1000 (fetch-one-extra-to-detect-truncation, the same pattern as `ROADMAP_EPICS_CAP`/`PUBLIC_BOARD_ISSUES_CAP`), edges filtered to only those between two retained nodes so a truncated graph is never internally inconsistent (no dangling edge to a cut node); strictly project-scoped (never crosses projects, both endpoints of every edge independently verified). **Tests:** 32 new API unit tests (`pages.service.spec.ts`, backed by a small in-memory fake Prisma client rather than per-call jest mocks — the tree/version/wiki-link reconciliation logic needed a working little "database" to exercise realistically) covering CRUD, tree-move + cycle rejection (self-parent + grandparent-under-own-descendant), version-write-on-content-change vs. no-write-on-parentId/rank/archived-only change, restore-writes-new-version-never-mutates-history, wiki-link parse+sync add/remove edges + self-link exclusion + unresolved-link-no-error, backlinks, and graph shape + the `MAX_GRAPH_NODES` truncation case (2002→2034 tests, 95→96 suites); 13 new shared vitest for `parseWikiLinks` (171→184); 11 new `pat-scope-matrix.fixture.ts` rows (all 11 routes) — `pat-scope-coverage.integration.spec.ts` (the drift guard) and `pat-scope-rollout.integration.spec.ts` (401→423, 22 new DENY/ALLOW assertions) both green; 11 new tenant-isolation-matrix rows (real cross-tenant HTTP attempts against a live second project's page/version, all BLOCKED — 401→423 in the same integration count, `tenant-isolation.integration.spec.ts`'s own "126 endpoints, 0 issues" summary line covers them). `tsc --noEmit` clean api/shared; `pnpm --filter @next-lane/api build` clean. **Live end-to-end verification** (real running API + Postgres, not just unit tests): created two pages, saved one with `[[link]] to the other, `GET /projects/:id/pages/graph` showed the resulting edge; restored an old version and confirmed a brand-new version number was written (history untouched); a childful page's DELETE returned the documented 400. **MCP: deliberately NOT exposed in this slice** — `docs/BACKLOG.md`'s own Ready queue sequences MCP CRUD tools (item #4) and MCP graph/backlink traversal tools (item #5, "the crown jewel") as separate, later items depending on this backend module; exposing them here would pre-empt that explicitly-planned slice rather than skip it. **Frontend, MCP tools, and issue↔page cross-linking are separate, not-yet-built Ready-queue items** (see the renumbered #1-#7 above) — this entry covers the backend module only. - [x] (P2, M) **SSO/OIDC — Phase 2: SAML + multi-provider + JIT provisioning** ✅ shipped 2026-07-06 (Ready queue #1) — closes the last "Admin controls" Better-than-Jira lever (VISION.md § Better-than-Jira gaps #2). **Library:** `@node-saml/node-saml` 5.1.0 (actively-maintained fork of the `passport-saml` lineage), used directly (not via its Passport strategy wrapper) — consistent with the existing architecture, which already talks to `openid-client` directly for OIDC, not through Passport. **Schema (additive, Phase-1 `OidcConfig` singleton left entirely unmigrated):** new `SsoProvider` table (migration `20260706130000_add_sso_providers`) — one row per ADDITIONAL provider (OIDC or SAML), `type`-discriminated with mutually-exclusive nullable OIDC (`issuerUrl`/`clientId`/`clientSecretEncrypted`) and SAML (`samlEntryPoint`/`samlIdpIssuer`/`samlIdpCertificate`/`samlSpEntityId`) column groups, plus `jitDefaultWorkspaceId`/`jitDefaultRole` (default `VIEWER`) on BOTH this table and two new nullable columns added to the legacy `OidcConfig` row — an existing single-OIDC-provider deployment keeps working completely unmigrated (the new columns default to `null`/`VIEWER`, i.e. Phase-1 behavior unchanged) while still gaining opt-in JIT provisioning for its own provider if the admin sets one. **JIT provisioning semantics (conservative by design, shared `sso-jit-provisioning.util.ts` used identically by the legacy `OidcService` and the new `SsoService`):** ONLY fires for a BRAND NEW user (the very first successful SSO login that creates their `User` row) — an already-existing user's memberships are never touched by a later SSO login regardless of a provider's JIT config; `jitDefaultWorkspaceId: null` (the default) means JIT is off — the account is created so they can authenticate but lands with zero memberships until an existing member invites them; when set, a `Membership` is auto-created at `jitDefaultRole` (default `VIEWER` — admin must explicitly opt into a higher default). **Runtime (`apps/api/src/auth/sso/`, new `SsoModule`, additive alongside the untouched `OidcModule`):** `GET /auth/sso/:slug/login` dispatches by provider type — OIDC gets the same PKCE/state-cookie/discovery shape as the legacy flow (parameterized per-row, client cached per-slug keyed by an issuer+clientId+secret fingerprint) via a new shared `discoverOidcClient` util extracted from (but not replacing) `OidcService`'s own inline discovery; SAML redirects via `SAML.getAuthorizeUrlAsync` (HTTP-Redirect binding, RelayState carries the slug). `GET /auth/sso/:slug/callback` is OIDC-only (query-param authorization-code exchange); `POST /auth/sso/:slug/callback` is the SAML ACS endpoint (form POST, `SamlCallbackDto`) — each 404s for the wrong provider type. **SAML assertion validation strictness (every claim proven live against the real library, not mocked — see Tests below):** `wantAssertionsSigned: true` hardcoded, never admin-configurable off — an unsigned or tampered assertion is rejected outright; `wantAuthnResponseSigned: false` explicitly (the assertion signature alone is the trust boundary — requiring the outer `<Response>` envelope to ALSO be signed would reject interoperable IdPs like Okta/Azure AD/ADFS that by default sign only the assertion); `audience` always enforced (never `false`) against the provider's `samlSpEntityId` (defaults to a computed `${WEB_BASE_URL}/api/auth/sso/{slug}` when unset) — rejects assertions minted for a different SP; `validateInResponseTo: ifPresent` with a new `SamlCacheProviderFactory` (Redis-backed when `REDIS_URL` is set — reuses the existing optional `RedisModule`, correctly shared/restart-safe across replicas — else an in-memory fallback, correct for the common single-instance self-host) enforces single-use replay protection with a 5-minute window (tighter than node-saml's own 8h default, matching the legacy OIDC state token's order of magnitude); `<Conditions NotBefore/NotOnOrAfter>` timestamps always checked by the library regardless of config. **Documented residual gap:** `@node-saml/node-saml` does not independently re-verify `SubjectConfirmationData/@Recipient` against our own ACS URL — mitigated in practice by the signed-assertion + audience-restriction + single-use-InResponseTo combination already enforced, which together prevent cross-SP replay for any IdP that signs assertions (required here); a full custom Recipient check would mean hand-parsing assertion XML ourselves instead of trusting the audited library path. **Admin API** (`apps/api/src/admin-settings/sso-providers.service.ts`/`.controller.ts` — new `GET/POST/PATCH/DELETE /admin/sso-providers` on the existing `AdminSettingsController`, instance-admin gated, `admin:read`/`admin:write` scoped): slug auto-generated from label (or admin-supplied, collision-retried like `WorkspacesService.uniqueSlug`) and immutable after creation (the runtime route + OIDC discovery cache key off it); secrets/certificates write-only (`hasClientSecret`/`hasSamlIdpCertificate` booleans only, raw values never echoed by any GET); SAML certificate sanity-checked for PEM markers at write time (a full X.509 parse happens on first real login attempt). `GET /auth/providers` (the public login-page capability probe) now also returns every currently-ENABLED multi-provider's `{slug, type, label}` alongside the unchanged legacy `oidc` field. **Frontend:** `AdminSsoSettingsPage.tsx` gains a JIT workspace/role picker on the legacy form plus a new "Additional providers" section (list + inline create/edit forms, type radio OIDC/SAML, per-type fields, its own JIT picker, enable toggle, delete-with-confirm) — reuses the existing `Field`/`Input`/`Select`/`Textarea`/`Switch`/`Button` design-system primitives, no new components; `LoginPage.tsx` renders one "Continue with `<label>`" button per enabled multi-provider alongside the legacy button. **Tests:** 68 new API unit tests (`oidc.service.spec.ts` +3 for the legacy provider's own new JIT path, `admin-settings.service.spec.ts`/`oidc-config.service.spec.ts` +9 for the legacy JIT config validation, `sso-providers.service.spec.ts` 19 new, `sso-jit-provisioning.util.spec.ts` 4 new, `sso.service.spec.ts` 13 new — OIDC dispatch mocked like `oidc.service.spec.ts`, `sso.controller.spec.ts` 9 new) — 1931→1999 tests, 90→95 suites, all green. **`saml.service.spec.ts` (10 tests) is a full end-to-end proof against the REAL, unmocked `@node-saml/node-saml` library** — zero network, zero real IdP: a self-signed X.509 keypair (`selfsigned`, pure-JS, no OpenSSL binary dependency) stands in for the IdP, and `xml-crypto` (already a transitive dependency, added as an explicit devDependency) produces a REAL enveloped XML-DSig signature over a hand-built SAML `<Assertion>` (`__fixtures__/saml-response.fixture.ts`); covers accept-valid, forged-keypair rejection, post-signature-tamper rejection (digest mismatch), unsigned-response rejection, audience-confusion rejection, expired-assertion rejection, unknown-InResponseTo rejection, and replay-of-consumed-InResponseTo rejection — every strictness claim above is proven, not asserted. Also 4 new `pat-scope-matrix.fixture.ts` rows (`/admin/sso-providers` GET/POST/PATCH/DELETE) + a new `sso` exemption category in `pat-scope-coverage.integration.spec.ts` for the three `@Public()` runtime routes (`SsoController#login`/`oidcCallback`/`samlCallback`) — full integration suite still green (401 tests, 3 suites). **Live end-to-end verification** (not just unit tests): registered a user, promoted to instance admin, created a workspace, created a SAML provider via the real HTTP API with a locally-generated self-signed cert, hit the real `GET /auth/sso/:slug/login` redirect, extracted the real AuthnRequest ID from the inflated `SAMLRequest`, built and POSTed a real signed assertion to the real ACS endpoint — got redirected to `/login/sso-complete#token=...` with a valid session JWT, confirmed via `GET /auth/me` the user was created and via a DB query that the `Membership` row was auto-created at the configured JIT role; replaying the identical POST was confirmed rejected (`ssoError=...` redirect). `tsc --noEmit` clean across api/web/shared/mcp; `pnpm --filter @next-lane/api build` clean; `scripts/smoke-config-parity.sh` passes unchanged (no new env vars — SAML config is entirely DB/admin-UI-driven, secrets reuse the existing `OIDC_CONFIG_ENCRYPTION_KEY` derivation). **MCP:** deliberately **not exposed**, same reasoning as Phase 1 (`apps/mcp/README.md`'s "Not exposed over MCP" table, extended) — every write carries a client secret or IdP signing certificate, and the runtime login/callback routes are unauthenticated browser redirects with no agent-appropriate shape (an agent can't "click through" an IdP's own login form). The route↔scope matrix was extracted from `pat-scope-rollout.integration.spec.ts` into a shared `pat-scope-matrix.fixture.ts` (one exported `MATRIX` constant + `normalizeRoutePattern` helper, imported by both specs — not duplicated, per the acceptance criteria). Placed as an integration spec (not a DB-free unit spec): booting `AppModule` triggers `PrismaService#onModuleInit`'s `$connect()`, so it needs the same real Postgres the sibling integration specs use, even though it issues zero queries itself — runs in the same `jest.integration.config.js` CI lane. **Writing the guard caught real, pre-existing drift**, fixed in the same commit: `github.controller.ts`/`gitlab.controller.ts` had been `@RequireScope`-gated since before the Hardening Night rollout (`4aec12a`) but were never added to the matrix (12 rows added covering both controllers' get/upsert/remove/automation/links/live-status routes); `GET /projects/:id/activity` (`ProjectsController#activity`) was scoped `projects:read` but missing from the matrix (1 row added); `GET /workspaces/:id/logo` (`WorkspacesController#serveLogo`, a `@Public()` branding-asset stream) had no exemption entry (added, category `public`). Matrix grew 143→190 rows; `pat-scope-rollout.integration.spec.ts`'s DENY/ALLOW behavioral tests now data-drive all 190 (380 assertions, up from 286); full integration suite 307→393 tests, all green; API unit suite unchanged at 1931/90 suites (the new specs are `*.integration.spec.ts`, outside that lane); `tsc --noEmit` clean. **Both-ways proof:** temporarily removed `@RequireScope('issues:write')` from `IssuesController#create` with no exemption — the spec failed, naming the exact route (`POST /issues (IssuesController#create)`) plus the now-orphaned matrix row; reverted, spec green again. Validated in an isolated `git worktree` (checked out at the pre-change commit, private `@prisma/client` + `@next-lane/shared` symlink chain) rather than the shared working tree, since a sibling agent was concurrently mid-edit on `admin-settings`/`auth` files in the same checkout — avoids false-negative/false-positive gate results from someone else's in-flight, not-yet-compiling code. - [x] (P2, S) **GitLab auto-transition e2e parity** ✅ shipped 2026-07-06 (Ready queue #3, promoted from P3) — new `apps/web/e2e/gitlab-auto-transition.spec.ts` mirrors `pr-auto-transition.spec.ts`'s GitHub coverage at the same depth, GitLab semantics: enables `gitlab-auto-transition-toggle` targeting "Done" via the Settings UI (persists across reload); POSTs a locally-crafted `Merge Request Hook` webhook carrying the real `X-Gitlab-Token` shared secret the API returned on connect (no HMAC — GitLab's webhook auth model, unlike GitHub's `X-Hub-Signature-256`) with `object_attributes.state: 'opened'` then `'merged'` (GitLab's literal merge signal, vs. GitHub's `merged: true` boolean — see `gitlab.service.ts#handleMergeRequestEvent`); asserts the board card's `issue-pr-badge`/`data-pr-state` badge — shared across both providers via `issue.mapper.ts`'s `prLinkSummary` aggregation, so no new badge testid was needed — flips open→merged; the linked issue's real `statusId` transitions to "Done" via a REST re-fetch (not just a UI observation); the MR link (`!<iid>`, merged state) renders in the issue drawer's `gitlab-links-section`; a disabled-by-default regression case (webhook links the MR but never moves the issue, verified both via the REST `autoTransitionOnMerge: false` default and an unchanged `statusId`); and a mobile (390px) no-horizontal-overflow case for the badge. 6 new e2e cases; zero GitHub/GitLab egress; zero app-code changes (the feature already shipped for both providers in earlier commits — this closes a regression-guard gap only). Verified in an isolated `git worktree` (own Postgres DB, own API :4000 / web-preview :3000 ports, `RATE_LIMIT_DISABLED=true`/`WEBHOOK_ALLOW_PRIVATE=true`) to avoid colliding with a concurrently-running sibling QA instance on other ports: 6/6 green on both the `chromium-desktop` and `mobile-chrome` Playwright projects; re-ran alongside the existing `gitlab-integration.spec.ts` (7 cases) and `pr-auto-transition.spec.ts` (3 cases) for regression — 22/22 green, no interference between the two providers' specs. - [x] (P1, S–M) **Config-parity CI smoke test: `docker-compose.yml` vs `.env.example`** ✅ shipped 2026-07-06 — closes the *class* behind the same-day compose env-passthrough fix (`3c22f21`, audit pass 13 Risk 1: SMTP/CORS/etc. never reached the container): a future documented env var that isn't forwarded (or a forwarded var that isn't documented) now fails CI instead of shipping silently broken. New `scripts/smoke-config-parity.sh` (mirrors the `scripts/smoke-web-csp.sh` precedent; bash + docker + jq only, no build/containers — `docker compose config` just parses + interpolates): parses `.env.example` for both active (`VAR=value`) and commented-optional (`# VAR=value`) declarations, anchored at line-start so prose mentions of a var name (`OIDC_ISSUER_URL — the provider's...`) are never mistaken for a declaration; renders `docker compose config --format json` with a **distinct sentinel value per documented var** (not just "is the key present" — proves real `${VAR}` passthrough, not a hardcoded/default value coincidentally matching); asserts every documented var (minus a reviewed, one-reason-each `IGNORE_NOT_FORWARDED` list — `DATABASE_URL`/`REDIS_URL`/`API_PORT` composed or hardcoded inline; `POSTGRES_*`/`REDIS_PORT`/`WEB_PORT` belong to other services' port mappings; `VITE_API_URL` is a web build ARG; `UPLOADS_DIR` intentionally container-internal; `RESET_BASE_URL` a dead fallback superseded by `WEB_BASE_URL`; `OTEL_*` a documented future/stub with no reading code yet) round-trips its sentinel into the rendered `api` service environment. **Reverse direction too:** every `${VAR}` the api service's own compose stanza reads must be documented in `.env.example` (empty `IGNORE_UNDOCUMENTED` list today) — this direction caught FIVE real, live documentation gaps at HEAD (`AUTO_SEED`, `WEB_BASE_URL`, `GITLAB_TOKEN_ENCRYPTION_KEY`, `GITLAB_WEBHOOK_BASE_URL`, `OIDC_CONFIG_ENCRYPTION_KEY` — all genuinely forwarded/consumed by code but absent from `.env.example`), fixed in `.env.example` in the same commit (closing the standalone `GITLAB_TOKEN_ENCRYPTION_KEY` P3 backlog item — Pass 13 Risk 5 — as a side effect) so the check is green, not silenced. Host-port vars (`POSTGRES_PORT`/`REDIS_PORT`/`API_PORT`/`WEB_PORT`) get a valid numeric sentinel instead of an alnum string (compose validates `ports:` values; they're ignore-listed for the forward check anyway). Wired into CI as a new early step in `.github/workflows/ci.yml`'s existing `docker-build` job ("Production image build (compose)" — the docker-compose build guard), before the slower image build so a config-parity break fails fast. Both-ways-proven: green at HEAD (29 non-ignored vars round-tripped, 33 forwarded vars all documented); locally reverting `SMTP_HOST`'s passthrough line in `docker-compose.yml` reproduced the exact original bug class and the script failed with `MISSING: .env.example documents "SMTP_HOST" but it does not appear at all in the rendered api service environment` — reverted after confirming. `shellcheck`-clean. [engineering-auditor Pass 13 Ideation #2]
