  affected on any mutation error) plus `pb-[calc(1rem+env(safe-area-inset-
  bottom))]` for notched-phone safe area; paired with capping `ui/Modal.tsx`'s
  panel at `max-h-[calc(100dvh-4rem)]` with a sticky header/footer + scrollable
  body so a tall form's footer action row can't newly collide with the
  bottom-pinned toast either (the review's flagged residual risk). **(2)
  "Merged" PR/MR badge dark-mode break** — `purple` added to
  `tailwind.config.js`'s CSS-var-backed `varScale()` pipeline (mirroring
  emerald/red/amber/etc.) with `:root`/`.dark` values in `index.css` derived
  via the same `applyBrandColor.ts` mix-toward-canvas/mix-toward-paper method
  as every other scale — the existing `bg-purple-50 text-purple-700
  ring-purple-200` classes in `IssueCard.tsx`, `GithubLinksSection.tsx`,
  `GitlabLinksSection.tsx`, `WorkspaceAuditLogPage.tsx`, and
  `WorkspaceMembersPage.tsx` all inherit dark-mode-legible values with ZERO
  per-component changes (fix-once-at-the-token-layer, per the review's
  prescription); contrast verified ≥4.5:1 in both modes (light 6.5:1/5.9:1,
  dark 5.1:1/4.7:1 for the 50/700 and 100/700 pairs respectively).
  **(3) Dashboard selection was shadow state** — `DashboardsPage.tsx`'s
  `selectedId` moved from local `useState` to the `?dashboard=<id>` URL
  search param, mirroring `BoardPage`'s existing URL-as-source-of-truth
  filter pattern; reload/deep-link/share now land on the same dashboard,
  with the 6447e76 synchronous-append create fix (new dashboard stays
  selected) preserved. **(4) P2 quick wins** — `GadgetCard`'s drag handle
  grew a `min-h-10 min-w-10` (≈40px) touch target on mobile (icon stays
  visually small, resets at `sm:`+ for pointer precision); `GithubLinksSection`
  /`GitlabLinksSection`'s live PR/CI and MR/pipeline status polls now render a
  small `Spinner` while `liveQuery.isLoading` instead of nothing, matching the
  drawer's established loading vocabulary (`ui/States`' `Spinner` extended to
  forward arbitrary HTML attributes for the new `data-testid` hooks). Gates:
  `tsc --noEmit` + web build clean; 142 targeted e2e green desktop+mobile
  (`dashboards.spec.ts`, `dashboards-phase2.spec.ts`, `pr-auto-transition.spec.ts`,
  `board.spec.ts`, `toast.spec.ts`, `personal-board.spec.ts`,
  `pulse-dashboard.spec.ts`, `webhooks-ui.spec.ts`, `components.spec.ts`,
  `csv-import.spec.ts`, `issue-templates.spec.ts`, `github-integration.spec.ts`,
  `gitlab-integration.spec.ts`, `issue-detail.spec.ts`, `issue-links.spec.ts`);
  all `data-testid`/`role`/`aria-label` hooks preserved (no e2e selector
  changes needed). [frontend-qa 2026-07-06 UI review, Hardening Night task #93]

- [x] (P2, M) **DNS-rebinding TOCTOU closed in the shared SSRF guard
  (2026-07-06, Hardening Night, audit pass 13 Risk 3)** — the old
  `resolveAndCheckBlocked` + raw `fetch()` pattern resolved DNS once to vet a
  target then let `fetch()` re-resolve DNS itself; a short-TTL attacker
  nameserver could answer the vetting query with a public IP and the real
  connection query with `169.254.169.254`/`127.0.0.1`/an internal host.
  New shared `apps/api/src/common/ssrf-safe-fetch.ts`: `ssrfSafeFetch()`
  resolves DNS exactly once and PINS the actual TCP/TLS connection to that
  one vetted address via a custom undici `Agent` `connect.lookup` (added
  `undici` as a direct `apps/api` dependency) that ignores whatever hostname
  it's asked to resolve — there is no second real DNS query left for a
  rebinding nameserver to answer differently; Host/SNI still use the real
  hostname so TLS cert validation is unaffected. All three outbound-call
  families now share this one path: webhook delivery (`webhooks.service.ts`,
  re-vetted fresh per retry attempt), `GithubClient`, `GitlabClient`
  (self-hosted GitLab's admin-supplied `baseUrl` was the primary risk).
  `redirect: 'manual'` semantics preserved; `WEBHOOK_ALLOW_PRIVATE=true`
  still bypasses the guard entirely for self-hosters. 15 new unit tests
  including a real-socket rebind simulation (mocked `dns.promises.lookup`
  answers public-then-private; the pinned request still hits the
  first-vetted address and `dns.promises.lookup` is proven called exactly
  once) plus direct `buildPinnedLookup` unit tests. All 71 pre-existing
  webhook/GitHub/GitLab SSRF tests pass unmodified. API unit 1823/1823,
  integration 293/293, `tsc --noEmit` clean. [orchestrator, audit pass 13
  Risk 3]

- [x] (P2, S) **`multer` DoS CVEs closed via root `pnpm.overrides`
  (2026-07-06, Hardening Night, audit pass 13 Risk 4)** — `apps/api`'s own
  direct `multer: "^2.2.0"` dependency was already patched, but
  `@nestjs/platform-express`'s `FileInterceptor` (used by every real upload
  endpoint — workspace logo, CSV import, issue attachments) bundled its own
  transitive `multer@2.0.2`, carrying 3 high + 1 moderate DoS advisories.
  Root `package.json` `pnpm.overrides` now forces `multer: "^2.2.0"` (and,
  since it was cheap and still major-version-4, `lodash: "^4.18.0"` for the
  one other HIGH — code injection via `_.template` — transitively pulled in
  by `@nestjs/swagger`). `pnpm audit --prod`: 5 high → **0 high** (10 → 7
  moderate; the remaining moderates — `js-yaml`, `qs`, `@nestjs/core`,
  `file-type` — are documented as deferred, not silently dropped: `qs`/
  `js-yaml` are minor-version-safe candidates for a follow-up pass,
  `@nestjs/core`'s fix needs a Nest v10→v11 major bump, and `file-type`'s
  fix needs v20+ (ESM-only) which the audit already flagged as its own
  ticket — none blindly bumped). Verified `pnpm why multer`/`pnpm why
  lodash` resolve every path to the patched version; live-verified with a
  REAL multipart `curl` round-trip against a running API instance (isolated
  port, so as not to disturb the concurrent QA agent's server on :4000) —
  workspace logo upload/download and issue attachment upload/download both
  byte-for-byte matched the uploaded file through the patched
  `FileInterceptor`/multer/`file-type`-sniff path. Attachment/workspace
  upload unit suites (241 tests) unaffected. [orchestrator, audit pass 13
  Risk 4]

- [x] (P3, S) **Issue-create `$transaction` timeout hardened against pool
  contention (2026-07-06, Hardening Night, qa-tester 2026-07-06 P3)** —
  `POST /issues` 500'd twice under 2-worker e2e parallelism in a
  resource-constrained sandbox ("Transaction already closed... timeout 5000
  ms" — Prisma's defaults). `IssuesService.createInner`'s default-status
  resolution (`status.findFirst` ×≤2, only when the caller omits `statusId`)
  moved OUT of the transaction onto the plain `prisma` client — a pure read
  with no dependency on the atomic write, trimming round-trips off the
  transaction's held-connection time — and the transaction now passes
  explicit `{ timeout: 12_000, maxWait: 12_000 }` (vs. Prisma's 5000ms/2000ms
  defaults), justified in a code comment tied to the QA finding. No
  correctness change: issueSeq increment, rank lookup, the insert, and its
  activity-log row still commit atomically together. 351/351 issue unit
  tests pass (2 test-double fixtures updated to mock `prisma.status.findFirst`
  at the top level, matching the moved call site). [orchestrator, qa-tester
  P3 2026-07-06]

- [x] (P1, S) **docker-compose env passthrough fix (2026-07-06, Hardening
  Night)** — audit pass 13 finding 1: compose forwarded only 7 of ~25
  documented operator vars; SMTP_* never reached the container so
  password-reset email was dead in every stock self-hosted deploy;
  CORS_ORIGINS/encryption keys/OIDC_*/THROTTLE_*/WEBHOOK_ALLOW_PRIVATE/
  MAX_FILE_BYTES/LOG_LEVEL equally inert; AUTO_SEED hardcoded. All forwarded
  now with code-matching defaults for `??`-consumed vars (empty string would
  override `??`; truthiness/`Number()||` vars pass through empty safely —
  each consumption pattern verified in code before choosing the form).
  Proven via `docker compose config` resolution. [orchestrator, audit pass 13
  finding 1]

- [x] (P1, S) **Activity logging for sprint/parent/component/label mutations
  (2026-07-03)** — MCP-QA pass 2's top finding fixed same-day: these
  mutations produced ZERO ActivityLog rows, so `list_project_activity` and
  agent-context `staleness` were blind to them (a bulk sprint re-scope of 6 +
  atomic re-parent of 14 left the feed empty and staleness unchanged while a
  single comment bumped it). Fix at the write path so both consumers inherit:
  `prepareUpdate` now logs `sprint`/`parent`/`component` field changes
  (single-issue AND bulk paths share it); label attach/detach logs a `label`
  activity in all three paths (LabelsService add/remove + bulk `addLabelIds`
  classic AND atomic — atomic keeps rows inside the shared transaction) with
  no-op-safe semantics (repeat attach / detach-of-unattached logs nothing).
  Live-verified: PATCH parentId wrote a `field:'parent'` ActivityLog row.
  9 new unit tests; affected suites 365/365; API total 1790. Web ActivityPanel
  renders the new fields via its generic fallback (id-resolution for
  sprint/label names stays a filed P2). [orchestrator, from mcp-consumer-qa
  pass 2 be6f7d3]

- [x] (P1, S) **Idempotency claim-first hardening (2026-07-03)** — code-review
  follow-up on AX Round 2 (all 4 findings fixed same-day): `withIdempotency`
  rewritten claim-first (pending row inserted before the mutation; unique
  constraint elects one executor; concurrent duplicate polls for the winner's
  response — closes the client-timeout-retry duplicate the old
  check-then-write TOCTOU allowed); failed attempts release the claim instead
  of poisoning retries; post-commit `notifyAssignment` guarded in
  create/finishUpdate (a notify failure no longer 500s a committed write);
  reused key + different payload → 409 via stored request hash;
  `bulkUpdateAtomic`'s post-commit fan-out loop per-item guarded. Migration
  `20260703083000_idempotency_claim_first` (responseBody nullable +
  requestHash). Live-verified: replay same-id, 409 on payload mismatch,
  dryRun no-write, cross-project 400s. API 1731/1731. [orchestrator]

- [x] (P1, M) **Agent Experience Round 2 — "production-grade for AI-agent-driven PM" batch — SHIPPED 2026-07-03** (founder-relayed field report #2). Per-criterion:
  1. **Cross-project write validation** — fixed the confirmed-live P1: `create()` now runs the same `assertSameProject` guard as `update()`/`move()` for statusId/sprintId/parentId (was only checking componentId on create). Already-safe relations verified and left unchanged: `update()` (statusId/sprintId/parentId/componentId), `move()` (statusId + beforeId/afterId neighbor), `LabelsService.addToIssue`/`removeFromIssue` (single-issue label attach), `VersionsService.setIssueVersions` (bulk-replace) — all already validated their referenced ids belong to the same project. One additional real gap found and fixed: `bulkUpdate`'s `addLabelIds` path (`attachLabel`) had NO project-scope check at all — new `assertLabelsInProject` helper closes it in both the classic per-issue path and the new atomic path. 19 new unit tests (create cross-project ×3 fields + accept-same-project, bulk label cross-project ×2).
  2. **Idempotency keys** — additive `IdempotencyRecord` model (migration `20260703070000_add_idempotency_records`, numbered after the newest existing folder), `key`+`userId`+`endpoint` unique, ~24h window, opportunistic `deleteMany` cleanup on write. `common/idempotency.util.ts` (`withIdempotency`) wraps `IssuesService.create` and `CommentsService.create` end-to-end (optional `idempotencyKey` DTO field); a retry with the same key replays the ORIGINAL response, no duplicate — live-verified (same issue id + same comment id on retry, only one row created each). MCP `create_issue`/`add_comment` accept `idempotencyKey`; descriptions tell agents to pass one on retries. 16 new unit tests (util + wiring).
  3. **Bulk parenting / transactional** — `parentId` added to `BulkIssueChangesDto` (cross-project guard reused from `prepareUpdate`'s `assertSameProject`). `update()` refactored into `prepareUpdate`/`writeIssueUpdate`/`finishUpdate` phases so `bulkUpdateAtomic` can validate every issue in a batch first (zero writes on any failure) then write all of them inside ONE shared `$transaction` (all-or-nothing) before firing side effects once per issue. Rollback mid-transaction reports every id in the batch as failed. MCP `bulk_update_issues` gained `parentId`/`atomic`/`dryRun` params (still a compact `{updated, failed, atomic?, dryRun?, wouldUpdate?}` envelope). Live-verified: one atomic call parented 30 tickets under an epic (`{"updated":30,"failed":[]}`).
  4. **Dry-run/preview** — `dryRun: true` (with or without `atomic`) validates every id independently via the same `prepareUpdate` path and returns `{updated: 0, wouldUpdate, failed}` — zero writes, live-verified (priority unchanged after a `dryRun` bulk call).
  5. **Comment edit/delete over MCP** — REST (`PATCH`/`DELETE /comments/:id`) already existed but was author-only; upgraded to author-or-effective-project-ADMIN (mirrors `WorkLogsService`'s gating exactly, via `getEffectiveProjectRole`). New MCP `update_comment`/`delete_comment` tools.
  6. **Change feed for agents** — new `GET /projects/:id/activity` (additive, VIEWER+, `projects:read` PAT scope): k-way-merges `ActivityLog` + `Comment` + `WorkLog` rows project-wide into one chronological, cursor-paginated feed (`since`/`cursor`, ascending order), with a per-item compact `summary`. MCP `list_project_activity` tool (92→97 tools). True push subscriptions remain a later item.
  7. **Wrong-project guard strictness** — `create_issue`'s MCP description now uses MUST-pass language for `expectedProjectKey`; server `instructions` (`apps/mcp/src/index.ts`) and `skills/project-context/SKILL.md` both gained the mandate + idempotencyKey + list_project_activity guidance; new optional `NEXT_LANE_MCP_STRICT_PROJECT_KEY` env var makes omitting it a hard error.
  Folded in from MCP-QA pass 1: `AgentContextService.computeStaleness` now also counts `Comment`/`WorkLog` rows newer than the doc (was ActivityLog+AuditEvent only — a project with an active comment thread and zero field changes looked falsely fresh); `GET /users` gained a server-side case-insensitive `q` name/email substring filter (`list_users` MCP param); new MCP `create_project`/`create_workspace` tools wrapping the existing `POST /projects`/`POST /workspaces` REST endpoints, echoing `id`/`key`/`slug` first in the response.
  Gates: api build + tsc clean; 1727/1727 API jest green (was 1683, +44); tenant-isolation 103/103 BLOCKED (1 new row: GET activity feed for project A); shared vitest 146/146 (types-only addition, untouched); mcp build + 97/97 vitest green (was 84, +13); apps/mcp/README.md updated (92→97 tools, new tables, env var, strict-mode note). [founder field report #2]

_SSO Phase 2 security-review follow-ups (2026-07-06, from the 5e0fe6c review; the must-fix InResponseTo replay bypass + the cheap hardening items were fixed same-day — these are the deferred deeper ones):_
- [ ] (P2, M) **SAML existing-user account-linking needs a verification signal** — `SsoService.findOrProvisionUser` logs an existing user in on bare `email` match with no linkage/consent check; the OIDC path at least rejects `email_verified === false`, SAML has no analogue. A compromised/misconfigured admin-added IdP asserting an arbitrary email could take over a matching local/OIDC account. Options: require a matching IdP verified-email attribute where available, an explicit admin "trust this IdP for account linking" flag, or a first-link confirmation step. Document the tradeoff prominently in `AdminSsoSettingsPage` regardless. [security-review 5e0fe6c should-fix #2]
- [ ] (P3, S) **`jitDefaultRole: ADMIN` UI confirmation** — an instance admin can configure a provider so every brand-new SSO identity is auto-promoted to workspace ADMIN with no invite (by design, single admin action, but silent). Add a confirmation step in the admin UI when ADMIN is selected as the JIT default. [security-review 5e0fe6c nit #7]
- [ ] (P3, XS) **`SsoProvider.samlWantAssertionsSigned` is dead code** — the column is persisted (doc-commented "explicit and reviewable per-row") but never read; the real invariant is hardcoded `wantAssertionsSigned: true`. Either wire it into `SamlProviderConfig`/`SamlService` as a defensive read or drop the column so a "looks configurable" security boolean doesn't get carelessly wired to a weakenable value later. [security-review 5e0fe6c nit #4]

_Hardening Night close-out ingest (2026-07-06):_

**Promoted to Ready 2026-07-06** (this pass — see § Ready item 3): GitLab auto-transition e2e parity, bumped P3→P2 to reflect that it's now the top of this queue's fast-follow work, not deferred hardening. Kept out of this list to avoid duplication.

- [ ] (P3, S) **`list_project_activity`: malformed `cursor` should 400, not silently restart from history start** — MCP-QA pass 2 finding 6: a garbage `cursor` value is silently ignored and returns the full history from the beginning (same as omitting it entirely) — a truncated cursor in an agent's polling loop restarts the walk with no signal, risking double-processing of already-seen items. `since` already 400s on a malformed value; `cursor` should too. [MCP-QA pass 2, finding 6]
- [ ] (P3, S) **`create_dashboard_gadget` accepts an unresolvable NLQL name at create time** — MCP-QA spot-check 2026-07-06 (`docs/MCP-QA.md`, "for the groomer"): a gadget query referencing an unresolvable user/sprint name (e.g. a typo) is accepted with a 200 at create time; the fail-loud 400 only surfaces later, on the next `get_dashboard_data` read. Defensible as designed (the name may come to exist by read time; per-gadget read-time flagging is the containment boundary), but a create-time warning would let the agent fix the typo at the cheapest possible moment. Reuse the existing `resolveQueryNames`/`queryReferencesMe` validate-layer helpers (`packages/shared/src/nlql/validate.ts`) as a non-blocking warning in the `create_dashboard_gadget`/`update_dashboard_gadget` response, not a hard 400 (a gadget referencing a not-yet-created user/sprint is a legitimate use case). [MCP-QA spot-check 2026-07-06]
- [ ] (P3, S) **Normalize id-param naming across dashboard MCP tools** — MCP-QA spot-check 2026-07-06: `get_dashboard_data` takes `dashboardId` while `delete_dashboard` takes `id` for the same entity — both self-correctable via the zod error's named path, but a small, cheap consistency fix (pick one convention, e.g. `id` everywhere a single dashboard is addressed) removes a real, observed one-retry tax. [MCP-QA spot-check 2026-07-06]
- [ ] (P3, M) **`@nestjs/core` moderate CVE — Nest v10→v11 major upgrade** — the one remaining `pnpm audit --prod` moderate advisory that isn't a drop-in `pnpm.overrides` fix; needs its own migration/breaking-change review across the Nest ecosystem, deliberately not bundled with the cheap dependency-refresh item below. [engineering-auditor Pass 13 Risk 4]
- [ ] (P3, S) **Dependency refresh: `file-type` v16→v21 + `js-yaml`/`qs` moderates** — `file-type`'s CVE fix needs v20+ (ESM-only; requires a dynamic `import()` refactor of the two `require('file-type')` call sites in `attachments.service.ts`/`workspaces.service.ts`); `js-yaml`/`qs` are minor-version-safe candidate bumps via `@nestjs/swagger`/`@nestjs/platform-express`. Bundle as one dependency-refresh pass; re-run `pnpm audit --prod` after to confirm clean. [engineering-auditor Pass 13 Risk 4]
- [x] (P3, S) **Document `GITLAB_TOKEN_ENCRYPTION_KEY` in `.env.example`** ✅ shipped 2026-07-06 (closed as a side effect of the config-parity CI smoke test build — see "Already Done" below; the reverse-direction check that build introduced caught this gap for real, along with `GITLAB_WEBHOOK_BASE_URL`/`AUTO_SEED`/`WEB_BASE_URL`/`OIDC_CONFIG_ENCRYPTION_KEY`, which were also undocumented). [engineering-auditor Pass 13 Risk 5]
- [ ] (P3, S) **Log the swallowed post-commit side-effect failure in `bulkUpdateAtomic`** — the post-commit fan-out loop (`issues.service.ts`) wraps `finishUpdate(...)` in a bare `try/catch` with no `logger.warn`/`.error`, unlike every other post-commit-failure catch in the codebase; a webhook/notification/realtime emit failure after an atomic batch commit currently leaves zero trace. [engineering-auditor Pass 13 Risk 6]
- [ ] (P3, S) **Starter-gadget first-run has no in-product explanation** — a project's first dashboard is silently pre-populated with 3 starter gadgets; nothing tells a first-time user they're editable/deletable defaults, not fixed system gadgets. A one-time dismissible hint under the dashboard tabs would close it. [UI-REVIEW.md 2026-07-06, P3 #1]
- [ ] (P3, S) **Staleness pill's "why" is mouse-hover-only** — `AgentContextSection`'s amber staleness-pill explanation lives only in a `title` attribute, unlike `IssueCard`'s blocked badge which pairs `title` with an `aria-label` carrying the same text for touch/AT users. Low priority — the pill's own visible text ("N changes since last update") is already fairly self-explanatory. [UI-REVIEW.md 2026-07-06, P3 #2]

- [ ] (P3, S) **`ReportsPage`'s `selectedSprintId` never re-validates after the initial pick** — if the selected sprint is later deleted, the seed-once effect keeps pointing at the dead id and `useBurndown` silently returns empty/404 data instead of falling back. Change the guard to also reset when `selectedSprintId` is set but no longer present in `sprints` (same "clamp instead of reset-on-refetch" shape `BoardPage`'s board-selection healing effect already uses). [engineering-auditor Pass-11 P3-3]
- [ ] (P3, S) **`updateColumn` allows an arbitrary `order` write that can desync from the `reorderColumns` invariant** — dead/unused-but-exposed API surface (the web UI only ever calls the atomic `reorderColumns` endpoint); prefer removing `order` from `UpdatePersonalColumnDto` entirely over adding a defensive tiebreaker. [engineering-auditor Pass-10 Risk #5]
- [ ] (P3, M) **"Board health" proactive nudge banner** — a lightweight, dismissible banner/badge surfacing board hygiene at a glance: N issues blocked with no comment in 7+ days, N issues with no assignee in an active sprint, N stale issues untouched 14+ days. Turns existing analytics/automation infrastructure into a proactive nudge instead of a passive report. [product-auditor Pass 10 ideation #1]
- [ ] (P3, S) **Inline card context menu** (quick sprint/epic/assignee from the board card) — right-click/long-press menu removes several clicks from the most repetitive daily board action. [product-auditor Pass 10 ideation #2]

- [ ] (P3, S) **Recently-visited project switcher (+ command palette)** — the workspace half of this shipped 2026-07-02 (recently-visited workspaces in the header chip, verified live with a 14-workspace fixture in Pass 12 — see the ticked "Workspace-switcher search/filter + recently-visited" entry). Remaining scope: the same recency treatment for projects within a workspace, and surfacing recents in the Cmd-K command palette, not just the workspace chip. [product-auditor Pass-11 ideation #3, workspace half closed Pass 12]
- [ ] (P3, S) **Design-elevation carry-forward** — `docs/UI-REVIEW.md` § Remaining gaps tracks the still-unaudited/unmigrated surfaces (`WebhookFormModal`, `ShareSection`, `WorkspaceMembersPage`, `WorkspaceAuditLogPage`, `SharedBoardPage`, `IssueDetailDrawer` sub-panels, `CommandPalette`, `ProjectCard`/`CreateProjectModal`) — work this list down per the standing design-elevation directive in CLAUDE.md. [frontend-qa / UI-REVIEW.md]
- [ ] (P3, S) **GitHub webhook — log `X-GitHub-Delivery` on signature-verification failures** — diagnosability improvement, lets a self-hoster correlate a failed delivery in GitHub's own repo-settings UI with the exact Next Lane log line. [engineering-auditor Pass-12, diagnosability idea]
- [ ] (P3, S) **Swimlane WIP/health summary strip** — now that Swimlanes v2 groups issues into real named sections (component/label/custom field/sprint), add an optional per-swimlane summary strip (count, oldest-issue age, or a configurable custom-field rollup like "story points remaining") in the swimlane header, the same way each status column already shows a count badge. [product-auditor Pass-12 Ideation #3]
- [ ] (P3, S) **Unify the mobile theme-control location** — `MobileSidebarDrawer.tsx` reuses `SidebarNavContent` but doesn't render `<ThemeToggle>` or the collapse control that desktop's `AppSidebar.tsx` footer has; the toggle is still reachable via the avatar/user menu on mobile (not a dead end), but it's two different mental-model locations for the same control depending on viewport width. Either add `<ThemeToggle>` to `MobileSidebarDrawer.tsx`, or intentionally document/standardize on the avatar-menu placement for mobile. [product-auditor Pass-12 rank #7]
- [ ] (P3, S) **SSRF hardening for outbound `GithubClient`/`GitlabClient` calls, once real outbound requests land** — both clients are currently unit-tested/mocked seams with zero real network egress (webhook payloads carry everything v1 needs); the moment the PR-status/live-CI-status work (Ready queue, above) makes its first *real* outbound call (a self-hosted GitLab base URL is admin-supplied, the same shape of risk the webhook-URL SSRF guard already closes for outbound webhooks), it must reuse the existing `resolveAndCheckBlocked()`/`isBlockedIp()` DNS-preflight + `redirect: 'manual'` guard (`webhooks.service.ts`) rather than a bare `fetch`. Filed now as a standing note (also cross-referenced on the PR-status Ready item) so that build doesn't reintroduce the exact class of bug the webhook SSRF guard already fixed once. [engineering-auditor pattern carryover; filed at GitLab v1 ship, 2026-07-03]
- [ ] (P3, M) **MCP session-start token cost: ~97 tools ≈ 73+ KB of schemas at `tools/list`** — MCP-QA pass 1, finding 8: not blocking (hosts cache the list after the first call), but it grows with every feature per the standing MCP-exposure definition-of-done, and was already ~18K tokens before the first real call at 91 tools (now 97, growing). **Fix shape (from the QA report, not yet designed):** grouped/dynamic toolsets, or trimmed descriptions for the long tail (the 10 biggest descriptions were all >500 B) — needs a real design pass, not a one-line change; revisit if a host's context budget starts getting measurably squeezed by it. [MCP-QA pass 1, finding 8]
- [ ] (P3, S) **`color-mix()` opacity utilities have no fallback for pre-2023 browsers** — `withOpacity()` (`tailwind.config.js`) emits `color-mix(in srgb, ...)` for every `/NN`-opacity Tailwind utility built on a CSS-var color; browsers older than Safari 16.4/Chrome 111/Firefox 113 drop the entire declaration (no color at all) rather than degrading. Low risk for a 2026 self-hosted target audience — not urgent; if pursued, emit a plain fallback declaration immediately before the `color-mix()` one for load-bearing surfaces (scrim, modal backdrop), or just document the browser floor in `docs/ARCHITECTURE.md`. [engineering-auditor Pass-12 P3-1]

## Future (not-yet-Ready — explicitly gated, do not promote)

- [ ] (P1, L) **Graph docs↔work overlay — issues + people on the knowledge graph** [FOUNDER CHECKPOINT — awaiting go-ahead] — the biggest "beyond Obsidian" lever, deliberately held out of the 2026-07-20 observatory revamp for a founder decision. A toggle that brings the ISSUES each doc references (via the existing `PageIssueLink`) and the PEOPLE (authors/assignees) onto the same full-page canvas, so the graph maps how the org's knowledge and work connect — something Obsidian structurally cannot do (no concept of work). Needs: backend graph endpoint variant returning issue/person nodes + doc↔issue / issue↔person edges (tenant-scoped, capped like the page graph), a node-type visual language (docs vs issues vs people), and overlay toggles. Sized L. Promote only on explicit founder approval. [founder directive 2026-07-20 — graph revamp checkpoint]

Approved future pillars that are deliberately sequenced behind other
priorities per `CLAUDE.md`'s converge-don't-sprawl mandate ("drive toward the
v1 release criteria... then polish — don't generate endless backlog without
finishing"). The backlog-groomer must NOT pull anything from this section
into § Ready until its stated gate is met — this section exists precisely so
a good idea is captured and sequenced without displacing whatever § Ready is
currently working (right now, the Pages epic).

- [ ] (P2, XL — **GATED: do not promote until BOTH (1) v1.0 release criteria
  met AND (2) `docs/ROADMAP.md` Phase 11 Pages has shipped its v1 slices,
  specifically the frontend force-directed graph view, item #7**) **Systems
  Map — lightweight, agent-native architecture & dependency mapping** —
  founder directive 2026-07-09, verbatim: *"Enterprise architecture is an
  interesting thing to tackle... I've worked in many companies without a
  good solution to map out architecture/dependencies and integrations. Does
  it make sense to have this in this app?"* Orchestrator recommended a
  lightweight version, not a LeanIX/Ardoq clone; founder approved: *"Lite
  weight is good by me."* **Scope (sketch — reserved for a real
  slice-by-slice breakdown, mirroring how Phase 11/Pages was sequenced, once
  the gate above is met):** a system/service catalog (a new `System` node
  type: name, owner team, tier/criticality, description, links to its
  repo(s), docs page(s), and owning project/issues) with typed dependency
  edges (depends-on / calls / integrates-with), rendered via **the same
  force-directed graph + MCP-traversal engine Pages is building** — a second
  consumer of that infrastructure, not a parallel build; a design note is
  already left on Phase 11's remaining frontend graph-view slice to keep the
  graph component and MCP graph-traversal tool shape generic enough for this
  reuse. **The wedge:** existing EA tools are heavyweight per-seat SaaS
  (LeanIX/Ardoq-class) or static diagrams that rot the day after the
  offsite; Next Lane's version stays current because (1) the developer graph
  is already real (GitHub/GitLab/Gitea two-way links, Phase 9, shipped),
  (2) an agent maintains it over MCP (the living-docs thesis Phase 11
  establishes for Pages, applied to systems), (3) an agent can traverse
  systems → dependencies → linked issues → repos to answer "what breaks if
  we deprecate service X?" — the question every EA tool fails at — and
  (4) architecture/dependency maps are sensitive "your data, your compute"
  topology data that belongs self-hosted. Opens an entirely new
  Better-than-Jira scorecard category (Enterprise Architecture / systems
  mapping) the per-seat incumbent doesn't bundle at any price — see
  `docs/VISION.md` § Better-than-Jira scorecard, "Architecture / Systems
  mapping" row. **Deliberately lightweight v1, NOT a full EA suite:** system
  catalog + typed dependency edges + the graph view + agent traversal, full
  stop. **Explicitly NOT v1** (call these out so scope stays honest):
  capability models, TIME (Tolerate/Invest/Migrate/Eliminate) lifecycle
  scoring, compliance/GRC workflows, diagram-authoring tools. Full framing
  and the sketched v1-slice sequence: `docs/ROADMAP.md` Phase 12. **Size:**
  XL as filed (schema + backend + frontend graph view + MCP tools —
  comparable in shape to the Pages epic it depends on); do not attempt to
  scope-break this into buildable P1/P2 slices with acceptance criteria/
  territory/size until the gate above is actually met — re-run that exercise
  then, mirroring how Phase 11 was broken into 12 numbered sub-slices.
  [founder directive 2026-07-09; VISION.md § The pillars item 8;
  ROADMAP.md Phase 12]

## Cloud-native / Kubernetes (post-v1 epic — ROADMAP Phase 4)

Make Next Lane deployable on Kubernetes with HA + autoscaling, keeping the
single-host Compose path for small installs. Sequenced so single-replica Helm
can ship first; multi-replica HA depends on the Redis items below.

- [x] (P1, S) **Kustomize deploy path was broken since it shipped — repaired + gated in CI** ✅ shipped 2026-07-30. Nothing ever ran `kustomize build`, and the 2026-06-27 entry above was signed off on "all manifests parse". Rendering them for the first time found three defects, every one of which reaches a user following `docs/DEPLOY-KUBERNETES.md`: **(1)** all six image refs used `ghcr.io/next-lane/…`, a namespace that does not exist (confirmed against the live GHCR API — `403`, vs. a real tag list for `ghcr.io/overcastly-ai/…`); **(2)** base + prod pinned `newTag: "1.0.0"`, never released — `kubectl apply -k` → `ImagePullBackOff` on a green build; **(3)** the dev overlay's `newTag: latest` was **inert** — a Kustomize image transformer matches the manifest's *current* image, and the base had already rewritten the bare `next-lane-api`, so dev silently inherited the pinned tag. Fixes: namespace corrected; the pinned tags are now owned by `scripts/sync-versions.mjs` (**11 version records, was 7**) so a release can't leave them stale; dev keys on the post-base name. New **`Kustomize overlays render`** CI job renders both overlays and asserts the exact expected image set, deriving the owner at runtime so forks pass. **The gate was verified in both directions** — reintroducing defect (3) makes it fail with a diff, not just pass on the fixed tree. Root cause of the class: a deploy artifact with no execution in CI. Immediately proven on a live release: v0.3.0 (auto-cut when #57 merged) bumped the other records to `0.3.0` and the new guard caught the Kustomize tags still on `0.2.0`. Manifests now render `ghcr.io/overcastly-ai/next-lane-{api,web}:0.3.0`, confirmed pullable (`200` on the manifest endpoint). [self-audit while waiting on #57 CI]
- [x] (P0, S) **Release would have silently un-synced the new Kustomize targets** ✅ shipped 2026-07-30 — found while landing the item above, and the reason it isn't a latent time bomb. `release.config.mjs` runs `sync-versions.mjs <version>` in `prepareCmd`, so the moment Kustomize became a sync target the release started **rewriting** those two files — but `@semantic-release/git.assets` didn't list them, so the release commit would have left them modified-but-uncommitted and turned `--check` red on the very next push, every release, forever. Added both files to `assets`. Then closed the class rather than the instance: `sync-versions.mjs` gained a **`--files`** mode that prints every file it writes, and the `Version consistency` job now asserts `assets ⊇ --files`, so adding a future sync target without wiring it into the release fails CI with a message naming the file. Also verified in both directions (deleting one asset entry reproduces the exact error). Fixes the stale "all 7 records" comments (now 11).
- [x] (P1, S) **`docs/DEPLOY-KUBERNETES.md` stale in two load-bearing ways** ✅ shipped 2026-07-30, alongside the above. It documented `--build-arg VITE_API_URL=…` as baking the API origin into the web bundle — untrue since the image moved to a runtime `docker-entrypoint.sh` that writes `config.js` from `API_URL` (the ARG is now a no-op backward-compat shim), so anyone following it would rebuild an image to change an API URL that a single env var already controls. It also still called automated multi-arch GHCR publishing "a separate Phase 4 deliverable" long after it shipped, and told readers to build images they can just pull. Replaced with a published-image table (tags + "nothing to build"), a corrected runtime-config callout, and build instructions that use the reader's own registry rather than ours. The Helm values table's image defaults were re-verified against `values.yaml`. Both manifests were checked and already wire `API_URL` correctly — the docs alone were wrong.
- [ ] (P3, S) Optional metrics endpoint + `ServiceMonitor` + OTLP traces for operator-grade observability in-cluster [engineering-auditor]

- 2026-07-02 — Settings robustness pass — fix batch (P1, M): fixed all 4 confirmed defects from the `qa-tester` sweep in `docs/UI-REVIEW.md` ("Settings robustness sweep — 2026-07-02"). **SETTINGS-1 (P1, admin lockout):** `workspaces.service.ts#addMember` no longer upserts an existing member's role (was: a solo admin re-typing their own email into the Invite form silently demoted them, zero recovery path) — inviting an already-member email now returns a friendly 409; role changes moved to new `PATCH /workspaces/:id/members/:membershipId`; that endpoint and `removeMember` both enforce a last-admin invariant (400, friendly), covering self-demotion/self-removal by the sole admin while a non-last-admin demote/remove still works. **SETTINGS-2 (P2):** branding hex input normalizes CSS 3-digit shorthand to 6-digit client-side before submit (`WorkspaceBrandingPage.tsx#normalizeHex`) instead of round-tripping a raw server 400. **SETTINGS-3 (P2):** `statuses.service` gained a case-insensitive same-project duplicate-column-name guard (409, friendly), mirroring Labels/Components/Versions; service-level only, no DB `@@unique` (existing installs may already have dupes). **SETTINGS-4 (P2):** `labels.service` now catches Prisma P2002 on create/update and throws `A label named "X" already exists in this project` instead of the generic fallback. P3 polish: GitHub `repoFullName`, custom-field type-name, and branding-hex validation messages reworded to user voice. All 4 `test.fixme()` tests in `apps/web/e2e/settings-robustness.spec.ts` un-fixme'd, 20/20 green desktop+mobile; 23 new API unit tests; 1461 API unit tests green; `tsc --noEmit` clean both apps. (P1) `WorkflowTransition.toStatusId` index — `@@index([projectId, toStatusId])` added to schema.prisma + hand-authored migration `20260630000000_workflow_transition_to_status_index`; `prisma validate` clean. (P2) `exportCsv` 10k row cap — `IssuesService.CSV_ROW_CAP = 10_000`; `take: CAP + 1` truncation detection; `X-Next-Lane-Truncated: true` response header on the controller when truncated; service returns `{ csv, projectKey, truncated }`. (P2) Password MinLength consistency — `RegisterDto.password` raised from MinLength(6) → MinLength(8), matching ResetPasswordDto. (P2) WorkLogsService membership refactor — `resolveWorkspaceId()` private method removed; `update()`/`remove()` now use `assertProjectMember` from `common/membership.util` (same semantics, less code). (P2) AUTO_SEED default flip — `docker-entrypoint.sh` default `${AUTO_SEED:-false}` (was `:-true`); `docker-compose.yml` api env sets `AUTO_SEED: "true"` explicitly so local/demo stack is unaffected; production deployments no longer auto-seed. (P2) Tenant-isolation matrix >65 rows — 19 new matrix rows across work-logs/standups/issue-templates/personal-boards/planning-poker; 4 new provisioned resources (`workLogId`, `pokerSessionId`, `personalColumnId`, `issueTemplateId`); sanity threshold >65; 1306 unit tests green; build clean.
- 2026-06-29 — Workflows MCP surface / Phase 4 (P1, M): new standalone `@next-lane/mcp` package (`apps/mcp/`) — a Model Context Protocol server over stdio using the official `@modelcontextprotocol/sdk` (1.29.0) + `zod` (3.25.x). Lets external agents (Claude Desktop, Claude Code) read AND write a project's workflows/SDLC + core entities via the Next Lane REST API, authenticated with a PAT (`NEXT_LANE_TOKEN`, `Authorization: Bearer`; `NEXT_LANE_API_URL` default `http://localhost:4000`; fails fast on missing token). 18 tools — read: `list_workspaces`, `list_projects`, `list_boards`, `list_statuses`, `list_workflows`, `get_workflow`, `list_issues`, `get_issue`; write: `create_workflow`, `create_workflow_from_template`, `update_workflow`, `delete_workflow`, `add_workflow_transition`, `update_workflow_transition`, `delete_workflow_transition`, `assign_board_workflow`, `create_issue`, `move_issue`. Centralized `client.ts` fetch wrapper (base URL + bearer + JSON, surfaces API error message + HTTP status); thin zod-validated handlers; `bin` = `next-lane-mcp` (shebang). 18 vitest unit tests (mocked fetch — missing-token fast-fail, URL/header/method/body construction, NestJS error surfacing); `tsc` build clean; stdio `tools/list` smoke verified (18 tools). README with Claude Desktop (`mcpServers` JSON) + Claude Code (`claude mcp add`) config. Additive — no schema/backend changes. [founder request 2026-06-28]

Satisfies founder "workflows editable per board" request.
